# Performance And Invalidation

The [Metapad milestone audit](baselines/2026-09-14-independent-component-milestone.md)
records the completed small-network conditional proof/edit/refine/reuse demonstration.
It separates trusted runtime contracts and finite validation from strong authority,
and records current model dependencies, costs and remaining generalization work.

Resource/notice runtime extension (2026-09-14, v120): the existing fixture now
adds the exact public resource supplier and its generated adapter as two translation
units. Its normal and compatible loop-edit implementations exercise the same real
consumer network. All nine native allocation/image-reference function bodies match
production. The runtime work leaves both consumer proof keys and engine inputs
unchanged; process/model/header-free imports take 6.963s and 7.400s. The baseline prepares in 0.0374s, compiles twelve translation units in 0.630s,
links in 0.0401s and runs 30 original/C executions in 7.783s, including Wine/display
startup. The compatible supplier variant runs the same 30 executions in 6.581s.
Ten current runtime tests pass; eight deliberate failures reject as expected.
All 31 phase records are retained in `consumer-resource-timings-v2.json`, with
source/test/evidence bindings under `practical-assurance/consumer-resource-*`. Keep this finite validation cost separate
from local proof and whole-pilot build costs; no application query or pilot rebuild
is needed for these test/adapter additions.

Real consumer runtime validation (2026-09-14, v119): preparation of the retained
cleanup overlay, both consumer interfaces and exact original caller inputs takes
0.0352s. Compiling ten translation units takes 0.520s; linking the PE32 fixture takes
0.0407s. Headless Wine runs 13 cases on both original/C sides in 7.732s, including
Wine/display startup. Six deliberate-failure runs each exit 42 as expected. Full
per-case compiler/link/runtime timings are retained in
`practical-assurance/consumer-runtime-timings-v2.json`; these execution times are
not solver benchmarks.

The runtime fixture has its own test dependencies, so adding actual UI/lifetime
checks changes no application proof engine. Current public save/UI proof imports
succeed with processes and model/header generation forbidden, taking 7.194s and
7.655s for recursive validation. No local proof, native deployment link or pilot
is rebuilt. The fixture link is a runtime test executable, not an authoritative
portable deployment receipt.

Eleven current native tests across two shards have 223 checked declared input
paths. The existing 27 local-proof tests also retain 25 matching declared inputs,
so their executions are reused rather than repeated. Nix setup and headless runtime
startup dominate this small fixture's compile/link costs. The final audit preserves
these separate costs and the narrower notice-suppressed, fixture-admitted scope.

Public selective contract invalidation (2026-09-14, v118): normal-frame export
is separate from the stronger cleanup proof domain. Withdrawing checked EDI
preservation takes 20.782s publicly, with 6.684s evidence import and zero model,
compiler or solver runs; all nine composition queries are reused. The save
consumer retains its exact proof key/files: 19.921s public latency, 7.716s supplier
input validation and 0.0227s local proof import, again zero model/compiler/solver
runs. The UI consumer reports the missing equality in 10.437s, including 6.853s
input validation, without reusing or generating a proof. No application body or
interface changes in this contract experiment.

New baselines were necessary for the changed checker: save uses a 387,880-byte
model with 1,828 properties, a 5.189s query (0.495s symbolic execution, 0.0370s SSA,
4.506s solver), 0.0000576s rendering, 0.124s dependency inventory and 0.106s compile.
UI uses an 861,641-byte model with 2,611 properties, a 2.568s query (1.998s symbolic
execution, 0.0162s SSA, 0.304s solver), 0.0000635s rendering, 0.122s dependency
inventory and 0.187s compile. The removed register-copy checks reflect fewer
assumed guarantees, not disabled safety obligations. Ordinary wrong edits and
compatible resource changes still run through both public workflows; neighbor
reuse takes 19.855s for save and 20.040s for UI with no consumer proof work.

The current composition baseline costs 28.488s for nine queries; the resource
neighbor reuses them in 18.892s. Source preparation, regional proofs and original
slices are retained; no native link or pilot is rebuilt. Complete commands include
recursive evidence validation and concurrent repository checks, so these are
observed latencies rather than controlled throughput benchmarks. Twenty-seven
targeted tests in five shards have 25 current declared input paths. Evidence:
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-contract-refinement-v1/`,
`public-cleanup-composition-v14/`, `public-cleanup-save-v5/` and
`public-cleanup-replace-v2/`.

Two real consumer operations (2026-09-14, v117): the guarded UI operation
compiles five owned original transfers and its ordinary C, with both cleanup
bodies absent. Its first model mixed four private argument pushes into public
write history: 2,811 properties took 84.981s. Tracking the admitted disjoint words
separately while checking all original pushes and argument values reduces the
direct query to 2.405s for 2,763 properties. Both use the same mandatory safety
and unwinding policy; the removed properties arise from the smaller memory
history representation, not disabled checks.

The optimized public model is 864,729 bytes. Its query takes 2.514s: symbolic
execution 1.960s, SSA conversion 0.0137s, solver backend 0.308s. Model construction
takes 0.000074s; dependency inventory uses four compiler processes totaling
0.117s and GOTO compilation takes 0.140s. Existing evidence import costs 6.962s.
Host/PE32 source preparation separately takes 0.0564s plus 0.00148s input handling.
No native linking, pilot rebuild or cleanup regional proof run occurs.

Public UI command latencies are 21.253s baseline, 13.550s wrong guard, 18.228s
repair and 19.009s compatible resource change. Repair and neighbor use identical
consumer proof keys/files with zero model/compiler/solver runs. The current shared
checker also rechecks save: 5.254s query, 24.470s public baseline, 17.408s wrong
arithmetic, 23.247s repair and 18.973s neighbor. Repair and neighbor reuse save's
proof as well. These complete-command measurements include recursive evidence
validation and other concurrently running validation; they are not controlled
throughput benchmarks. Evidence import and command overhead exceed solver cost.

The audit matches all 23 declared inputs of 17 passing tests across four Nix
shards, including conservatively declared complete `src` inputs. The boundary
test retains exactly its two existing failures/seven size offenders. Evidence is
under `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-replace-v1/` and
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-save-v4/`. Contract-compatible application
edits reuse proofs; changing the shared checker correctly required new baseline
proofs. Concrete runtime applicability and general refinement remain separate.

Real save-operation consumer (2026-09-14, v116): two original caller transfers
and the complete ordinary C are compared using the cleanup summary, with both
callee bodies absent. The strengthened model is 399,484 bytes and checks 1,992
properties in 5.258s: symbolic execution 0.561s, SSA conversion 0.037s, and solver
backend 4.506s. Model rendering takes 0.000060s, dependency inventory four compiler
processes totaling 0.122s, and GOTO compilation 0.104s. Loading and validating the
existing dependency evidence takes 6.791s. Host/PE32 source preparation separately
takes about 0.070s; retained transfer-plan loading takes about two seconds and
exact caller slicing under 0.01s. No native link, pilot or regional proof rebuild
is needed for this caller change.

Public baseline latency is 23.903s, wrong arithmetic 18.132s, repair 18.799s,
and compatible resource-neighbor change 18.624s. Repair and neighbor reuse all
consumer proof files with zero model/compiler/solver runs. The resource change
rebinds the cleanup contract evidence and then save evidence while preserving
contract identity and both consumer proof keys. Recursive evidence import and
command overhead now exceed this small caller's solver cost. Model size, original
inputs and compiled source inventory are audited in
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-save-v3/`; this is one consumer context,
not a claim of general caller or runtime qualification.

Service-prefix composition (2026-09-14, v115): a naive entry snapshot array
exceeded the 600-second complete-query limit. The stronger sufficient premise
that no public writes precede entry service calls passes in 44.698s with 3,431
properties, retaining safety and unwinding assertions. Its query spends 9.451s
in symbolic execution, 0.876s converting SSA and 32.906s in the solver backend.
The new composition query contains only bounded event records: 13,220 bytes,
12 properties, 0.000029s model generation, 0.0325s compilation and 0.2332s query
execution (0.00826s symbolic execution, 0.00765s SSA and 0.1947s solver backend).
These timings measure different obligations; the small composition query does
not replace the regional correctness queries.

The public baseline takes 29.451s; a compatible resource edit takes 19.665s with
zero consumer model/compiler/solver runs in both tail and composition. Rebinding
and validation still have visible latency. Entry/loop/tail rechecks were necessary
for the changed engine binding, using retained source preparation and exact
inputs; no pilot rebuild was needed. Canonical JSON reordered two independent
register initializations and exposed an overly strict spatial reader. Accepting
the two exact consecutive orders preserves semantics and contract identity,
while an intervening stack effect rejects. Evidence is under
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v11/` and
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-regions-v3/`.

The setup-binding change exposed remaining test invalidation overhead: operator
source-check and entry-proof test shards declare the whole `src` directory, so
both must be refreshed even when their narrower regional engine dependencies
are unchanged. Their prior input audit correctly rejected stale source bytes;
inputs were not omitted to claim reuse. Application-neighbor proof reuse and
proof-engine/test-package invalidation are distinct measurements.

Scoped control composition (2026-09-14, v114): the public rank/return-frame
query compiles to 14,642 bytes and checks seven properties in 0.0707s. Model
construction takes 0.000037s and compilation 0.0324s; symbolic execution takes
0.00121s, SSA conversion 0.00130s and the solver backend 0.0471s. Scoped guarantee
and graph checks consume existing regional evidence without generating or
executing application bodies. Unbounded loop repetition is handled by the rank,
while complete regional queries establish segment-local termination.

The complete public baseline takes 27.168s. Selecting the compatible resource
implementation takes 19.074s, reusing both tail and composition queries with zero
consumer model/compiler/solver work and identical proof files. This is continued
proof reuse, not evidence that full state/trace or runtime qualification is done.
All 16 targeted Nix tests pass across three shards; nine declared input paths match
current bytes. No pilot or regional correctness query is rebuilt. Evidence is
under `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v8/`.

Production accessor composition (2026-09-14, v113): the actual returned-view
relation compiles to 64,043 bytes and checks 797 properties in 1.603s. Model/header
construction takes 0.000215s and compilation 0.0384s; symbolic execution takes
0.0426s, SSA conversion 0.00957s and the solver backend 1.498s. The model contains
only the common ABI, bound accessors and explicit reference/hook contract models.
The source-use check traverses the 149 reachable compiler instructions without
unrolling loops; its body and seven-region coverage bind the substitution premise.

Public baseline latency is 28.571s. The compatible resource supplier change takes
19.537s, reusing both tail and composition proof files with zero model/compiler/
solver runs and updated dependency receipts. Imports forbid processes and all
header/model generators. No pilot or original regional query is rebuilt. This
preserves proof reuse while adding a necessary premise; it is not a claim of
improved overall latency or completed network qualification. Evidence is under
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v7/`.

Complete adapter integration (2026-09-14, v112): current-plan parsing, interface
normalization and production overlay rendering take 1.677s in the retained
preparation. No solver is invoked. Four repository PE32/Wine integration tests,
including six complete operation cases and three negative controls, take 21.881s
in their Nix shard with peak RSS 70,732 KiB. All 218 declared source/resource paths
match current bytes. The checks reuse the existing compiler and headless Wine
fixtures; no pilot or regional application query is rebuilt. These are native
runtime validation costs, not a measurement of a newly qualified local proof or
public neighboring-component edit cycle. The reference and fault representation
bridge remains a proof obligation despite passing finite executions.

Descriptor and grant composition (2026-09-14, v111): the actual canonical accessor
query compiles to 53,503 bytes and checks 669 properties in 0.600s. Model/header
construction takes less than 0.001s and compilation 0.037s. It uses only the common
runtime ABI headers and bound accessor definitions, without application bodies.
The source grant check visits 149 instruction/state pairs over the complete owned
graph; it does not expand loop iterations. Proof import checks retained headers
directly without invoking header generators or subprocesses.

Public baseline latency is 25.238s. A compatible checked resource supplier change
takes 17.981s and reuses the tail and composition queries with zero model,
compiler or solver runs. Current dependency receipts are rebound; the proof files
and key remain identical. Evidence is under `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v6/`.
The original regional proofs and pilot are retained. These timings include the
conditional descriptor/grant checks, not completed runtime or network qualification.

Current-memory substitution (2026-09-14, v110): the public composition query
uses arbitrary current bytes rather than the predecessor's accumulated event
history. Its 19,788-byte model checks ten properties in 0.303s, with 0.031s
compilation and less than 0.001s model construction. The exact regional memory
guarantees, consumer premises and supported reader are bound into the proof key.
No original/authored body or buffer-sized heap is added to the composition model.

The complete public baseline takes 24.960s, including 5.552s regional-evidence
import inside the build. Changing the compatible checked resource supplier takes
18.504s; both its tail consumer and the composition product reuse their proof
files without model generation, compilation or solving. The retained query's
solver timing is historical on reuse. This closes a specific memory-transport
obligation; it does not establish independent lifting of a complete component
network or remove descriptor/lifetime and concrete compatibility checks.

The affected-test selection for this change also includes existing shards whose
declared input is the complete `src` directory, including the real tail operator
test through its supplier-test dependency. Those shards rerun local proofs after
an unrelated production-module addition. This is test-input invalidation, separate
from the measured public supplier-edit reuse. Narrowing those fixture inputs
requires preserving their actual dynamic imports and command dependencies;
omitting required validation or calling an old result current would not fix it.

Shared private-object proof (2026-09-14, v109): the fixed private transport
recipe and extracted tables compile to 28,627 bytes and discharge 138 properties
in 6.678s complete query time. Symbolic execution takes 0.161s, SSA conversion
0.103s and the solver backend 6.361s; compilation takes 0.034s and model/data
construction less than 0.001s. The proof contains the 76-byte private object and
cell layouts, with original and authored application bodies absent.

Full public baseline latency is 24.782s, including 5.755s regional-evidence import
inside the build. A compatible checked resource implementation rebinds the tail
and then composition products in 18.647s; both consumers run zero model,
compiler or solver stages. Composition import takes 5.573s. All query files are
identical on reuse, and their timings are labeled retained evidence. No regional
correctness query or pilot is rebuilt. The current evidence is
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v4/`; private representation
transport is checked while physical admission, public memory and lifetime
composition remain unfinished.

Independent admission phase (2026-09-14, v108): the public composition product
imports all three current regional receipts in 5.731s. Its existing/proposed/
nonempty-domain queries take 0.0551s, 0.1442s and 0.1210s respectively; compilation
totals 0.0936s and rendering totals less than 0.001s. Full public latency is
17.626s. The model contains only extracted admission expressions and coordinate
transport, with application bodies absent.

A compatible resource supplier edit reuses the tail proof and then the admission
proof, with zero model/compiler/solver runs in either consumer. Complete public
latency is 27.871s, including the extra tail-product rebind; admission evidence
import takes 5.594s. This is not an overall latency improvement claim. Retained
query timings are explicitly historical on reuse. Source preparation, local
proofs and pilot/link products are retained. The semantic phase boundary is
justified by independent admission requirements and measured proof reuse.
Evidence is under `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v2/`; it proves
spatial implications only, leaving concrete admission and state/lifetime
composition incomplete.

Shared public operation graph (2026-09-14, v107): entry, loop and tail use the
same source-preparation product. Their query times are 45.174s, 18.380s and 172.548s;
complete CLI times are 60.532s, 26.144s and 187.278s. They check 3,418, 1,880 and
4,420 properties respectively. Entry/loop/tail paired compilation takes 0.500s,
0.198s and 0.865s; symbolic execution takes 9.160s, 0.348s and 6.806s, and solver
backend time 33.688s, 17.784s and 163.646s. The retained audit includes input,
model, compiler, inventory, evidence and query phases separately.

An actual allocation-flag error is rejected in 62.462s public latency. Repair
reuses the entry's exact proof files in 8.758s without model generation,
compilation, inventory or solving. This is exact repair, not another neighboring
implementation-reuse result. The current engine/graph changes correctly require
new regional checks; they do not silently import old producer bindings. No pilot
or link is rebuilt. The shared graph covers 156 reachable source instructions and
the exact original slices cover 35 transfers, but state/runtime composition and
complete-component edit latency remain unqualified. Evidence is under
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-regions-v1/`.

Public terminal baseline (2026-09-14, v106): the Nix-backed cleanup-tail product
checks 4,420 properties in 194.016s, with 213.736s complete CLI latency. Preparation
takes 0.209s, model rendering 0.001s, host type checking 0.013s, paired compilation
0.973s, dependency discovery 0.094s and inventories 3.777s. Its new supplier live-word
assertions change the model from v105; these timings do not isolate an optimization.
Neighbor implementations are absent, but local original-code expansion still
produces 188,803 compiled instructions in the tail function. Small authored
regions therefore do not by themselves guarantee a cheap theorem. The
6,600,058-byte model's symbolic execution takes 6.844s, SSA conversion 0.346s
and solver backend 184.834s. The actual generation error is rejected in 237.222s
complete CLI latency. Exact repair takes 11.959s; a separately checked supplier
implementation change takes 11.562s, both with zero model/compiler/inventory/solver
runs and identical source preparation, proof key and proof files. An incompatible
private frame rejects before these stages in 5.045s. All five retained products
import without processes or model rendering; the audit is
`practical-assurance/public-tail-workflow-v1/audit.json`. Reused query timings in
that audit describe the retained theorem, not a new solver run. These measurements
include concurrent validation and do not complete runtime or network qualification.

Editable tail validation (2026-09-14, v105): the type-corrected and strengthened
consumer checks 4,405 properties in 136.029s using Cadical; combined host type
checking and GOTO compilation take 0.969s. That combined preparation figure is not
a separately measured compiler benchmark. Full view/reference checks, arbitrary
scalar service results and explicit post-release write checks change the model,
so comparison with v103 does not isolate a performance effect. Reference-generation,
post-release-write and result-sensitive source edits fail in 49.931s, 42.575s and
48.291s respectively, with first-failure diagnostics. Source admission and actual
compiled terminal transport pass before each query. No pilot was rebuilt.

Public boundary/backend selection (2026-09-14, v104): the public region Nix
product accepts `solver = "cadical"`; Z3 remains the default. The complete query
option policy is checked during evidence import, with no external-solver process
needed for SAT. Explicit runtime revision 3 adds the actual cleanup consumer's
writable text descriptor and failed-allocation invariant, and checks all eight
public-memory predicates at both normal cuts. Moving from revision 2 invalidates
`contract` and `runtime_contract` and reconstructs/rechecks the local model.
Performance remains conditional on the selected region and contract; this does
not establish the latency of a complete independently editable component network.

The seven-case public audit is `practical-assurance/public-region-check-v7/audit.json`.
Revision 3 takes 27.874s end to end: model construction 0.001s, paired compilation
0.223s, compiler dependencies 0.087s, inventory 0.843s, query 20.023s (symbolic
execution 0.401s, SSA conversion 0.022s, solver 19.344s). The 1,417,996-byte model
checks 1,880 properties. The wrong store fails in 26.431s; repair takes 6.904s and
outside-region growth 9.874s with zero model/compiler/inventory/solver runs.
All public evidence imports with subprocesses and model generation forbidden.
Concurrent repository validation affects these timings. Outside-region edits do
not demonstrate compatibility of an independently requalified neighbor.

Supplier-bound tail (2026-09-14, v103): the actual resource supplier and its
byte-reading loop edit recheck through the public command in 10.234s and 9.128s.
Both expose the same checked transition domain; their distinct implementation
receipts and products are retained. The actual tail now imports its result
reference, clobbers and private frame without either supplier body in its model.

The complete 4,295-property tail query passes with the existing Cadical backend
in 115.637s: symbolic execution 6.311s, SSA conversion 0.276s and solving 107.468s.
Paired compilation takes 0.856s. Z3 exhausts 180s on the identical 6.5 MiB model;
the SAT comparison regenerates and compiles no model and retains every safety and
unwinding check, including `--no-array-field-sensitivity`. This demonstrates a
completed query under that backend, not predictable arbitrary-component latency.
The preserved-span optimization is separately checked against reference bytes;
its speed contribution has not been isolated. A real source resource-ID error
fails the call-argument obligation in 47.824s using a first-failure diagnostic.

After aligning entry/loop text descriptors with writable copy-back, complete
entry and loop queries take 52.322s and 12.080s using SAT (3,418 and 1,880
properties; compilation 0.562s and 0.216s). These change descriptor bindings as
well as solver choice, so comparison with v102 is not an isolated solver benchmark.
Some runs overlap repository validation. No pilot rebuild or link run occurs.
At v103 the public region checker still required Z3; those tail/descriptor
experiments did not change its solver policy or runtime-contract revision.
The explicit v104 public selections above supersede that limitation. Evidence
is retained in `practical-assurance/cleanup-tail-supplier-sat-v1/` and
`practical-assurance/cleanup-descriptor-v1/`.

Cleanup tail refinement (2026-09-14, v102): the real 14-unit tail is prepared
from retained transfer inputs without its resource supplier body. Compiled source
transport now matches its normal return and five early returns in 1.565s using
retained inventories. This is transport time, not a component proof or edit cycle.

Establishing the null-scratch/pending-CRLF refinement at entry checks 3,418
properties in 28.336s (compilation 0.504s; symbolic execution 8.971s, SSA 1.922s,
solver 15.982s). Preserving it and the full public domain at both loop exits
checks 1,880 properties in 140.790s (compilation 0.184s; symbolic execution 0.338s,
SSA 0.023s, solver 140.176s). These retain the actual implementation and complete
property sets; they do not demonstrate a performance improvement.

The combined tail query with the refinement exhausts 60s and then 180s; paired
compilation takes about 0.83s. A prior diagnostic without the refinement fails
`tail-copy-live-source` in 42.594s. Its counterexample describes an inadmissible
intermediate state that the existing generic loop domain had not excluded.
An exhaustive null/live allocation split retains all 4,140 properties per case.
The null case passes in 22.570s (compilation 0.861s); the live case still times
out at 180s (compilation 0.889s). Both pass compiled body transport. These runs
overlap repository validation and are not controlled serial performance comparisons.
Reusing call-argument and current-memory assertions as subsequent assumptions
also times out at 180s on the live case; it retains the assertions but establishes
neither complete correctness nor a performance improvement. This experiment is
retained as `cleanup-tail-live-lemmas-v1`, not a new public proof policy.
No pilot rebuild or link run occurs. New engine edits conservatively invalidate
older producer bindings; the v101 public reuse audit is historical for those
bindings. See `practical-assurance/cleanup-contract-refinement-v1/audit.json`.

Cleanup entry and revised loop domain (2026-09-14, v101): the retained entry
baseline checks 3,417 properties in 24.833s, with source/model preparation 0.436s
and paired compilation 0.503s; total fixture cycle is 28.949s. The checker reports symbolic execution 8.971s, SSA conversion 1.934s and solving 12.462s. Preparation retains
its source compiler and graph phases separately, while paired rendering is still
included in the preparation total. Wrong allocation/copy/view cases take
33.126/32.116/35.868s overall and fail their intended properties. These are
retained conditional entry experiments, not public complete-component latency.
No pilot rebuild or link runs.

Under runtime-contract revision 2, the public loop baseline/wrong-store/repair/
outside-edit/incompatible-boundary/unsupported-return cycles take
62.778/54.282/6.182/9.463/4.385/7.506s. The complete loop query still has 1,872
properties; its baseline symbolic execution is 0.333s and solver time 43.887s.
The domain now allows text spans extending beyond the string terminator, so this
is not an identical-query performance comparison with v100. Retained source
preparation store paths had been garbage-collected; the successful v6 public
run rebuilds the small source preparation products from retained ordinary C.
The failed v5 attempt did not run a proof. No pilot inputs were regenerated. The six successful v6 experiment products now have indirect Nix GC roots under their case directories (`retained-product`); their closures retain source preparation and proof inputs for follow-up work. Removing those links releases this retention.

All six public cases pass a current-producer audit with model generation and
subprocesses forbidden during evidence import. Repair and the outside edit
preserve the baseline proof files with all generation/compiler/inventory/solver
counts zero. This continues to demonstrate reuse within cleanup; supplier-growth
reuse for the completed component network remains open. Evidence is retained in
`practical-assurance/cleanup-entry-v5/` and
`practical-assurance/public-region-check-v6/audit.json`.

Public conditional loop comparison (2026-09-13, v100): baseline/wrong-store/
repair/outside-loop-edit/incompatible-boundary commands take
41.817/46.243/6.264/9.102/3.958s. Baseline costs are input preparation 0.069s,
solver identity 0.025s, producer binding 0.639s, model construction 0.001s,
compiler/include checks 0.287s, inventories 0.751s, compiled state transport
0.268s, and checker 34.221s (symbolic execution 0.334s, solver 33.619s).
Its model is 1,411,181 bytes and checks 1,872 properties. The prototype changed
the private-register nonwrapping guard to the declared actual offsets, so its
solver timing is not an identical-domain comparison with the earlier v97 model.
No general solver speedup is claimed.

Repair and the outside edit preserve all proof files and execute zero model
generation, compiler, inventory or solver runs. The outside edit adds an instruction before the loop and changes a later
resource ID and still requires fresh ordinary-source preparation; the displayed
latency includes it. Import validates the current selected source body and state
transport as well as the retained complete checker envelope. This is scoped
proof-region reuse inside cleanup, not another application component or proof of
the changed outside behavior. An unsupported early-return outcome also rejects without proof compilation or
solving (7.387s public latency including fresh source preparation). No link step
or pilot rebuild runs. The public
artifacts and process-forbidden revalidation are retained in
`practical-assurance/public-region-check-v4/audit.json`.

Public manual graph preparation (2026-09-13, v99): the real cleanup
baseline/body-growth/wrong-store/repair/uncut-cycle commands take
5.180/5.815/5.135/1.060/5.051s through the Nix-backed operator path. Normal source
preparation takes 0.002s, host/PE32 and inventory compilation 0.203s, and inventory
extraction plus graph construction 0.159s. Baseline feedback import takes 0.058s
with compiler and solver subprocesses forbidden. No solver or pilot rebuild runs.
Exact repair reuses the baseline product; its retained build log describes the
original build, not new compilation work during repair.

The copy-region semantic binding changes for both an added local byte variable
and an incorrect store expression, while loop-decision and advance bindings stay
identical. This excludes absolute instruction positions from local invalidation
without discarding typed operands or control destinations. The whole source
preparation product still recompiles for changed source. No local correctness
proof is imported, and no neighbor-proof reuse or independent edit-cycle speedup
is claimed by this graph preparation result. Evidence and producer checks live
in `practical-assurance/public-region-graph-v1/audit.json`.

Compiled loop and shared original-region composition (2026-09-13, v97/v98):
binding full compiled ordinary C to the local loop model gives a complete
1,872-property check in 24.471s. A separate copy/advance transition then checks
851 properties in 0.643s and is consumed from both real loop and short-input
contexts with its original instructions absent. The loop consumer checks 1,883
properties in 42.450s (symbolic execution 0.141s, conversion 0.016s, solver 42.087s);
the prefix checks 1,493 in 12.832s (0.053s, 0.004s, 12.662s respectively). Initial
retained preparation, rendering and three compilations total 1.873s; individual
compilations take 0.066–0.125s. No pilot rebuild or link step runs.

The composed loop model is 863,296 bytes, the prefix 176,612 and the supplier
140,356. Smaller original slices have not improved solver time here. Source copy
statements still execute in the consumers, and these are experimental local
queries, not public edit-cycle or neighbor-growth measurements. The earlier
public resource-call reuse result retains its separate scope. All inputs and
timings are bound in `practical-assurance/shared-copy-composition-v2/audit.json`.

Four corrupt shared transitions (memory, flags, progress and failure behavior)
are rejected in 0.589–1.523s each. An actual wrong prefix store, whose compiled
source correspondence passes, fails the paired memory check in 11.238s. The
first loop driver omitted SPX_JUMP from its admitted edge kinds and failed;
the successful run adds that real edge without weakening inputs or properties.
The ordinary loop fixture's new Nix shard depends on 271 files. Importing a
general producer-binding module merely for checker options initially inferred
the entire `src` directory; reusing the existing readonly checker options removes
that accidental dependency. The latest three-test Nix shard passes, with the
two pre-existing repository size failures reported separately.

Body-free real caller cuts (2026-09-13, v93): one complete model covers the two
actual resource-call regions at 0x5646/0x570e using a validated conditional
supplier transition. Its 1,816 properties pass with model rendering 0.001s,
compilation 0.107s and checker process 0.352s. Checker subdivisions are symbolic
execution 0.146s, conversion 0.011s and solving 0.033s. Negative argument,
live-stack, call-count and current-input-memory controls take 0.394–0.478s each.
A temporary difference outside the supplier's readable union is accepted in 0.413s;
the callee summary does not require unrelated global bytes to correspond.
No source-store count or supplier implementation enters the consumer model.

An earlier version kept both worlds in an array of structs and exhausted a
60-second check during SMT processing. Separate world objects avoid expensive
whole-array updates with the pinned array backend, while retaining both call
contexts and all properties. The model also checks the actual word-only caller
footprint and separates the supplier's disjoint private frame from live caller
words. No admitted caller input or selected assertion was removed to obtain the
passing result. The earlier incomplete attempt is not proof evidence.

The public supplier baseline/edit/wrong-ID/repair/extended-private-frame cycle
takes 11.376/11.014/16.370/0.890/12.385s. The correct edit preserves its complete
consumed transition domain. A retained caller-proof rebind then validates the new
supplier in about 0.1s and consumer evidence in 0.007s, with consumer C generation,
compilation and solving forbidden. The independently checked extended stack domain
invalidates both callers. This is actual conditional caller-cut reuse driven by
public supplier products, not yet public source-bound caller-component reuse.
Raw artifacts: `compact-callers-v4/public-supplier-reuse.json` and
`public-original-v2/audit.json` under the practical-assurance directory.

Native image/logical adapter facet (2026-09-13, v92): thirteen retained 32-bit
queries execute actual native lookup bodies and the generated logical caller with
the full 500-byte buffer and all four Metapad section origins. The baseline costs
0.005s preparation/model rendering, 0.052s compilation and 1.114s checker time,
including 0.802s symbolic execution, 0.054s conversion and 0.110s solving. All
thirteen cases total 6.523s, including eleven rejected corruptions and a deliberately
broken native-admission negative control. No pilot rebuild or native-link work
runs. These are runtime-facet checks, not public network edit/reuse latency.
Raw evidence and current-input audit: `practical-assurance/native-image-transport-v4/`.
An earlier retained-driver attempt omitted the `__CPROVER__` compilation macro and
selected the host test loop instead of symbolic input; it was stopped and is
excluded. The final retained compiler command explicitly selects the proof branch.

The new authority input is isolated under `tests/fixtures/native-image-shared-transport`.
Putting it under the common resource-text fixture initially selected 28 Nix test
shards, including slow existing shared-summary proofs. The current full manifest
has 42 consumers of that common fixture and only one consumer of the new directory.
The relocation preserves all thirteen generated C models byte-for-byte; an audit
rebinds their current fixture/producer inputs without compiling or solving again.
This is fixture invalidation hygiene, not the outstanding application-neighbor
proof-reuse demonstration. See `native-image-transport-v4/relocation-audit.json`.

Public functional edit cycle (2026-09-13, v91): on the retained real resource-text
leaf, `component check --source --local-contracts` completes the baseline in
10.822s and loop edit in 10.347s, rejects an incorrect resource ID in 15.138s,
and returns the repaired loop product from Nix cache in 1.009s. No pilot rebuild
is needed. Each new functional model links the already validated authored GOTO
object; it does not compile authored C a second time. Source-contract checks,
ordinary host/PE32 compilation and original model compilation remain measured
separately in the build log.

Baseline phases: preparation 0.095s, compiler 0.368s, model 0.304s, checker
processes 5.840s. The original-comparison query includes 0.657s symbolic execution,
0.113s SMT conversion and 4.326s solver time; these are subdivisions, not extra
time to add to the checker total. The loop edit's corresponding subphases are
0.712s, 0.120s and 3.256s. The repair's retained build log describes the earlier
loop build, not newly executed work. This is an exact product hit, not consumer
proof reuse after a neighbor change. Audit:
`practical-assurance/public-original-v1/audit.json`. Runtime compatibility and
real-network composition remain unfinished.

Compact real original/source comparison (2026-09-13, v90): retained resource-text
inputs need no pilot rebuild. With arbitrary current bytes, the full 500-byte
buffer and the ordinary selected service profile, baseline and loop-edited C
complete in 5.292s and 5.485s of checker time. Model generation takes about
0.003s; compilation 0.113s/0.102s; symbolic execution 0.688s/0.680s; SMT conversion
0.116s/0.119s; solving 4.309s/4.523s. The complete selected safety/unwinding checks
remain active. The model does not assume a NUL output on service failure.

The prototype exposed two implementation costs: sending original memory accesses
back through typed callbacks caused recursive function-pointer expansion; direct
access to the existing sparse world removes that recursion. SAT still timed out
at 60s on the corrected model, whereas the existing pinned SMT array backend
completes it. Havoc must target a dedicated object or a scalar temporary: applying
object havoc to a field pointer can corrupt its enclosing context. The retained
failures and corrected models remain available for inspection.

Audit: `practical-assurance/compact-shared-machine-audit-v1.json`. These numbers
describe a conditional functional experiment, not completed public edit latency,
runtime qualification or a like-for-like speedup over the old full-world proof.
Current rendering reproduces the retained model bytes. Public exact evidence
binding, source-opacity checks and caller/runtime composition remain next.

Shared-consumer extension (2026-09-13): the same evidence-rebinding path now
supports shared-state/service source theorems consuming checked fixed-memory
dependencies. The consumer's own service/alias contract must also remain exactly
unchanged. Service count, current memory, aliases, frames and dependency transport
are checked in the existing sparse event model. Supplier implementations remain
absent; only their declared fixed view bytes enter memory-summary functional keys.
Thus dependency view size can still increase model cost even with small supplier
bodies. No shared-callee or transitive machine-summary authority is granted.

The public Nix-backed full-extent resource-text run completes normal checking in
8.014s and a changed module-reader implementation in 7.724s. The latter reuses
both consumer queries and all GOTO bytes without model compilation; ordinary
host/PE32 source compile checks still run. Wrong returned alias takes 5.624s,
incompatible reader extent 8.919s, and a changed service-call bound 5.757s.
Exact repair takes 1.148s by reusing the already realized grown product, so that
time is a Nix cache hit, not another supplier-rebinding measurement. Individual
runs overlap repository validation. `public-shared-memory-v2/audit.json` retains
the build-log phase timings and verifies model bodies, compiler inputs and current
producer hashes. Solver timings combine symbolic execution and solving.

A separate synthetic mutable-copy fixture with eight output bytes and nine
memory events exhausted 60s in the input-dependence check, although its frame
check passed. The one-byte/two-event rule fixture passes, including the negative
event-capacity control. This is an unresolved performance limitation, not evidence
that the real 500-byte buffer domain was narrowed or that mutable summaries scale.

Independent-consumer reuse checkpoint (2026-09-13): fixed-memory auxiliary source
theorems now have a supplier-rebinding path that retains existing consumer GOTO
models, inventories and solver outputs. The regression requalifies a changed leaf
and reuses two consumer levels in different directories with compiler and solver
subprocesses forbidden. Incompatible dependency extent changes rebuild and reject
the call. Evidence validation still checks current source, contract, model meaning,
tools and supplied proof artifacts; composition receipts change with the supplier.
This demonstrates avoided consumer compilation for the supported auxiliary theorem
domain, not real-network latency or complete application-proof reuse.

Retained measurements use the existing one-byte-view fixture, not a narrowed
Metapad input domain. Initial middle/outer construction takes 0.870s/0.980s;
current rebinding takes 0.395s/0.660s with compiler and solver execution forbidden.
The current runs separately record preparation at 0.067s/0.131s and evidence reuse
at 0.300s/0.474s; remaining wrapper/package work is included in total latency.
The incompatible extent control rechecks and fails both its writable-frame and
dependency-view obligations. These individual runs establish model retention,
not a general throughput estimate. Evidence: `consumer-contract-reuse-v1/` and
`consumer-contract-reuse-v2/` under the practical-assurance directory.

The earlier retained application partition run is terminal: 958 properties pass,
one exact-output-read validity assertion exhausts its deadline, and 107 properties
remain unexecuted. Wall time is 2,451.102s; cumulative query time is 4,214.818s,

bounded incomplete check, and further subdivision is not the new network milestone.

Exact-consumer path witnesses (2026-09-13, v87): retained Metapad v85 source
admission, matching `lookahead_one` and matching `tail` are witnessed in 29.288s,
29.710s and 34.016s. Coverage inventory construction costs 0.684s and the processed

against their original model, tool, argument and output bindings. These runs
overlap the application property-partition run, so their times are not isolated

`application-path-coverage-audit-v1.json` in the retained practical-assurance tree.

This is bounded existential coverage under the same five runtime premises,
removing correctness assertions under the existing nonvacuity policy while
retaining original assumptions and unwind bounds. It establishes that these
admitted paths exist, not their correctness or coverage of every lifetime,
allocation and failure case. The 1,066-property partition run is separate and
finished incomplete, as recorded above.
Retain failed parent-query costs when evaluating subdivision, and keep cumulative
process time separate from parallel wall time. No strong-baseline speedup follows.

Public read/decision edit (2026-09-13, v86): the actual Metapad boundary now
includes the unchanged byte-read call and failure return. A checked common-prefix

Normal/repair transport 25 existing bounded safety queries without application
solver runs; repair also reuses the local query and processed-context relation.

The normal source-edit check takes 92.603s, including baseline-context work of

totals overlap. Public wall time includes discovery, realization and rendering;

separate symbolic-execution/solver durations, so its full check time must not be
reported as solver time alone.

This demonstrates an ergonomic wider boundary and a measured public edit cycle,
not a complete application-proof speedup. Most normal-edit time here is spent
producing and comparing the four processed compiler inventories. Repeated repair
reuses that work, while a new edit still requires context validation. The original

pilot rebuild or link run occurs. See [scope and controls](current-goal.md) and
`public-effect-prefix-v1/phase-measurements.json`.

(6.114s symbolic execution, 1.965s SSA conversion; no completed solver timing).

Preparation costs 0.015s; initial compilation 0.493s; qualification compilation

A local adapter edit qualifies in 12.563s; its preparation and model steps total

The successful protocol is a revised boundary, not evidence that a generic full
registry snapshot is cheap. Pointer/by-value generic variants time out near 240s;

The protocol retains capacity and checks its stronger phase premises at the actual
callers. A complete matched application-proof speedup remains unestablished. See

`image-admission-protocol-audit-v1.json`.

Application byte-memory refinement (2026-09-13, v84): the exact v81 model and
1,047-property selection fail at `lookahead_one` in 193.170s when their external

Preparation is 0.009s and compilation 0.540s. Qualification and consumer
instrumentation take 1.226s/1.240s; inventories and property discovery are
recorded separately. No pilot rebuild or link run occurs. This is a correctness
refinement, not a demonstrated speedup: the new pure observer retains the
existing sparse-memory logic. Remaining performance work should factor the
actual incoming application boundary, including contents correspondence,
instead of sampling further solver variants on this model. See
[current evidence](current-goal.md).

The wrong-byte contract is rejected in 27.766s and cannot reuse the original

The seven-query audit retains the incomplete aggregate attempt as non-reusable

The implementation rewrite qualifies in 34.545s; a prefix violation fails the

The snapshot is real caller storage with checked content correspondence and
separation. The original query retains its original model binding; reuse is not

initializers and nine original service-consumer assertions remain. No pilot or

Origin read-footprint qualification (2026-09-13, v82): the unchanged retained

Preparation takes 0.010s, compilation 0.587s and the raw function inventory

Full-parent composition experiment (2026-09-13, v79): all 682 contract guards
pass in 155.452s on the retained parent. All 369 authored assertions time out

Consuming all three reader summaries in the full parent exposes a contract

bindings are retained alongside the drivers. No pilot rebuild or link run was

First-run compiler/model/local-query phases total 2.275s/89.603s/0.019s. Repair

The retained-input probe emits approximately 595 MB of processed function JSON
per model. Production compares all four models once per common query policy,
sharing that check across 25 property partitions. It records inventory digests
and retains checked function summaries and the editable function; the bulky
regenerable JSON is transient. Repair may reuse this context only when all four
raw model hashes, checker tools, query policy and local source comparison agree
and current readers accept every retained artifact. Newly changed contexts still
incur the first-run cost; this is not a claim of cheap arbitrary local edits.

The exact 25 original process outputs also match retained baseline timing rows:
380.393 summed process seconds across a 126.726s elapsed span. The processes
ran concurrently, so their sum is not wall time. This historical query-only span
excludes baseline preparation and differs from the current full public command;

and solving at 0.000111s/0.000029s. These extra local measurements are outside

Evidence: `public-query-transport-v2/{result,timings}.json`,

Next prioritize complete component-level composition and contract refinement;

Earlier checkpoint v76:

Two separate verbose checks on the exact public models measure the correct
query's symbolic execution at 0.000608s and solver at 0.000124s; the incorrect

The large test source inventory is passed through a Nix file after its JSON

With retained complete GOTO models, inventory loading costs 0.754s, context and

Invalidation lesson: a same-contract source edit can be checked against the

The specification-binding audit also corrected an invalidation gap: ordinary
function signatures omit CBMC's separate `contract::` clauses. Bind the actual

edit can preserve that identity while still requiring callee requalification;
a clause edit must not inherit a signature-only dependency key. The retained
consumer clauses all match under the stronger checker. See [audit and limits](current-goal.md).

Complete-inventory measurement (2026-09-12): the existing partitioner on the

`practical-assurance/real-text-memory-region-audit-v2.json`.

are diagnostic transformations, with no production summary reader. The remaining

attempt above passes safety, unwind and relation nonvacuity.

Despite assertion placement before either implementation, symbolic execution

experiment with changed inputs, not a matched comparison or measured speedup.

The three-fact consumer hypothesis adds the separately checked IAT-read
permission. Preparation takes 0.014 seconds and compilation 0.508 seconds;
the selected query remains incomplete after 180.21 seconds. Its partial output
reports 12.7901 seconds symbolic execution, 405,497 expression steps and 3.93769
seconds SSA conversion. No final solver runtime or clause count was emitted.
Do not label the remaining wall time as a measured solver phase. All previous
paired source, exact fragment and header files remain byte-identical; the two
consumer variants add only their listed cut hypotheses to the prior harness.
There is no proof reuse, public qualification or complete local edit measurement.
Audit: `practical-assurance/native-admission-cut-refinement-audit-v1.json`.

Conditional permission abstraction (2026-09-12): adding a shared residual access
function and preserving tracked allocation admission costs 0.48 seconds to compile
the retained real consumer. The exit-control query fails in 123.79 seconds:
12.83 seconds symbolic execution and 104.37 seconds solving, with 6.39 million
variables and 24.93 million clauses. Correcting a discovered target-read fault
channel yields another counterexample in 72.84 seconds (12.99 symbolic execution,
52.87 solver seconds). This is not a matched proof speedup: both queries fail,
their adapter semantics differ, and targeted validation may overlap execution.

A public two-second-budget integration takes 86.17 seconds, executes 47 consumer
and 59 entry processes and reuses none from the four-contract predecessor. Both
entry and region remain incomplete. The contract and model changes correctly
invalidate those keys; unchanged application C alone cannot justify reuse. The
public run precedes the subsequent fault-channel repair. The ordinary world
renderer, without the new contract, retains identical bytes in the checked small
baseline. Audit: `practical-assurance/native-admission-contract-audit-v1.json`.

Native permission diagnosis (2026-09-12): a retained PE32 prefix replay compiles
and links in 0.37 seconds after 0.003 seconds preparation. Three executions take
8.90/6.31/6.33 seconds, including Wine launch and first-prefix setup. There is no
solver or pilot rebuild. Native denial makes the original null store fault;
deliberate admission reproduces the disagreement; successful original/portable
prefixes reach the same existing cut with matching bytes. This is finite replay
latency, not a proof speedup or complete edit cycle. The initial failed compile
(missing the unreachable-service stub) is retained separately. The audit is
`practical-assurance/native-admission-prefix-audit-v1.json`.

The next model change must bind native permission context and fault behavior.
Changing those semantics invalidates affected paired and entry queries; their
old successes cannot be imported merely because the application C is unchanged.
Unchanged independently bound contract evidence remains a candidate for reuse.

Measured application-first diagnostic (2026-09-12): the public unchanged-model
check returns an exit-control counterexample in 123.11 seconds. The selected
consumer query costs 98.75 seconds; 42 consumer processes are reused and one is
fresh. Entry qualification reuses 168 processes and executes three regrouped
queries (0.144, 0.147 and 0.112 seconds). Both model hashes and all application
inputs and contract identities match the preceding legacy run. New grouping
correctly requires new process keys; the driver's all-171 reuse expectation was
wrong and is recorded separately from the successful public command.

This is a useful diagnostic result, not a matched cold speedup: the new order
uses safety evidence completed by the legacy continuation. The legacy order
never reached the exit-control query before its metadata budget exhaustion.
The counterexample exposes exact-side address-zero admission versus a portable
empty-view fault; it does not establish a native application defect. Further
budget escalation or proof splitting cannot substitute for matching permission
and fault semantics. The complete local edit/refine/reuse milestone remains open.
Audits: `consumer-application-first-audit-v1.json` and
`consumer-admission-counterexample-v1.json` under `practical-assurance/`.

Earlier real consumer safety and scheduling (2026-09-12): the 180-second continuation
uses the same 2,013,059-byte paired model and unchanged application inputs. All
38 safety/unwinding queries pass (23,074 sites), followed by nine successful
and fourteen exhausted authored-query attempts, including subdivision parents.
Five singleton context/metadata/extent assertions remain unresolved. Nonvacuity
passes in 55.72 seconds. Ordinary queries reuse 19 processes and execute 47;
the independent entry reuses all 171 processes with zero fresh entry queries.
Compilation costs 0.469 seconds. Safety consumes 618.01 worker-seconds and the
authored attempts 3,427.48 worker-seconds; these parallel sums are not wall time.

The initial public command ran for 1,470.48 seconds but its mutable-source client
failed while importing the updated result reader after the immutable Nix build
finished. A pinned-client recovery takes 2.24 seconds with no proof rerun. Retain
both costs and do not present either as successful cold qualification latency.
The audited receipt and raw outputs are indexed by
`practical-assurance/consumer-qualified-entry-audit-v2.json`. Subsequent clients
are pinned to the same immutable source snapshot as their engines. Some focused
validation overlapped the legacy proof run; these timings are observations, not
an isolated speedup comparison.

V14 conditional SAT scheduling now checks application exit observations before
transported-memory and descriptor-metadata assertions. It retains V13 safety
groups, assertion instrumentation, full inventory and exact process keys. Old
receipts retain their old ordering; no generic cache admission rule is relaxed.
Controlled tests show an actual C counterexample can be returned before a
metadata timeout, and that passing observables alone never qualify the shard.
The full legacy-to-new-policy test passes both readers and reuses only identical
queries. All 50 selected Nix tests and four repository checks pass. The public
same-model comparison returned the audited counterexample above; complete
component assurance and full edit-latency improvement remain unmeasured.

Earlier audited public entry reuse (2026-09-12): the same real Metapad entry model is
fully qualified in both public variants under the four existing runtime contracts.

The baseline already reuses partial evidence and is not a cold benchmark.
Ordinary queries use two seconds and entry queries use 180 seconds. Both variants

`practical-assurance/public-entry-qualified-v2/result.json`.

Consumer cost after entry reuse (2026-09-12): a public continuation at 30 seconds
per ordinary query takes 676.25 seconds, while reusing every entry process. The

including subdivision parents. This is not 989.01 seconds of wall time. The final

are retained alongside the 171 entry records. Evidence:

The same exhausted pointer singleton passes in 36.79 seconds with a larger budget
and verbose timing, using the preserved paired GOTO bytes without compilation.
Symbolic execution costs 13.58 seconds, SSA conversion 3.48 and solving 16.04.
This attributes one real consumer timeout to its budget, without establishing
complete safety, component qualification or a speedup. The unchanged public
consumer resumes from the validated partial evidence at 180 seconds per query;
`practical-assurance/public-consumer-qualified-entry-v2/result.json` records its
pending result. No pilot or native link is rebuilt.

Earlier retained entry qualification (2026-09-12): after the earlier budget
continuations, the unchanged real entry passes all 259 authored assertion sites,
all safety partitions and nonvacuity. The final continuation at 180 seconds per
query takes 417.78 seconds: 101 fresh processes and 40 reused processes.
Reference-address validity passes in 120.43 seconds. These costs exclude prior
continuations and are not cold qualification latency.

Revalidation against the actual edited public model takes 0.91 seconds and
reuses all 141 entry processes, with a guard forbidding fresh solver execution.
The entry model, preparation binding and prefix relation match while the paired
model changes. This is complete retained entry-proof reuse; it excludes source

neighboring business-component proof reuse or whole-component edit latency.
Evidence: `practical-assurance/retained-entry-budget-v3/` and
`practical-assurance/retained-entry-edit-replay-v1/`.

`component check --conditional --entry-query-timeout SECONDS` now gives this
first-time entry qualification its own budget while `--query-timeout` controls
ordinary queries. Without an override the budgets remain equal. This is a
scheduling control: semantic recipes, obligations and exact evidence keys remain
unchanged. Entry timing records include the selected budget. The public
integration above uses 180 seconds for entry queries and two seconds for ordinary
queries; the next consumer check can reuse the complete entry evidence.

The first public budget integration failed after 601.35 seconds because a legacy
incomplete ordinary receipt had discarded its property evidence when coverage
timed out. Its retained but unpublished inventory could not be imported. This
is failed workflow cost, not qualification latency. New conditional receipts
retain both property and coverage results and validate their aggregate selection.
Legacy receipts lacking property evidence admit only coverage cache entries;
unpublished property files cause fresh execution instead of an attempted import.
The strict reader still rejects corrupt or unbound admitted outputs. Failed-build
retention is enabled on the rerun; the independent retained qualification and
edit-replay evidence was unaffected.

Independent entry query reuse (2026-09-11): a real public counter edit reuses
46 completed safety queries and four inventories across an unchanged independent
entry model. The paired model changes. Evidence binds model and tool bytes,
arguments, working directory, runtime contracts and retained outputs. Exhausted
attempts remain diagnostics and cannot become reusable successes.

| Phase or result | Baseline | Counter edit |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Complete public command, seconds | 623.98 | 275.02 |
| Entry compilation, seconds | 0.506 | 0.733 |
| Entry type inventory, seconds | 0.326 | 0.387 |
| Additional compiler inventories, seconds | 2.185 | 2.244 |

| Entry properties / nonvacuity | incomplete / incomplete | incomplete / incomplete |

These commands use the same eight-second query limit and retained application
inputs, with only the actual C loop rewrite and regenerated source package
changed. Targeted validation ran concurrently. They measure incomplete diagnostic
costs, not complete component-proof/edit latency or an isolated speedup. Five
boundary requirements and eleven deferred regions remain. No pilot or link runs.

The entry has 259 assertion sites and 21,638 safety sites. Baseline safety costs
326.53 cumulative worker-seconds in 46 successes and 304.98 worker-seconds in 38
exhausted attempts; workers overlap. The edit reuses those successes but still
exhausts 46 safety attempts and nonvacuity. Neither reaches authored assertions.
The terminal single pointer-safety query passes in 7.79 seconds when retried:
an eight-second budget is sensitive to preparation cost and contention.

A retained-model continuation raises only the per-query limit to 30 seconds.
All 25 safety queries pass, including unwinding; four assertion queries pass
before `spx-bisimulation-view-reference-byte-index` exhausts its budget.
Nonvacuity also exhausts 30 seconds. The run takes 135.56 seconds
without compiler or pilot work. All 35 queries are fresh: its working directory
differs from Nix's `/build`, so prior exact evidence is correctly ineligible.
This result diagnoses a poor subdivision budget and identifies the next native
view assertion; it is not a public edit-speed comparison or entry qualification.
Retained evidence: `practical-assurance/retained-entry-budget-v1/`.

A later verbose query on the identical original model passes byte-index in
45.91 seconds. It reports 4.86 seconds symbolic execution, 2.13 seconds SSA
conversion and 37.82 seconds SAT solving; 191,100 symbolic steps produce 3.01
million variables and 11.56 million clauses. The previous timed-out output's last
`converting SSA` message was buffered evidence, not a reliable phase diagnosis.

The 90-second continuation uses the same local working directory and exact prior
entry evidence: 33 reused processes and ten fresh processes, 380.16 seconds
overall. It passes scratch admission, owner, alignment and escape assertions,
and nonvacuity in 54.97 seconds. Reference-address validity still exhausts 90
seconds; subsequent assertions remain unchecked. The command includes exhausted
parent groups followed by successful subdivision, so ten fresh processes do not
mean ten newly completed proofs. No compilation or pilot runs occur.

An explicit span-bound assertion in the same real harness passes byte-index in
35.20 seconds but needs another 45.18 seconds to prove the added bound. The
baseline GOTO reproduces the public model exactly. Since no overall benefit is
established, this candidate is not adopted. Z3 on the original byte-index query
exhausts 60 seconds, and an aggregate of all 259 original assertions exhausts
90 seconds. These are bounded, non-authorizing experiments, not evidence that
the original statements are false. They do not support changing proof policy.

The current small reference-view fixture qualifies all entry assertions, safety
and nonvacuity. A repeat takes 3.32 seconds overall and reuses all 51 entry
queries with fresh CBMC execution forbidden; the first full fixture run takes
71.59 seconds alongside other checks. This is a small-fixture measurement.

All 51 targeted Nix tests and four repository checks pass. The coverage observer
now has a native-Boolean no-op body so ordinary safety checks retain missing-callee
checks without failing on that observer. This changes compiled model hashes;
old models are not relabeled as matching evidence. Audit:
`practical-assurance/entry-queries-audit-v1.json`. Compiler inventories still
account for about 195 MB per entry; totals above include query outputs and failed
attempts. Neither partial query reuse nor the small fixture establishes
neighboring business-component proof reuse.

Earlier public compiled-prefix correspondence (2026-09-11): both retained real counter
variants now match through the conditional `component check` path. Each comparison
covers 464 instruction pairs, all 43 unknown branches, two false-assumption exits
and one guarded frontier; all 289 other compiled functions and static state match.
The entry GOTO bytes and relation digest survive the actual counter edit, while
the paired model hashes differ. Exact model/root/inventory bindings remain visible
and checked. Full source conformance and entry obligations remain unchecked.

Baseline/edit entry compilation takes **0.607/0.686 seconds**, type inspection
**0.368/0.360 seconds**, the additional compiled inventories **2.314/2.186 seconds**,
and structural comparison **2.212/1.922 seconds**. Complete public diagnostic
commands take **50.50/48.93 seconds**, including Nix, preparation and deliberately
exhausted ordinary queries at two seconds each. Repository validation ran alongside
these experiments. Both commands remain incomplete with five boundary requirements
and eleven deferred regions. These timings do not establish complete component
proof/edit latency. No pilot rebuild or native link occurs.
Retaining the full compiler inventories currently costs approximately **195 MB
per entry variant**. This storage cost remains to be reduced before large networks;
the measured comparison latency does not imply inexpensive evidence retention.

The actual public entry's selected escape assertion passes in **7.14 seconds**.
The earlier comparison, with startup generated at compilation, passed in **6.75
seconds**; its compiled negative disables the probe in the real calling harness.
Correspondence rejects that change and CBMC violates the escape assertion in
**42.98 seconds** (5.14 seconds symbolic execution, 2.07 seconds SSA conversion,
32.39 seconds solver time; 3.10 million variables and 11.91 million clauses).
The public compilation convention is now shared by both models: CBMC generates
startup from the explicitly selected query root. The ordinary public model was
reproduced byte for byte before this adjustment; no differing initializer was
silently discarded. Selected escape checks do not qualify full entry safety,
coverage, a component theorem or public entry/neighbor proof reuse.

The checker retains effects, DECL/DEAD order, failed-pointer bindings, helper
bodies and semantic metadata; it rejects silent cycles and unsupported preludes.
Three targeted Nix shards pass **22 tests without skips**, and all four repository
checks pass. Original public assertion and cover queries still use the original
model. Audit: `practical-assurance/entry-cfg-correspondence-audit-v1.json`.

Public entry preparation (2026-09-11): the production conditional path now
compiles the compiler-derived entry source separately and checks the actual
compiled type/tag closure. It retains `source-entry/model.goto`, its generated
source, preparation binding, commands, compiler output and include inventory.
Existing public details distinguish `compiled_types_checked: true` from
`source_conformance_checked: false` and `entry_obligations_checked: false`.
The preparation is not a proof receipt. Original assertion, safety and cover
queries still use the original paired model; no proof reuse is claimed here.

`public-entry-preparation-v1` runs the real Metapad baseline and counter edit
through the public conditional command with a two-second per-query limit.
Regenerating the edited source package changes only that manifest and its C
file; all other material and supplier inputs are identical. Entry models have
identical GOTO bytes and boundary bindings; paired models differ. Compilation
of the independent entry takes **0.517/0.550 seconds**, and compiled type
inventory takes **0.341/0.351 seconds**. Complete commands take **43.80/44.46
seconds**, including Nix/preparation and deliberately exhausted ordinary queries.
Repository validation also ran on the host, so these are observed diagnostic
latencies, not controlled solver or completed component-edit benchmarks. Both
commands remain incomplete with five boundary requirements and eleven deferred
regions. No pilot rebuild or native link occurs.

The preparation profile rejects stateful pre-BEGIN prefixes, stable constants
and aliases rather than forgetting them. Only discarded nonvolatile parameter
values may precede BEGIN; dereferences and arithmetic remain unsupported.
Parameter escape changes the havoc dependency. Tests check recursive type
closure, changed widths/field order, source tampering, and public packets that
forge conformance, theorem status or deferred execution. Three targeted Nix
shards pass all **29 tests without skips**. Source-entry conformance, routing
entry queries to the new model, full entry obligations and public neighboring
proof reuse remain open. Audit:
`practical-assurance/public-entry-preparation-audit-v1.json`.

Entry-isolation comparison (2026-09-11): `entry-source-isolation-v1` inventories
the real source separately for baseline, counter edit, repair, 1,000 added
post-cut statements and a boundary-local type change. Its generated entry uses
the unchanged proof header and relation harness. Baseline/edit/repair/growth
produce identical GOTO bytes and boundary bindings; changing `length` from
`uint32_t` to `uint64_t` changes both. The source operation's loop temporaries
are absent in its compiler inventory. Source inventory compilation takes
0.033-0.046 seconds, inventory extraction 0.056-0.103 seconds, entry compilation
0.55-0.62 seconds and compiled type/body inspection 1.23-1.28 seconds. Complete
preparation/compilation/inspection takes 2.23-2.38 seconds per variant. This is
entry-model construction latency, not complete local component-edit latency.

On this prototype the two original entry predicates pass in 102.68/95.78
seconds, escape-from-cut rejection in 5.96 seconds and nonvacuity in 57.69
seconds. Its selected predicate still has 192,522 symbolic steps and about
12 million SAT clauses: body absence fixes the measured invalidation boundary,
not the native-reference solver cost. Existing exact-query caching reuses four
completed checks for each edit/repair/growth model, with zero fresh queries;
the type change has no hits. No public proof packet is manufactured.

Full entry checking then finds
`spx-bisimulation-memory-fact-construction-order:allocated:scratch_tail` in
both isolated and original models (54.88/55.43 seconds). The trace fails before
the source operation is called: eager stack-cache reset has already observed
initial bytes before the scratch-tail fact constructor. Selected predicates and
coverage did not establish this obligation. Production generation now defers
stack-cache population whenever initial-memory facts are selected. Reads use
the existing byte-log fallback, full stores populate the cache and overlapping
partial stores invalidate it. Existing eager initialization remains when there
are no such facts. The regression checks arbitrary bytes, paired correspondence,
full/partial writes, reset and actual late-observation rejection; it fails on
the original generator and passes on the corrected one. Two Nix shards pass
all 24 tests without skips.

The corrected retained entry compiles for baseline and edit to identical GOTO
bytes. Construction order passes in 7.65 seconds; nonvacuity passes in 74.54
seconds. Both selected checks reuse after the real edit in 0.008 seconds with
zero fresh queries. The full corrected entry still times out at 120 seconds;
this is a correctness repair, not a completed performance or qualification
claim. The source-isolation profile rejects pre-BEGIN storage/code, stable
aliases/constants and unsupported type spellings. Actual parameter escape
changes the havoc dependency; changing a compiled record layout rejects the
old boundary binding. General source-entry conformance, public entry admission,
real effect guards and neighboring component proof reuse remain open. Audit:
`practical-assurance/entry-source-isolation-audit-v1.json`. No pilot or link run.
The prior measurements below retain the pre-correction results explicitly.

Byte-frame composition measurement (2026-09-11): `input-byte-frame-summary-v2`
checks a public, readable byte with no matching write/shadow history and either
no allocation or a live imported allocation. Under this explicit predicate, the
complete retained one-byte read equals its initial-memory value, including its
observation effect. The arbitrary-state bounded lemma passes in **15.13 seconds**;
an overlapping-write invocation fails its domain guard in **0.82 seconds**. The
two original outgoing text-byte reads then consume the candidate summary. The
compound text assertion passes in **22.98 seconds**, while both required domain
queries time out at 120.05 seconds. This is not a completed consumer proof.

The event-transport version records a predicate at entry and invalidates its
witness on overlapping writes, shadow installation, relevant allocation/release,
exposure changes and initial-fill changes. Ghost capture restores the original
observation flag; successful consumption performs the original observation.
The revised arbitrary-state actual-write transport check passes in **9.74
seconds**, an overlapping-write invocation is rejected in 1.06 seconds, and
concrete lifecycle/effect controls pass in **1.74 seconds**. Those controls retain
null allocation, unrelated fresh allocation, failed release, successful release,
address reuse without revival, failed overflowing stores, shadow and call-range
effects, and exposure invalidation. Deleting the real write-invalidation hook
violates the same transport law in **1.95 seconds**. These bounded proof-runtime
checks do not establish arbitrary application confinement or native qualification.

Separate checks capture the entry predicate before source restoration and assert
it after the original full incoming relation, without application effects. SAT
establishes the exact/source predicates in **101.73/94.19 seconds** on identical
GOTO bytes to the two 120-second SMT timeouts. Entry nonvacuity subsequently passes
in **56.94 seconds**. Its model reports 192,522 symbolic steps, about 3.12 million
SAT variables and 11.95 million clauses, despite executing no application effects.
Its initial-world setup matches the paired execution byte-for-byte after
assertion-label normalization. These selected entry assertions and nonvacuity
do not constitute full safety or entry qualification.

`tracked-byte-frame-composition-v1` consumes that entry dependency explicitly in
an experimental constructor, keeping the generic checked constructor for helper
validation. No input byte value, successful allocation or lifetime premise is
added. The compound text assertion passes in **23.24 seconds**, but both required
effect guards remain incomplete at 120 seconds with SMT and SAT. Symbolic execution
takes about 12.6 seconds and produces 385,325 steps. An isolated observation's
reachable call graph contains only startup/initialization and its witness reader;
the whole application still calls the original accessors and reference helpers.
This does not establish whole-consumer body absence or body-growth independence.

The measured result supports factoring native-view admission and access into
reusable contracts. The byte law and event witness alone leave those implementations
inside the component model. Do not register this candidate, claim practical
latency or count a conditional text assertion as completed preservation. Public
composition admission, full entry/consumer qualification and real edit/neighbor
reuse remain open. The audit checks source deltas, actual mutation-hook removal,
matched setup phases, model/command/raw-output bindings and the scoped call graph:
`practical-assurance/byte-frame-composition-audit-v1.json`. No pilot rebuild or
native link occurs. The initial prototype's include-order failure and the first
body-scope driver's output-path failure are retained; neither produced proof
evidence.

`entry-local-edit-invalidation-v1` measures an ordinary C counter rewrite in the
first two-iteration prefix loop, after the allocated cut: ascending `k` becomes
descending `k`, and that loop body does not use the counter. The entry relation
stops at the earlier cut. The entry source prefix, cut header and relation harness
remain byte-identical. Baseline, edited and repaired variants use the same virtual
source paths and compile in **0.55/0.56/0.55 seconds**. The edit changes the entry
GOTO hash; repair restores the exact baseline hash. No solver query is run and no
application-equivalence or neighboring-proof reuse result is claimed. Entry-only
execution therefore has not delivered implementation-independent model binding.
Factor a model with absent dependency bodies before reusing such evidence; do not
ignore source or model hashes to simulate reuse. The new boundary must retain
semantic dependencies on entry types/layouts, aliases, relation recipes and
selected runtime contracts. Result and retained variants:
`practical-assurance/entry-local-edit-invalidation-v1/result.json`.

Reference-lifetime isolation (2026-09-11): a proof-only flag scopes diagnostic
assertions to the original outgoing caption translator. Its lookup, current
lifetime call, return guards and full application assertion remain. No assumption
is added. Four queries on one compiled model, at concurrency two, give:
