| --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
| Issued-origin selection | Satisfied | 101.85 | 83.90 |
| Current-world lifetime | Incomplete | 120.17 | Unreported |
| Resulting address | Satisfied | 110.91 | 92.20 |
| Permissions and span | Satisfied | 104.65 | 86.53 |

Compilation takes 0.52 seconds; the four queries together take 224.82 seconds
wall time. Symbolic execution takes 12.30–12.93 seconds per query and SSA
conversion 3.26–3.48 seconds. These are conditional checks at the translator's
existing control points, not independent admission or complete application
proofs. Source-delta, command and raw-output audit:
`practical-assurance/reference-stages-audit-v1.json`.

A following checked origin witness normalizes the selected image record's base,
extent and lifetime generation only after asserting equality. The original
lifetime test remains, and the witness is queried with the full caption
assertion. That query times out at 120.20 seconds; a separate reachability query
before the witness assertion also times out at 120.18 seconds. This does not
establish a useful consumer optimization or reachability. No production change
is made from this candidate (`practical-assurance/image-origin-witness-v1/`).

The next candidate factors the image/allocation separation that allocation
admission already checks. For a nonempty image-contained span with lifetime
generation one, it asserts the current allocation table is disjoint from the
image, assumes that checked premise, and returns the equivalent lifetime result.
Other inputs retain the original lifetime helper. A separate comparison uses
five exact retained helper bodies and a reduced world structure with all their
accessed fields. Under image disjointness, the original and specialized functions
return the same result for arbitrary query values, arbitrary valid image intervals
and three arbitrary allocation records in **12.57 seconds**. An overlapping-image
control fails the guard in 0.06 seconds; deleting the guard changes the lifetime
result and fails equivalence in 0.08 seconds. These checks establish the scoped
helper lemma, not an original-binary or whole native-runtime validation.

The real combined caption/frame query still times out at **120.22 seconds**.
Separate premise/application queries on the same compiled model also time out
at 120.22/120.20 seconds. The checked shortcut is neither registered nor
integrated. Audit: `practical-assurance/image-lifetime-audit-v1.json`.

The next comparison explicitly trusts the mapped-image lifetime instead of
re-establishing image/heap disjointness inside each reference lifetime query.
`mapped-image-lifetime-experiment:1` is additional, unregistered trust: a live,
fixed loaded-image interval stays separate from owned heap storage throughout
this execution. Only nonempty image-contained spans with borrowed lifetime
generation one take the shortcut. All nonimage/heap cases retain their original
lifetime implementation; reference origins, metadata, permissions and bytes
are unchanged. This removes the new diagnostic frame guard and assumption,
not any original application assertion. The original caption capture-context
assertion then passes in **102.68 seconds**, after 0.53 seconds compilation.
Audit: `practical-assurance/trusted-image-lifetime-audit-v1.json`; GOTO:
`a48dd85be7de0da9c7ba27a1689c19c45be89a9a3733ba615e52217636f41091`.

All **eight** existing allocation-freshness Nix tests pass without skips. They
exercise the actual emitted native helpers and proof-world allocation paths,
including symbolic image collisions, deleted-guard controls, crossing/adjacent
ranges, null outcomes, borrowed-range identity preservation, host execution
and PE32 compilation. Native image admission is in
`candidate/runtime_range_ownership.py`. These tests and the helper lemma do not
establish whole-runtime invariant composition or prove that arbitrary caller
code cannot corrupt private runtime metadata. Validation audit:
`practical-assurance/image-lifetime-native-validation-audit-v1.json`.

The broader selected-region diagnostic (`trusted-image-lifetime-region-v1`)
finishes **incomplete in 1,099.66 seconds**: 1,045.24 seconds for primary checks
and 54.39 seconds for passing nonvacuity. All 26 safety groups pass (23,008
instrumented IDs), while nine leaf application assertions remain unresolved.
All original requirements remain. The standalone caption pass does not
reproduce within this regional budget, despite the same model and property
command apart from diagnostic output flags. This observation does not isolate
concurrency as the cause. Exact command/output audit:
`practical-assurance/trusted-image-region-audit-v1.json`.

A real ordinary-C edit copies the caption view,
sets its extent to zero and uses the altered view at the existing loop cut.
The original caption assertion rejects it in **84.06 seconds**. Repair compiles
the exact prior GOTO in **0.53 seconds** and reparses its bound passing result
in **0.02 seconds**, with zero new solver queries. The negative GOTO is separately
reconstructed and its hash matches the original negative run. This is selected
assertion evidence, not public component or neighboring-theorem reuse. Audit:
`practical-assurance/trusted-image-edit-audit-v1.json`.

The original four registered runtime contracts remain unchanged; the fifth
premise is not admitted by the public workflow. No pilot rebuild or native link
runs. Some probes and validation overlap the regional run. This is one completed
application assertion, not a complete component speedup, acceptable edit latency
or reuse of neighboring business proofs.

The existing typed-GOTO opacity checker accepts the real cleanup source in
0.069 seconds including separate authored-source compilation and inventory.
A write-and-restore through the opaque service context is rejected in 0.070
seconds with `shared_source_transport_not_opaque`. The lexical C profile accepts
both sources. This separates useful early source-access checking from semantic
frames, lifetime preservation and whole-runtime invariant composition. The
standalone diagnostic does not establish public source-summary admission.

On the original four-contract model, selecting Z3 instead of CaDiCaL proves the
full caption context assertion in **32.50 seconds**, with no compilation, model
change or fifth trust premise (`caption-smt-v1`). All its original command
bounds and assertion flags remain. The recorded installed Z3 version and hash
match the current resolved executable, but that preliminary bare `--z3` command
did not explicitly pin the external child. The follow-up uses existing backend
pinning and process-group timeout cleanup. Audit of source inventories, mutation,
command delta and reparsed raw SMT result:
`practical-assurance/opacity-and-smt-audit-v1.json`.

The broader `hybrid-solver-region-v2` diagnostic is terminal and incomplete in
**510.29 seconds**, including 53.10 seconds passing nonvacuity. It uses packed
SAT safety and single Z3 application queries with field-sensitive array encoding.
All 26 safety groups (23,008 instrumented IDs) and 15 application queries pass.
Text-view metadata and extent remain incomplete at 120.08 seconds each; the
executor then defers **294 of 311** authored assertions. The pinned caption
query passes in 33.44 seconds. The script explicitly overrides only authored
query grouping through the existing singleton implementation; public proof
policy remains unchanged. Of 48 raw queries, 15 reuse exact-model, exact-command
completed inventory/safety outputs and 33 are fresh. Unfinished queries are not
reused. The manifest binds the resolved external solver executable, version and
SHA-256. The fifth image-lifetime premise is absent. Audit:
`practical-assurance/hybrid-solver-region-audit-v2.json`.

The preliminary 32.50-second single-caption output reports symbolic execution
11.98 seconds, equation postprocessing 0.26 seconds, SSA conversion 2.24 seconds
and solver time 16.49 seconds (decision procedure total 18.75 seconds). These
are stages of that diagnostic query, not a decomposition of the later regional
wall time. The region reuses its model, so it runs no preparation/compiler/model
construction, pilot rebuild or native link.

A real ordinary-C caption-extent error under the pinned four-contract policy is
rejected in **53.49 seconds**, after 0.008 seconds preparation and 0.62 seconds
compilation. Repair compiles the exact prior GOTO in **0.56 seconds** and reparses
the completed regional caption output in **0.001 seconds**, with zero fresh
solver queries. The negative GOTO is retained and independently reconstructed;
raw negative and repaired positive outputs are checked by the existing backend.
Audit: `practical-assurance/hybrid-source-edit-audit-v1.json`. This overlaps the
regional run and checks one assertion; it is not public component editing or
neighboring-theorem reuse. Whole-region completion remains unresolved.

The alternate SMT array encoding also leaves both text queries incomplete at
120.07 seconds. Symbolic execution takes 8.47/8.51 seconds, equation postprocessing
0.34/0.35 seconds and SSA conversion 4.98/5.05 seconds; there is no completed
solver time. The two-query run takes 120.07 seconds wall time, reuses the exact
model and changes only array encoding plus diagnostic output flags. The pinned
backend leaves no CBMC/Z3 child running after timeout. Audit:
`practical-assurance/hybrid-text-encoding-audit-v1.json`.

The inspected metadata obligation compares the dynamic outgoing text address's
resolved identity/generation/offset/extent/permissions with the source descriptor.
The extent obligation additionally requires admitted spans, bounded nonempty
NUL extent and preserved zero terminator bytes in both output memories. The next
boundary experiment should use existing frame/service composition to establish
preservation of those incoming text facts across the length call and disjoint
scratch writes. No new preservation summary is yet checked or admitted; fixed
image results and unchanged pointer shapes do not establish these requirements.

Pure-lookup comparison (2026-09-11): `functional-namespace-v1` replaces only the
concrete world resolver with an explicit, unregistered deterministic abstraction.
The key passes allocation count and all active records' live flag, native rule,
base, size and native generation, plus call inputs and normalized selector mask.
Outputs overapproximate the concrete resolver; no successful lookup or application
fact is assumed. All original application assertions remain. Both text queries
still time out at 120.05 seconds, after 12.84 seconds symbolic execution and
2.64–2.68 seconds SSA conversion. This is not a matched concrete input-domain
or whole-consumer speedup.

The exact retained concrete helper separately proves equal status/output and
unchanged worlds for equal namespace projections in **7.34 seconds**, with
arbitrary other world state and the real five-rule, three-slot authority.
A control changing native generation while retaining the other key fields fails
in **2.01 seconds**. The existing native-context/projection Nix shard passes all
four tests with zero skips and the same manifest input digest. It covers status,
outputs, frames and deliberate family/expiry/permission changes, plus body
absence in the existing projection generator. Those tests do not qualify the
new functional abstraction. Audit:
`practical-assurance/namespace-experiments-audit-v1.json`.

Checked namespace normalization at the real loop cut yields:

| Model | Snapshot guard | Address guard | Namespace guard | Original text metadata | Original text extent |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Four-contract concrete lookup | 13.45 s pass | 18.93 s pass | 20.64 s pass | 120.04 s incomplete | 120.04 s incomplete |
| Plus unregistered functional lookup | 14.44 s pass | 20.04 s pass | 21.91 s pass | 120.05 s incomplete | 120.04 s incomplete |

Each substitution follows its queried equality guard. Neither model assumes byte
contents or drops original application assertions. These are selected queries;
safety, nonvacuity and other regions were not rerun. Both candidates remain
unintegrated. Audit: `practical-assurance/text-frame-normalization-audit-v1.json`.

Inspection explains why namespace footprint alone is an insufficient boundary:
`spx_proof_authority_runtime_resolve` still invokes origin registration, which
checks public-range admission, actual allocation lifetime generation and prior
origin consistency. The next experiment must compose preservation of already
issued-reference validity through permitted calls/writes, including invalidation
for release/reuse and conflicting origins. The terminator's current bytes remain
a separate application obligation. Do not treat a pure lookup lemma or stable
native namespace fields as a complete origin/lifetime frame.

Text preservation and explicit cut refinement (2026-09-11): decomposing the
unchanged original text-extent assertion isolates a passing shape/admission
conjunct (21.70 seconds) and unresolved exact/source terminating-byte checks
(120.05 seconds each). An additional entry separation assertion is violated in
44.29 seconds. A stronger same-object/same-generation overlap query is violated
in 31.84 seconds, with text base 226443264, extent 2, scratch base 226443265,
extent 1 and common generation 4194304. These values describe a counterexample
in the admitted internal-cut domain; they are not a native allocation trace.
The new assertions add no assumptions and retain the original compound check.
This demonstrates that separation needs an explicit boundary fact; it does not
establish that aliasing explains every outstanding timeout.

`disjoint-byte-write-law-v1` proves that an arbitrary observed byte outside an
actual one-to-four-byte store is preserved, using the complete retained writer
and byte reader. It keeps arbitrary admitted allocation/write/shadow histories,
private/exposed ranges, store values and fault outcomes within the retained
capacities. The check passes in 20.00 seconds, and an overlapping store that
omits separation fails in 0.90 seconds. This checks a runtime-model frame law;
it is not full native-runtime validation or application composition.

The existing local-view renderer now lowers `bytes_address` through the current
checked input/output machine coordinate. This introduces no new artifact or
production C API. The original descriptor/owner/lifetime checks remain required.
A regression demonstrates valid resumption and endpoint reachability, a valid
interior-view move rejected by the address invariant, and forged generation
rejected by the codec. Both targeted Nix shards pass, totaling 21 tests without
skips; lint, registry, metadata and architecture checks also pass.

`public-conditional-disjoint-cuts-v1` proposes overflow-safe separation using
existing `ule32`, `sub32`, `and` and `or` expressions at all eight scratch cuts.
The public command rejects the stale exact-C proof binding in 10.95 seconds.
Version 2 regenerates the exact slice from the retained transfers in **1.70
seconds**; exact source files and forced labels are byte-identical, with a newly
bound manifest. The public check returns explicit incomplete feedback in
**15.36 seconds**, under a deliberately short one-second query budget. All twelve
models are prepared, the allocated region compiles, nonvacuity times out, and
eleven regions remain deferred. The five prior boundary requirements remain.
This is public proposal/preparation/invalidation feedback, not a completed
refinement, practical proof latency or neighboring-consumer proof reuse. The
application source, original component input contracts and existing supplier
receipt remain unchanged. Internal cut domains are intentionally strengthened;
the allocating predecessor must establish the new fact.

`disjoint-cut-consumers-v1` reconstructs the exact public allocated model and
compiles its allocating predecessor from the same retained preparation (0.53
and 0.55 seconds). Pinned SMT queries both the original full text-extent
assertion and a separately exposed predecessor separation conjunct; each remains
incomplete at 120.05/120.07 seconds. No consumer byte premise is assumed and no
cache candidate is used. Do not promote the strengthened cut to an established
invariant or treat the helper frame law as discharge of these application
obligations. Evidence: `practical-assurance/disjoint-cut-refinement-audit-v1.json`.

The corrected `disjoint-cut-consumers-v2` diagnostic exposes the exact separation
conjunct already rendered in the predecessor invariant, including its checked
output text address. The previous diagnostic redundantly translated that address.
The corrected selected query passes in **95.02 seconds**, after 0.57 seconds
compilation. This changes diagnostic encoding, not the runtime trust selection or
application semantics. The separate real-prefix null/success coverage query still
times out at 120.04 seconds; full predecessor qualification is not established.

On the same public refined preparation, `disjoint-input-address-v1` checks the
current text address after all incoming restoration writes, then uses that equal
coordinate in the original input invariant. The equality guard passes in
**18.25 seconds**, but the original full text-extent assertion remains incomplete
at 120.04 seconds. Preparation takes 0.08 seconds and compilation 0.52 seconds.
The timed-out query reports 12.41 seconds symbolic execution, 380,576 expression
steps, 0.28 seconds equation postprocessing and 2.44 seconds SSA conversion;
there is no completed solver-time measurement. This diagnostic is not integrated.

`disjoint-text-parts-v1` instead retains the unmodified public input relation and
adds three redundant queries of the original outgoing compound assertion. Under
the proposed separation, shape/admission passes in **22.43 seconds**, while the
exact and source terminator conjuncts remain incomplete at 120.05/120.04 seconds.
No application assumption, byte premise or runtime contract is added. Thus the
new separation does not by itself demonstrate tractable current-byte transport.
Do not substitute successful descriptor checks for the failed-to-complete content
checks. Model hashes, precise source changes and raw output hashes for these
follow-ups are checked by `practical-assurance/refined-cut-consumer-audit-v1.json`.
No pilot or native-link rebuild is involved.

The subsequent `byte-write-selection-v1` changes the public byte reader to
select the newest matching write record before evaluating its byte oracle once.
The private-write branch, lifetime floors, shadows, imported/fresh/dead allocation
fallbacks and initial-fill observation behavior remain. An arbitrary-state check
at the retained capacities compares the complete old and new readers, including
the observation flag, and passes in **5.46 seconds**. Selecting the oldest
overlapping write instead fails in **0.82 seconds**. Exact/source reader structure
is identical after world-name substitution; application source, header and other
compiled C files are unchanged.

The original real text-extent assertion nevertheless times out in **120.04
seconds**, after 0.007 seconds preparation and 0.49 seconds compilation. It reports
12.00 seconds symbolic execution, 380,579 expression steps, 0.28 seconds equation
postprocessing and 2.40 seconds SSA conversion. The earlier incoming-address
diagnostic has 380,576 steps: neither candidate establishes a useful model-size
or completed-query improvement. These are different diagnostic models, not a
controlled timing comparison. Keep the write selection experimental; it does
not warrant a production change or another trusted contract. Audit:
`practical-assurance/byte-write-selection-audit-v1.json`.

The next composition experiment must change the dependency boundary, not keep
rearranging equivalent per-byte lookups: establish a live-view and current-byte
frame at entry, consume it through permitted reads/writes and service effects,
and invalidate it on lifetime or relevant metadata changes. Use the existing
summary and service machinery and measure absence of the substituted bodies.
Keep incoming heap contents, overlapping views, failed accesses, release/reuse,
integer limits and effect order explicit. No such composed summary is established
by the diagnostics above. A valid reference or read-only service declaration
alone cannot justify it, and any conditional runtime assumption must remain
explicit and independently validated before public practical-assurance claims.

Issued-reference footprint reuse (2026-09-11): the unregistered diagnostic
`issued-footprint-cache-v1` snapshots successful resolution arguments, allocation
count and eight fields per active allocation, private/exposed ranges and the
observed origin prefix. It preserves both native and actual lifetime generation.
Only exact argument matches with an unchanged footprint may reuse the result;
misses call the complete original resolver. Appended origins are allowed when
the previously observed prefix is unchanged. Current bytes are not cached.

The original text metadata passes in **32.17 seconds**, while original extent
remains incomplete at **120.04 seconds**. Preparation takes 0.011 seconds,
compilation 0.574 seconds and inventory 1.502 seconds. Both queries run together
in 120.045 seconds wall time. All original application assertions and the four
registered contract identities remain, with no new application assumptions.
The candidate substitution itself remains unregistered and requires validation;
assertion preservation alone does not prove its soundness.

`issued-footprint-validation-v1` checks 17 bounded scenarios against the exact
retained full proof resolver. Its 29 fixture assertions pass in **6.37 seconds**,
with bounds, pointer, arithmetic and unwinding checks enabled. The model-wide
property inventory also contains unreachable functions; do not present its
28,802 reported IDs as independent scenario coverage. A separate `--cover cover`
run reaches all 17 scenario endpoints in **6.49 seconds**. The unrelated original
application witness is outside the fixture entry and remains unreached.
The scenarios cover release failure/success, release and same-address reuse,
actual/native generation, extent and requested permission changes, private and
exposed ranges, conflicting and appended origins, current byte writes, null,
one-past and integer-edge requests. Removing actual lifetime generation from the
cache guard produces the required original-status mismatch in **4.15 seconds**.
These are bounded proof-runtime checks, not native-runtime or allocation-creation
qualification.

`issued-footprint-law-v1` attempts the broader conditional law with arbitrary
initial arguments and admitted state, arbitrary subsequent world fields outside
the footprint, and arbitrary appended origins. Initial successful resolution is
an explicit antecedent. It remains incomplete at **120.07 seconds** with SAT;
symbolic execution takes 5.02 seconds and SSA conversion 0.69 seconds, leaving
solver work unresolved. Pinned Z3 on the same model and obligations also times
out at **120.01 seconds**. The general substitution rule is not established.

`issued-footprint-edit-v1` flips one bit of the actual ordinary-C text view's
generation before the loop. Its original metadata assertion remains incomplete
at **120.05 seconds**, so the control has not demonstrated rejection. Restoring
the exact source reconstructs the original model in **0.66 seconds** and reparses
its bound passing metadata output in **0.004 seconds**, with zero fresh queries.
This measures exact restoration only, not a compatible implementation edit or
neighboring-proof reuse. Neither the positive metadata result nor bounded
scenario validation justifies integrating the cache. Its miss path still
contains the original resolver; current bytes, general validity preservation,
complete regional checking and the public workflow remain open. No pilot or
native link was rebuilt. Audit: `practical-assurance/issued-footprint-cache-audit-v1.json`.

The separate `issued-footprint-edit-v2` control sets the real C text generation
to zero. The original metadata assertion rejects it in **84.74 seconds**; repair
compiles in **0.57 seconds** and reparses the exact passing output in **0.003
seconds**, with one reused query and zero fresh queries. An independent source
reconstruction reproduces both negative GOTO hashes (0.57 seconds each). The
bit-flip control remains incomplete; do not replace its result with the easier
zero-generation failure. These controls ran alongside other diagnostic solver
work and do not establish isolated or stable edit latency.

Checked cut-owner transport (2026-09-11): the next experiment removes the
additional runtime-liveness premise. A checked substitution in the write
dispatcher passes its isolated lifetime property in 17.06 seconds, its two
applicability guards in 45.65 seconds and its first 1,024 pointer checks in
20.39 seconds. However, a smaller change at the actual `allocated` cut proves
sufficient for both the dispatcher and the earlier native accessor check:
after the complete restored-descriptor check, assert that the access context
equals the current runtime owner (or null for a null object), then substitute
that equal pointer. The cut-only prototype changes one generated proof header;
authored C, native accessor/overlay bytes, original requirements and the four
selected runtime contracts remain unchanged. Both lifetime properties pass
together in **15.83 seconds**, after **0.56 seconds** compilation. No runtime or
guest-object liveness is assumed by this change. GOTO SHA-256:
`be35dc62efcae6022612214dc0e602334e4eec4a53338fa7aa973c80a850bead`.
Evidence: `practical-assurance/public-cut-owner-only-v1/`.

The broader cut-only run (`cut-owner-region-v1`) uses the existing partitioned
executor, original application checker policy and original requirements plus
the owner guard. All **26 safety query groups pass**, covering **22,990 property
IDs**, including unwinding. Inventory construction takes 3.68 seconds; safety
queries finish 303.84 seconds after executor entry. These are bounded checks in
the boundary model, not evidence that every static property is reachable.
Application checking then completes five assertion queries and times out on
eighteen parent/child queries, including outgoing view-context, metadata and
extent checks. Primary checking remains incomplete at **1,052.81 seconds**;
nonvacuity passes in **52.77 seconds**, giving **1,105.60 seconds** total.
Auxiliary frame probes and eleven other regions are outside this diagnostic run.
The combined owner/complete-input-codec guard query times out at 120.14 seconds.
Separate queries then prove the new owner equality in **37.43 seconds**, while
the existing full input-descriptor assertion remains unresolved at **120.23
seconds**. Safety-query success remains subject to completion of all required
admission assertions. Evidence: `public-cut-owner-guards-v1/` and
`public-cut-owner-guards-v2/`, under `practical-assurance/`.

A follow-up prototype uses guarded snapshot pointers inside the outgoing context
matcher, preserving its false returns and all runtime-field comparisons. The
actual `spx-bisimulation-capture-context:loop:caption` query still times out at
120.23 seconds after 0.59 seconds compilation. This rewrite is not integrated.
Evidence: `practical-assurance/public-context-owner-canonical-v1/`. The completed
source, command/output and coverage audit is `practical-assurance/cut-owner-audit-v1.json`.

The preceding liveness-assumption prototype (`runtime-borrow-region-v1`) is now
terminal: primary checking remains incomplete at 1,510.52 seconds and nonvacuity
passes in 54.93 seconds, for 1,565.47 seconds total. Its final isolated blocker is
`spx_component_result_view_write.pointer_dereference.10`, before the dispatcher
where liveness was assumed. Thus write-only liveness trust does not establish
the complete regional benefit. The checked cut substitution is the selected
implementation; the extra liveness contract stays unregistered. A real-model
expired-matching-owner control for the discarded dispatcher-only substitution
also remains unresolved at 120.16 seconds. Small native/generated-model controls
detect expired owners, but that does not establish quick real-model detection.

The implemented cut policy is `canonical-service-view-cut-v2`, with mandatory
owner equality in Python and JQ. Existing v1 models keep their original checks;
they are not relabeled or resealed. The native accessor stays byte-identical,
so this change avoids invalidating a neighbor merely by adding proof-only
conditional branches to shared native source. All **66 tests in seven targeted
Nix shards pass without skips**, including corrupted-owner, expired-owner,
nullable-view and v1/v2 reader controls. Lint, metadata, format and architecture
checks pass. Audit: `practical-assurance/cut-owner-validation-audit-v1.json`.
The integrated public `public-conditional-cleanup-cut-owner-v1` check finishes
incomplete in **1,123.12 seconds** on the selected real region, at 120 seconds
per query. All 26 safety groups and nonvacuity pass; application assertions
remain unresolved. Compilation takes 0.52 seconds and nonvacuity 56.63 seconds.
The audit verifies 54 raw query/attempt outputs and 29 compiler input files,
the same retained application inputs and helper receipt as the prior public
run, the four unchanged contract selections, five pending boundary requirements
and eleven deferred regions. No pilot rebuild or native link ran. Integrated
GOTO SHA-256: `397ccb4c23f7343772c98fece1586e84816d2f8cbb18d44a09cf6b01fbad1c90`.
Evidence: `practical-assurance/cut-owner-public-audit-v1.json`.

The real caption capture-context assertion also calls reference realization.
A diagnostic reconstructs the exact integrated GOTO, retains that assertion,
and adds a separate check of only its context-identity/runtime-field prefix.
The added predicate passes in **17.99 seconds** (12.30 symbolic execution,
3.21 decision procedure, including 0.09 solver; compilation 0.54 seconds). No
assumption is added. This isolates a passing prefix; it does not prove the
remaining reference translation or the full conjunctive assertion. Repeated
runtime-field comparisons alone are insufficient evidence for a new trusted
frame boundary. Evidence: `practical-assurance/context-prefix-v1/`.

A following diagnostic replaces the indirect call in the proof-only reference
span helper with direct forwarding to the same issued-reference translator.
The callback-target assertion is queried alongside the original complete
caption assertion; all arguments, translator-body checks and return behavior
are retained. It still times out at **120.19 seconds**, after 0.54 seconds
compilation. Symbolic execution completes in 12.07 seconds and SSA conversion
in 3.26 seconds; no terminal solver result is reported. This does not demonstrate
that direct dispatch solves the translation cost, and the substitution is not
integrated or registered as a trusted contract. Source and raw-output audit:
`practical-assurance/context-translation-audit-v1.json`. The next experiment
should isolate issued-origin selection and address/current-lifetime checks,
rather than infer that the already-passing context prefix needs weakening.

These runs establish more completed checks, not a matched full strong-versus-
conditional speedup or interactive edit latency. Some diagnostic probes and
validation tests overlap the runs; cumulative worker times are not wall times.
Preparation, symbolic-execution and solver costs are not separately reported
by the normal-verbosity regional logs. The remaining application assertions,
complete local edit/repair cycle and neighboring-business-proof reuse are open.

Runtime-dispatch integration (2026-09-10): the shared returned-view accessor now
contains an explicitly selected `canonical-view-write-dispatch:1` branch. Its
contract hash selects the checked direct dispatcher during conditional proof
compilation; ordinary compilation retains the native indirect callback. The
target assertion is mandatory in both model generation and evidence validation.
Conditional receipts record the specialization and do not claim execution of
the ordinary production overlay. This is a trusted forwarding contract, not a
strong adapter-equivalence receipt.

All 64 tests in seven targeted Nix shards pass without skips, including five new
dispatch tests; lint, metadata, format-registry and architecture checks pass.
The new tests compare the actual generated accessor's native and conditional
branches for symbolic arguments and failure returns, exercise 384 native cases,
and detect an unexpected callback and an altered forwarded value. Receipt tests
reject deletion of the required guard and reject conditional evidence through
the ordinary reader. These forwarding tests use instrumented callbacks and a
resolver; they do not independently establish allocator or lifetime semantics.
Existing allocation, reference-authority and namespace tests are included in
the targeted validation. Audit: `practical-assurance/dispatch-integration-v1/validation-audit.json`.

The first integrated public caller attempt stops during preparation after
10.46 seconds: `proof overlay differs from the checked bytes`. Changing shared
accessor generation invalidates the retained helper's exact overlay binding,
even though its authored C and selected contracts are unchanged. No solver or
pilot/link build runs. Recheck the supplier with current generated bytes before
retrying the caller; do not reseal its old receipt. This is generator-change
invalidation, not a measurement of local application-edit or neighboring-proof
reuse.

The public helper recheck (`public-conditional-supplier-v4`) completes in
746.87 seconds with a satisfied conditional refinement receipt: 71 fresh
queries, zero reused queries. Its packet inputs, required application assertions
and model bounds match the prior helper exactly. Compilation takes 0.21 seconds;
the mutable-entry check takes 357.20 seconds. The stronger exact-memory and
machine-exit-frame probes still produce counterexamples and are not supplied as
proved facts. The caller retry (`public-conditional-cleanup-dispatch-v2`) uses
this current helper evidence and selects only `cleanup/sync:allocated`, with
120 seconds per query. It terminates incomplete after **1,488.74 seconds**.
Documentation checks briefly overlap the helper recheck, so this is not an
isolated benchmark.
Audit: `practical-assurance/dispatch-integration-v1/supplier-audit.json`.

The terminal caller audit (2026-09-11) retains all application inputs, required
assertions, machine units, bounds and five pending boundary requirements.
Compilation takes 0.57 seconds; assertion, safety, baseline and loop inventories
take 1.43, 1.47, 0.12 and 0.67 seconds. All 454 bounds properties pass in
54.01 seconds and nonvacuity passes in 53.04 seconds. Sixteen safety queries
contain five satisfied results and eleven timeouts. Successive subdivisions
isolate `__CPROVER_spx_checked_runtime_write.pointer_dereference.4`, described
as `dead object in runtime->write`. No complete application-equivalence result
is reached. Eleven other regions remain deferred and no supplier or activation
receipt is emitted. The final query interval is 1,474.98 seconds; the difference
from public wall time includes setup and output handling and is not a separately
measured preparation cost. Some later queries overlap the short reconstruction,
trace and solver trials below, so these are diagnostic timings, not an isolated
matched speedup. Audits: `dispatch-integration-v1/public-audit.json` and
`dispatch-integration-v1/public-phase-audit.json`, under `practical-assurance/`.

Normal-verbosity timeout output ends at `converting SSA`; that does **not** prove
the solver never started. A verbose trial of the isolated dead-object property
with SAT preprocessing disabled reports 12.93 seconds of symbolic execution,
3.23 seconds of SSA conversion and SAT entry, then times out at 120.22 seconds.
Minisat solves a different receiver check (`pointer_dereference.1`) in
39.44 seconds, but its first 1,024-property batch times out at 120.23 seconds.
Changing the generated runtime owners to static storage also leaves the actual
dead-object check unresolved at 120.23 seconds. These trials do not justify a
solver or storage-policy change.

Scoped runtime borrow experiment (2026-09-11): the unregistered revision 2
checks a nonnull receiver against the current source-runtime binding before
assuming that runtime object's liveness during its owning invocation. It changes
only the generated dispatch prefix, retaining authored C, guest memory rules
and all original required assertions. The isolated dead-object property passes
in **15.38 seconds** after **0.50 seconds** compilation. Separately, the owner and
dispatch guards pass in **40.85 seconds**, and the original first **1,024 pointer
properties pass in 19.40 seconds**. These runs use the same prototype GOTO hash
`f4b801ff593bd3dc6ea01a57c676b0ce91f9e901892447da7f8fe54c533fb0c9`.
They do not create admissible conditional evidence: an explicit native/proof
issuer binding rule and remaining regional obligations are still required.
The real-model negative controls are recorded below. Evidence: `public-runtime-borrow-v2/` and
`public-runtime-borrow-check-v2/`, under `practical-assurance/`.

The subsequent real-model negatives complete: substituting an expired foreign
runtime owner violates `diagnostic:borrowed-runtime-owner` in 87.53 seconds;
setting both receiver and binding to null violates it in 87.77 seconds. Each
case retains the original model except for its generated injection and each
reports that exact guard, rather than an unrelated application failure. The
nonnull condition is material: equality alone would admit two null pointers.
These controls do not establish that rebinding both pointers to the same expired
object is safe. Evidence: `practical-assurance/runtime-borrow-negatives-v1/`.

The earlier retained run (`runtime-borrow-region-v1`) uses the existing partitioned
executor with the original public checker policy, bounds and required assertions,
adding only the owner guard to the required inventory. It checks primary safety,
application properties and nonvacuity against the same prototype GOTO. It is
terminal and incomplete as recorded above, and cannot emit an admissible packet. Its first six
1,024-property pointer batches pass, as do all 454 bounds checks (51.64 seconds).
A later 1,024-property pointer batch times out at 120.23 seconds and is
subdivided down to the unresolved accessor lifetime property; regional
performance remains unresolved. Auxiliary frame
probes and the other eleven regions are outside this diagnostic run. No pilot
rebuild or native link is needed.

Independent native controls use the unchanged emitted
`spx_native_run_initialized` and returned-view accessor, with unrelated dispatch
and context layout stubbed. Without assuming owner liveness, 121 CBMC properties
pass in 0.06 seconds for parent/nested/parent calls with distinct runtime owners,
preserved parental aliases and writes in order 11, 22, 33. An expired-owner
control violates its explicit lifetime assertion. Host AddressSanitizer runs
accept the live case and reject an actual accessor use after the issuer returns.
These controls validate the lexical runtime owner, not guest allocations, all
platform behavior, persisted views or asynchronous use. Native issuer SHA-256:
`5b3a48b8f8114b16fb28de618d676b3bbc0dbd87c79244b0133e375b599307b2`.
Evidence: `practical-assurance/native-runtime-borrow-v1/`. Matching a receiver to
an expired or incorrectly rebound owner would not validate this assumption;
lowering must bind the owning scope independently.

Retained write-dispatch experiment (2026-09-10): narrowing the selected property
alone does not bound symbolic-execution cost. The unchanged allocated-region
model times out at **120.25 seconds** for one bounds property, after entering
symbolic execution at 0.76 seconds and without logging completion or solver
entry. Step logging shows a scratch-buffer write exploring an unrelated
`LoadString` callback through CBMC's indirect-call expansion. This does not prove
that the call is feasible. Source cut termination is present on both branches.

The diagnostic prototype checks the actual write pointer against the two
generated runtime write functions before invoking the selected function directly.
It changes two generated adapters and adds the checked dispatcher to the harness;
authored C, original machine-region code, cuts and runtime contracts are unchanged.

| Retained-model query | Result | Query wall time | Symbolic execution | Solver |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Original single bounds property | Timeout | 120.25 s | No completion logged | No entry logged |
| Same property plus checked write target, prototype | Pass | 51.81 s | 13.63 s | 30.95 s |
| All 454 bounds properties plus checked write target, prototype | Pass | 65.12 s | 19.32 s | 37.22 s |
| Wrong callback injected into the real prototype adapter | Dispatch assertion violated | 65.56 s | Separately retained in output | Separately retained in output |

Positive and negative compilation each take **0.57 seconds**; the broader query
reuses the positive GOTO. All runs avoid pilot rebuilds and native linking. SSA
conversion and other query overhead account for the remaining wall time; CBMC's
decision-procedure time includes solving and must not be added to solver time.
The retained audit binds model bytes, checks the generated-source delta, checks
the complete bounds inventory and identifies the exact negative-control property:
`practical-assurance/allocated-dispatch-audit-v1.json`.

These are prototype query measurements, not a complete strong/conditional
comparison or full-region speedup. The adapter substitution needs its own checked
equivalence/composition rule or an explicitly selected conditional runtime
contract before public integration. Its applicability assertion must remain a
required obligation, with exact source/tool binding and all other safety,
progress, nonvacuity and equivalence checks. No supplier or activation receipt is
created. Other pointer targets and broader component networks remain unmeasured.

Constant propagation, depth-first (`--paths lifo`) exploration, earlier rejection
of invalid restoration, and a prototype rejection stub for the unused neighbor
all fail to complete their selected queries within 120 seconds. Constant
propagation is additionally rejected because the installed `goto-instrument`
explicitly warns that the pass is not sound. The combined points-to/constant
propagation query was stopped deliberately on discovering that warning. None of
these trials justifies changing the checker policy. The measured next step is
checked runtime dispatch on the real consumer, with the adapter/proof boundary
qualified before integration.

Focused public checks (2026-09-10): the existing conditional workflow accepts
`--region OPERATION/OBLIGATION` and `--query-timeout SECONDS`. On the retained
Metapad cleanup, selecting `cleanup/sync:allocated` takes **77.09 seconds** at
five seconds per query, compared with **816.83 seconds** for all twelve regions.
Only one region is compiled (**0.50 seconds**); eleven retain explicit deferred
coverage and supply no execution evidence. The selected GOTO and execution
binding hashes are unchanged, and every proof input except selection matches
the full run. This reduces diagnostic work; it does not speed up or complete the
selected proof. Its 42 safety attempts and nonvacuity query still time out.

The operator can now investigate an existing region without repeating every
other region or rebuilding a pilot. Focused checks cannot export supplier
theorems. Exact completed queries can be reused from a focused result; deferred
regions cannot be borrowed as evidence. Seven targeted Nix shards pass (89 tests,
zero skips), with repository checks. See `practical-assurance/focused-check-v1`.
The retained 53.78-second antecedent observation below belongs to `sync:loop`;
an earlier status update mislabeled it as the allocated region. Evidence IDs
were correct and are now joined explicitly in audits. Selecting `sync:allocated`
by ID subsequently gives a passing antecedent in **53.22 seconds** with a
60-second budget on a hash-identical reconstructed model. This diagnostic
observation does not establish safety or equivalence or change the public
incomplete receipt.

Local conditional readiness (2026-09-10): authored pending requirements can now
be investigated through the conditional public path, while structural errors
still stop preparation. Requirements are bound separately from actual local
query results; pending requirements with passing queries remain incomplete and

The real public cleanup diagnostic run (`public-conditional-cleanup-v4`) takes
**816.83 seconds** at five seconds per query and returns incomplete. All 12
nonvacuity queries and 507 safety attempts time out; authored equivalence

Queries overlap; these cumulative durations cannot be added to elapsed time.
The command retains all five binding requirements and emits no supplier theorem
or activation artifacts. This is public diagnostic integration, not a caller proof
or a useful edit-latency result. No pilot rebuild or native link occurs.

Two selected antecedent queries on reconstructed, hash-identical GOTO models
pass in **19.29** and **53.78 seconds** with a 60-second budget. The first entry
bounds group (561 properties) remains incomplete at 60 seconds. Thus the short
budget explains the headline nonvacuity failures but does not establish an easy
full-proof repair. Retain those observations separately from public evidence;
use a focused existing region before another full caller run. Safety splitting
already stops after an exhausted terminal child; repeated subdivision along
that first branch and concurrently started groups still consume substantial
time when even the smallest selected query cannot finish.

The private-frame regression exposed an omitted cost for internal CALL sites
without typed service bindings. Region-local summary budgets now also count
those exact caller sites, using the maximum of typed and exact counts for each
unit. The next-cut path bound remains in force; callee growth and calls in other
regions do not inflate this region's added summary costs. Capacity assertions
remain required, so this undercount produced a counterexample rather than a
false positive. The positive caller passes in 125.52 seconds, and the residual
private-byte observer fails on its returned value in 72.32 seconds, using the
same retained supplier. The complete private-frame Nix shard and the other nine
targeted shards pass (84 tests, zero skips). Runs overlap with other validation;
these are diagnostic elapsed times, not isolated speedup measurements.

It completes in **13.21 seconds**, with **78 reused queries and zero fresh

The full retained cleanup model prepares **12 obligations in 5.21 seconds**

measure caller proof time, neighboring-proof reuse or growth independence.
The public caller command returns in **6.71 seconds**, stopping on existing
binding-readiness blockers before compilation/solving. Removing helper-body
coupling therefore does not by itself remove the public workflow's readiness
barrier. Preserve the unresolved obligations while making their local checking
practical; do not count a blocked command as proof success or editing speedup.

The wider-entry theorem now passes. Two stronger frame candidates still fail;

Conditional query reuse (2026-09-10): the existing prior-proof importer can now

It checks packet bindings, obligation/model correspondence and exact assurance.
Each hit still binds the current compiled model, checker/compiler, solver where

The source include path remains part of compiled-model identity; moving an

The real public Metapad helper resumes in 22.20 seconds, reusing 25 queries and

At a 120-second per-query budget, the same public helper check completes in

(`practical-assurance/public-conditional-complete-v1`). Its model and contracts

The public command detects the typed buffer-argument violation in 118.08 seconds,

This reveals an edit-latency cost outside the ordinary proof: successful cached
core queries do not make unresolved auxiliary checks reusable. Public feedback
now distinguishes the ordinary proof from available or unavailable composition
facts. At that checkpoint, `mutable_entry_contract` was incomplete and the generic

interactive, determine which auxiliary facts the selected shared-state contract
actually needs and account for their checks in total edit latency. Do not weaken

Native comparisons cover arbitrary world records and lookup inputs at allocation

The combined real Metapad reconstruction generates all three runtime summaries

Issued-reference access experiment (2026-09-10): the retained
`issued-access-consumer-v1` replaces native re-realization and origin registration
on access with the existing issued-origin lookup and current-lifetime checks.

Requested permissions, offset arithmetic, one-past policy and lifetime remain
executable checks. All five classes in this retained namespace permit interior
pointers; this condition is checked in the binding audit rather than generalized
to other namespaces.

The compiled inventory omits the old native realization wrapper and world
realization implementation, in addition to the five previously omitted helpers.
Growing the old realization body leaves the replacement source and actual

body, not neighboring application-component proof reuse.

Combined application/domain queries time out at 180 seconds. Splitting the

The complete regional scheduler subsequently passes in **1,614.70 seconds**

The ordinary permission-metadata mutation still times out unrestricted at

The memory observer selects the fresh scratch tail. This diagnoses admitted
model executions, not original-program reachability or an all-input proof.
Each model additionally passes 18,881 language-safety properties in a separate
25.3–25.5-second query. The unsliced unwinding checks pass separately in about
two seconds each. All 340 original harness admission assumptions remain
text-identical after renaming the chosen memory functions back for comparison.
The two-second failure query alone is not the complete safety-checked diagnostic
cost.

The discarded first witness used a sliced counterexample's visible nondeterministic
returns as a complete call sequence. That sequence omitted unused return values
and failed only its own input-exhaustion assertion. It is not evidence of an
application violation. The corrected witnesses assign explicit fields/call sites
and check the intended failure property. Unrestricted negative queries remain
incomplete; these finite witnesses do not replace them.

The independent PE32 Wine test now also checks unavailable permissions, bound
permission/extent metadata, invalid generations, wide offsets and explicit

checks the exact fixture source in the new Nix derivation; the content-addressed
