platform theorem or qualification of the new consumer model.

Fresh-object storage experiment (2026-09-10): the retained
`fresh-object-consumer-v3` adds separate symbolic value and written-mask arrays
for fresh allocations. Unwritten bytes retain their common generation/offset
birth function, or zero for zero-initialized allocations. Stores update current
contents independently on each side. Imported storage, shadows and range effects
retain the original history implementation. Allocation admission, lifetime,
reference transport, application code and cut assertions remain in place. A
line-subsequence audit confirms that all original harness lines are retained;
the seven external inputs and application/cut sources are unchanged.

This uses 64-bit proof-internal backing pointers to represent full 32-bit
extents. Machine words and addresses remain explicitly 32-bit, but the ABI change
has not been separately qualified against native PE32 transport. The new named
contract is experimental and non-authorizing. It is not the complete allocator
summary boundary or a supported public assurance mode.

The small representation fixture checks arbitrary 32-bit sizes, common birth
contents, current stores, aliases, independent neighbors and release. Its 65
properties, including pointer-safety checks, pass in 0.20 seconds. Three changed
fixtures reject erased current stores, shared neighbor storage and ignored
release in 0.04–0.06 seconds. These are representation controls, not detections
of a real application edit. Whole-array-copy prototypes fail in CBMC with
`array too large for flattening`; the retained mask representation avoids those
copies. Forcing array-UF encoding globally also exposes a backend internal error
on the real later-write query. Such failures remain incomplete.

With the baseline's automatic array policy, the same compiled consumer proves
the original `cleanup.assertion.44` in **176.60 seconds**
(`fresh-object-auto-v1`). Preparation takes 0.007 seconds and compilation 0.52
seconds; the policy rerun reuses the compiled model. The separate profile takes
8.77 seconds, reporting 7.62 seconds of symbolic execution, 194,519 steps and
63,870 constraints. The retained body-absent result below takes 161.04 seconds
and has fewer symbolic steps. This experiment therefore establishes no speedup
for that assertion; the query time is not isolated solver CPU time. No pilot
rebuild or native-link run was performed.

The corresponding real later-write control remains incomplete with the
180-second query limit (209.78 seconds observed by the driver, including timeout
handling). Its separate profile takes 54.39 seconds, reporting 45.83 seconds of
symbolic execution, **1,139,073 steps and 299,475 constraints**. Thus a single
ordinary source write still expands the consuming query substantially. Repository
metadata refresh overlapped the negative query/profile; these are observed
latencies, not an isolated CPU benchmark. The consumer has not demonstrated
detection of this edit, a complete regional check, or improved edit-cycle
performance. Keep the retained baseline and revise the semantic contract boundary
before integrating this representation into the public workflow.

Profiling the already compiled baseline later-write model separately gives
**1,073,013 symbolic steps and 282,343 constraints**, with 42.10 seconds reported
symbolic execution (47.90 seconds for the profile command). No compiler or solver
rerun was needed. The large write-path expansion therefore already exists in the
baseline; the new storage representation does not remove it. Syntactic VCC
attribution still shows extensive exact-byte, allocation-generation, overlap,
external-call and reference-realization expressions. This attribution counts
expressions, not CPU time, and does not isolate one causal bottleneck. The next
semantic boundary needs to address the consuming view-access and transport path,
not only how fresh bytes are stored.

Reference-body omission comparison (2026-09-10): the retained checked world
reference summaries are combined with the checked byte-reader projection in
`body-absent-consumer-v2`. Qualification model, source and retained output hashes
are rechecked before use. The same real compound `cleanup.assertion.44` passes
in **161.04 seconds** (`body-absent-consumer-query-v2`), using the existing
180-second limit and unchanged application assertions and input domain.
Preparation takes 0.13 seconds, compilation 0.49 seconds, and the separate
symbolic profile takes 8.30 seconds (7.20 seconds reported symbolic execution,
187,221 steps, 62,209 constraints). The query time includes model loading,
symbolic execution and solving; it is not isolated solver CPU time.

The actual GOTO inventory omits both native conversion bodies, native context
construction, dynamic instance lookup and object-rule base lookup. Growing the
two conversion bodies by 128 defined unsigned assignments each adds 7,644 bytes
to the original harness but leaves the 1,745,515-byte compiled consumer
byte-identical. This establishes independence from these proof-support bodies,
not from the allocator, service implementation or neighboring business code.
The complete existing regional scheduler finishes **incomplete in 1,979.14
seconds** on the same model. It executes 106 queries and reuses the selected
invariant once. Both exhausted assertion batches are discharged by their child
queries. The terminal timeout is `spx_proof_exact_output_read.assertion.1`,
checking exact-output read validity. Its stop leaves 112 of the 305 discovered
assertions unresolved, including 17 of the 93 required assertions. Language
safety and the retained entry nonvacuity check pass. This is neither completed
regional qualification nor interactive performance.

Two preparation errors are retained separately: version 1 compared a raw stdout
hash against the backend's combined stdout/stderr hash; version 2 initially
passed unsorted contract IDs to the evidence constructor. Both failed before a
solver launch. The query driver corrects ID order and consumes the already
compiled, hash-checked version 2 model without rebuilding or repeating profiling.

The actual source-edit control (`body-absent-edit-v1`) inserts
`scratch.extent = text->extent + UINT64_C(1)` immediately before the existing
allocated cut in an owned copy of ordinary `cleanup.c`. The unchanged invariant
rejects this implementation in 56.83 seconds; preparation and compilation take
0.008 and 0.67 seconds. Repairing the source reproduces the baseline model and
reuses the completed query through the current result parser in 0.002 seconds,
with zero solver executions and a 0.62-second selected-query repair cycle. The
regional check runs concurrently; the recorded measurement context makes this actual
elapsed latency, not isolated performance. Changing a contract revision or
semantic digest prevents the repaired query from reusing the old evidence.
These are internal source-edit and cache-dependency controls. They do not
establish public workflow integration or neighboring business-proof reuse.

A complete repaired regional check (`body-absent-region-repair-v1`) reuses
**104 queries** and executes only the unresolved exact-output read-validity
query. It remains incomplete in **181.39 seconds**, including 0.57 seconds of
compilation. This is the measured full regional repair check; 0.62 seconds above
is only the selected-invariant repair. The failed parent batches are bypassed
using their completed child-query evidence, without weakening property coverage.

The later-write control inserts a guarded `spx_view_write_u8` that sets the last
fresh scratch byte to one before the allocated cut. The original baseline
memory-correspondence assertion passes within its regional batch, but the
altered program's `cleanup.assertion.47` times out at 180 seconds
(`body-absent-write-control-v1`). Its compiled property inventory confirms that
the query targets `spx-bisimulation-world-memory:allocated`. A directed negative
observer at the inserted store (`body-absent-write-witness-v1`) also times out.
Partial replay of register, input-allocation and response values from the
feasible extent counterexample (`body-absent-write-replay-v1`) remains incomplete,
as does the same compiled replay model under pinned Z3. Replay leaves other
inputs symbolic. None of these results detects the later-write error or proves
its absence. They are evidence of a practical limit, alongside the successful
extent counterexample and the separate finite native-runtime controls.

Allocation projection and explicit frame experiments (2026-09-10): the retained
`compact-allocation-memory-v2` model selects an allocation record once per byte
read instead of rescanning allocation history for every candidate store. It
retains private writes, visible public writes, shadow initialization, imported
current bytes, fresh initialization and expired-storage behavior. The retained
original and projected readers agree for arbitrary world records in this fixed
layout (`compact-projection-law-v1`, 17.75 seconds). Three altered readers reject
in 1.30–1.53 seconds. Separate concrete history cases also reject in 0.73–0.75
seconds: nonzero imported storage, a pre-birth store at a reused address, and a
store in a released object. This validates the projection in that fixture; it
does not qualify a new general component summary or native platform contract.

The same compound real `cleanup.assertion.44` remains incomplete under each
retained candidate below. Application assertions and source inputs are retained.
Each query has a 180-second limit; smaller symbolic profiles do not establish
practical complete-region performance.

| Candidate | Symbolic steps | Constraints | Query result |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| One allocation selection per byte read | 264,900 | 82,138 | Timeout, 180.17 s |
| Plus allocation-result view decoded from its new instance | 262,995 | 81,517 | Timeout, 180.17 s |
| Plus preserved issued input-object memory projection | 265,877 | 82,120 | Timeout, 180.17 s |
| Allocation-result model with explicit pointwise allocator guarantees | 267,086 | 82,671 | Timeout, 180.17 s |

The last candidate (`allocation-frame-guarantees-v1`) deliberately adds two
named, conditional runtime assumptions at allocation: an observed live old byte
equals its captured pre-call value, and an observed byte of a nonempty zeroed
allocation is zero. The old byte is not assumed zero; the caller still owes its
incoming fact, size arithmetic and output invariant. Later writes remain in the
model. This experiment is different from an equivalence-preserving renderer
optimization, and its conditional evidence cannot enter strong qualification.
Its semantic contract identity excludes the consumer's probe choice; that
instantiation is bound by the compiled model. The earlier input-object prototype
still hashes its whole harness into its experimental contract definition and
therefore does not demonstrate a stable public contract or neighboring reuse.

The existing pinned SMT path also times out on this exact compound property:
180.09 seconds for the unchanged original model and 180.07 seconds for the
input-object conditional model. Both compiled models are reused; Z3 identity,
encoding options and query outputs are bound by the existing evidence mechanism.
An additional interior-offset realization experiment was cancelled after source
inspection contradicted its premise: the input helper resolves the base tuple
and then adds the offset. Its cancelled query is not comparison evidence.

The PE32 integration fixture now also checks later writes through fresh aliases,
preservation of those written bytes after allocation failure, full returned-view
metadata with a distinct generation, all 33 interior byte offsets of the sample
object, and rejection of one-past byte access. New negative executions erase the
later write, substitute an older generation, or alter an offset. The normal run
and seven negative executions use production native runtime bodies and actual
allocation APIs. These finite checks complement the existing native generation
and address-reuse tests; they do not prove arbitrary Windows runtimes or the new
conditional summaries' complete composition.

Runtime-assurance evidence binding (2026-09-10): the existing CBMC query cache and
obligation executor now accept explicit conditional runtime-contract identities.
Contract revisions and semantic digests partition reuse even when compiled C is
unchanged; matching selections retain the normal byte/tool/output checks. The
execution digest and produced auxiliary certificates retain the same assurance.
Both strong readers reject conditional shards and extracted conditional frames.
Tests exercise real checker reuse, changed identities, malformed selections and
strong-reader rejection. This implements identity and authority separation,
not contract lowering, independent validation or public workflow selection.
The real-region comparison used the previously started version 2 model; its
results are separate from these infrastructure tests.

Runtime validation and inverse-reference experiment (2026-09-10): the complete
version 1 cached region finishes **incomplete** in 1,146.59 seconds. Its remaining
singleton timeout is `cleanup.assertion.37`, the view-context check; metadata
passes by exact query reuse, and the extent/method pair passes in 155.17 seconds.
This does not establish a whole-region speedup over the original incomplete run.

The version 2 experiment (`issued-realization-v2`) also reuses inverse address
realization for the exact issued reference tuple, preserving original dispatch
on misses and the class-provenance obligation. No application assertion or cut
premise changes. Its context and extent assertions pass in 89.04/162.91 seconds.
The full existing scheduler finishes incomplete in **2,002.02 seconds** on this
model (`cached-region-v2`), with those two completed queries available for checked
reuse. Version 1 evidence is not reused under the changed version 2 model. The
remaining singleton timeout is `cleanup.assertion.44`: the allocated-cut
invariant combining size bounds, scratch zero initialization and the old text's
terminator. Old-reference memory correspondence passes in 165.63 seconds;
call-order equality passes in 16.47 seconds. Seven incomplete queries consume
1,261.30 seconds in summed query wall time, including failed batches before
subdivision. Query wall times overlap and are not total elapsed time or isolated
solver CPU cost. The result does not support publishing this cache prototype as
an interactive regional proof mode.

The retained comparison (`invariant-decomposition-{baseline,conditional}-v1`)
splits that compound assertion into nested individual checks. Each operand is
evaluated once, in the original short-circuit order, with no new assumptions;
the successful-path predicate is unchanged. Both the original runtime and the
conditional model receive the same transformation. This distinguishes query
structure from additional runtime trust before attempting a broader allocator
contract. It is diagnostic; a split query result does not replace full-region
safety, progress, coverage, composition or native qualification.

Both variants finish incomplete. The two size checks pass in 47.78/53.03 seconds
with the original runtime and 47.51/51.21 seconds with the conditional runtime.
Both scratch-zero and old-text-zero checks exceed 90 seconds in each variant.
Preparation is 0.018/0.013 seconds, compilation 0.524/0.501 seconds and property
discovery 1.099/1.063 seconds (original/conditional). These runs overlap on the
same host, so the small timing differences are not speedup evidence. The split
queries retain unwinding assertions; the previous full scheduler checked
progress separately. Separate profiles show 30 verification conditions for a
memory query, including the unwinding checks. Consequently these timeouts do
not isolate the memory assertion. The version 2 comparison reuses the compiled
models and matches the full scheduler's `--no-unwinding-assertions` query policy;
progress remains a separate regional obligation. It passes the two size checks
in 16.56/51.13 seconds (original) and 16.88/53.78 seconds (conditional). The
conditional scratch-zero assertion passes in 87.67 seconds; the original reaches
90 seconds. Old-text preservation reaches 90 seconds in both. These near-limit
observations support a selected-query benefit but not a stable speed ratio,
complete invariant or interactive regional latency. No full-region equivalence
or evidence reuse across changed compiled models is claimed. Next test the explicit
[allocation-transition boundary revision](independent-portable-lifting-plan.md#measured-runtime-boundary-revision-2026-09-10),
rather than further caching around the same native/history model.

Separate profiles of the matched scratch-zero query contain one verification
condition each: 86,339 constraints and 349,203 symbolic steps with the original
runtime, versus 87,620 constraints and 354,212 steps with the conditional runtime.
Tool-reported symbolic execution takes 12.70/12.05 seconds; complete profile
runs take 14.87/14.02 seconds. This does not measure isolated SAT solver CPU time.
The conditional model still expands slightly more state, consistent with a
shortcut helping a particular solver query rather than removing runtime bodies
or establishing independence from neighboring implementation growth.

`win32-runtime-v2` independently compiles a small PE32 program with production
native allocation/resolve/realize/release bodies, a finite fixture rule table and
real `GlobalAlloc`/`GlobalFree` calls. Under Wine 11.0 it checks successful and
actual maximum-width failed allocation, requested extents, zero initialization,
preserved old contents, interior aliases, expiration and ordered outcomes. Four
separate negative executions reject wrong extent, corrupted old contents,
omitted release registration and an actual reversal of the two release calls.
The executable, source, compiler, Wine and four provider DLLs are hash-bound.
These finite observations validate the proposed laws in this runtime context;
they do not prove all Windows implementations, the original Metapad binary or a
complete ABI/activation contract. In particular, physical allocation size can
exceed the requested extent, as documented by
[Microsoft's GlobalAlloc contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-globalalloc).

The PE32 case is now a repository integration fixture in
`tests/integration/native/test_issued_reference_contract.py`, reusing the native
lifetime fixture generator and the existing compiler/headless-Wine test fixtures.
Its first Nix run hit a Wine launch timeout despite passing standalone runs.
Disabling optional CLR, HTML and menu-builder startup in the isolated test
environment resolves that failure. The integrated PE32 test and all four
existing native lifetime tests now pass through Nix with no skipped tests.
The public conditional-proof mode, explicit contract dependency/invalidation
workflow and neighboring business-component reuse remain outstanding.

Practical-assurance comparison, first retained experiment (2026-09-10):
`build/independent-lifting/practical-assurance/cached-v1` checks the same real
Metapad `cleanup.assertion.34` allocator metadata obligation with an explicit
`metapad-issued-scratch-reference` version 1 contract. A successfully issued,
nonnull scratch reference can be reused at the same address, with compatible
requested extent and permissions, across fresh allocation. Release and world
reset invalidate the cache. The original resolver handles misses. Application
C, adapters, existing assertions and assumptions remain unchanged; the original
resolver body is retained unchanged except for its name.

The selected assertion passes in 61.90 seconds; the retained identical-query
baseline with the original model timed out at 180 seconds. Preparation and
compilation take 0.027/0.463 seconds. The separate profile takes 13.16 seconds
(tool-reported symbolic execution 11.35 seconds). Its 351,081 symbolic steps and
81,206 constraints slightly exceed the original 349,175 steps and 80,923
constraints. This first result supports investigating simpler solver reasoning
about reference reuse; it does not demonstrate reduced model size, an isolated
solver CPU speed ratio or complete interactive latency. Host load was not
controlled and the original timeout is a lower bound, not a completed proof time.

`edit-controls-v1` changes ordinary cleanup C immediately before the allocated
cut to corrupt the text view's extent. The same application assertion rejects
in 63.87 seconds. Repair recompiles to the byte-identical cached model and
reparses its completed query in 0.00147 seconds, executing zero checker queries.
This is a retained local edit/repair check, not public-command integration or
neighboring business-component reuse.

Contract validation remains separate from the application result. A shadow
experiment compares proposed cache hits with the original resolver and checks
all reference fields and occupied origin records. Its combined query remains
incomplete at 120 seconds (`shadow-v2`); the first attempt used an invalid
property-list syntax and executed no solver query. `native-check-v2` compiles
the retained routines with the host compiler and passes 150 concrete comparisons
across nine address/extent layouts, invalid requests, null/end references and
failed/successful release. Independent original-routine comparisons detect three
poisoned contracts: wrong extent, wrong generation and omitted release
invalidation. These are concrete tests of the retained proof/native-reference
routines, not original Win32 binary, allocator-failure or full ABI validation.

All these results are explicitly non-authorizing. The cached result is
conditional on the reference contract; the concrete checks do not establish
that contract universally. The original resolver remains reachable, so no
callee-body independence is established. The full existing regional scheduler
is being run separately in `cached-region-v1`; its terminal result must be
inspected before claiming regional completion. Broader contracts, independent
runtime validation, public trust/dependency/invalidation handling, complete
local edit latency and required qualification exits remain unresolved. No
production assurance mode, pilot rebuild or native link was introduced.
`checkpoint-v1.json` binds the terminal experiment results, unchanged
application/adapter inputs, external inputs and original resolver comparison.

Complete conditional paired allocator frame (2026-09-10):
`allocation-paired-partition-v2` passes the existing full partition scheduler in
730.65 seconds: all 142 authored assertions, including the 94 named frame
assertions, and 4,141 inventory-checked safety/progress properties. The one
180-second authored-batch timeout is retained; its checked children complete.
The unchanged original paired harness has successful allocation/failure outcome
witnesses in 11.37/11.44 seconds (`allocation-paired-witnesses-v1`).

The helper model originally retained unrelated cleanup assertions and lacked the
partitioner's named typed-focus entry. Preparation now explicitly lowers indirect
calls with the pinned GOTO tool, then removes trivially unreachable bodies. All
60 retained bodies preserve their instructions and branch targets across removal,
modulo skip instructions and location numbering; the resolver target assertion
remains checked. A tiny typed-focus wrapper calls the complete helper entry, with
no extra assumptions. This is retained proof preparation, not a production rule.
The failed direct-tool invocation and failed instruction-comparison attempt are
retained separately and executed no solver queries.

Entry compilation, indirect-call/dead-body transformation and wrapper linking
take 0.495/0.291/0.087 seconds. The final GOTO model is 928,346 bytes.
`allocation-paired-partition-reuse-v1` reparses all 59 completed queries in 0.156
seconds with zero checker executions and the same authored/safety inventory.
That is same-model helper evidence reuse, not a local business-code edit,
neighboring-theorem reuse, or complete edit/link latency. This single run is
well above the interactive cold-proof target; it establishes completion and
cheap retained-query consumption, not a general solver speedup.

The theorem starts from identical represented histories. Actual regional
consumption, correspondence for the real source histories, caller/service-target
admission and independent-reader integration remain unresolved. Full-network
acceptance is unchanged. No pilot, native link, production assumption or weaker
assurance mode changed. Exact inputs, body inventories, tool outputs, environment
and timings are bound by `checkpoint-paired-frame-partition-v1.json` under the
retained real-network evidence.

Returned-view frame and real premise discharge (2026-09-10):
`allocation-returned-view-frame-v2` calls the complete retained typed allocator
thunk, including result conversion and origin issuance. Its full local check
passes in 173.63 seconds. The frame preserves every previously occupied origin
and permits at most one new record, while preserving old instance metadata,
the old reference and an arbitrary current byte in its span. The returned view
must have the requested extent and byte width when present. Allocation response
success/failure have witnesses in 4.78/4.71 seconds; changing the actual return
adapter's extent or retiring an old instance rejects at
`allocation-frame:returned-view` / `allocation-frame:resolve-status` in
14.36/7.75 seconds. This is a frame theorem, not full allocator equivalence or
native call-target qualification. The failed v1 preparation omitted the public
view-type header; it ran no proof query.

`real-allocation-frame-premises-v1` adds an assertion at the actual allocation
transition in the unchanged real regional model. Numeric bounds and class
provenance pass in 44.18 seconds. The separate
`real-allocation-frame-reference-premises-v1` verifies that resolving the old
text reference succeeds, preserves every origin field and leaves capacity for
the new reference (119.41 seconds). The observation restores the origin snapshot
and asserts that it was unchanged; no new assumption is introduced.
`real-frame-premise-controls-v1` witnesses both exact and source allocation sites
and rejects a falsely asserted empty-origin premise. These discharge named local
premises, not all obligations of the region or the activation domain.

`allocation-returned-view-paired-frame-v1` additionally executes source replay
against the recorded response, starting from identical represented histories.
The complete combined check exceeds 180 seconds. The same model with the main
engine's `--symex-cache-dereferences` option also exceeds 180 seconds
(`allocation-paired-cache-v1`); no bounds or obligations are dropped. Neither
run reports a counterexample, and neither supplies a completed composition
theorem. Replay/frame composition, source-history relation generalization and
checked consumption in the actual region remain required before adopting a
frame shortcut. No production rule, assumption, native link or pilot changed.

Checked reference-summary and frame experiments (2026-09-10): retained native
metadata conversion summaries now cover context projection and both conversion
directions. The scoped qualifications pass in 2.92 seconds (native realization),
4.16 seconds (world projection plus realization), and 23.65 seconds (world
projection plus resolution). They bind the actual five-rule authority and
three-slot allocation model; they are not universal rules for arbitrary
authorities. Capacity, binding, family, dead-instance, generation, ambiguity,
permissions, requested-span and unknown-selector mutations reject. Origins,
current lifetime and byte correspondence remain separate from pure conversion.

`allocator-bidirectional-summary-v2` consumes the qualified conversions in the
two runtime wrappers and seven direct memory-fact domain observers. Class
checks, their evaluation order, origin issuance and current lifetime checks
remain. The selected real metadata query has 276,505 symbolic steps and 63,561
constraints (12,261,767 textual characters), versus 349,175 and 80,923 in the
baseline. Compilation takes 0.49 seconds; profiling takes 12.45
seconds, including 10.98 seconds symbolic execution. The query still exceeds
180 seconds. This approximately 21% constraint reduction is not a demonstrated
solver speedup or completed region. The earlier v1 preparation failed because
it had not replaced the direct memory-fact observers; it did not run a solver.

`reference-summary-growth-v1` adds 128 defined unsigned assignments to each
original conversion implementation and requalifies the unchanged summaries in
4.16/25.05 seconds. The same consumer transformation produces byte-identical
generated C and a byte-identical 1,745,306-byte GOTO model. Its compiled body
inventory omits both original conversions, context construction and generic
namespace lookup helpers. This establishes independence from these proof-support
bodies only. The timed-out consumer query has no completed receipt to reuse;
no business-component edit or multilevel neighboring-theorem reuse is claimed.

`allocation-issued-reference-frame-v2` instead checks a conditional preservation
law using the original runtime resolver and real allocation-service transition.
It passes in 41.19 seconds: an already issued scratch reference, previous
allocation records and the origin registry survive the allocation interaction.
Both success and failure outcomes have witnesses (2.29 seconds each); retiring
the old instance or changing its extent rejects at
`allocation-frame:resolve-status` in 3.48/3.63 seconds. The separate
`allocation-issued-reference-byte-frame-v1` passes in 76.37 seconds with an
arbitrary byte in the old span and arbitrary bounded public write/shadow
histories. Its premises include at most two existing allocation records, at
most three origins before initial resolution, class provenance and successful
initial resolution. These are local theorem premises, not established caller
admission. Source-side issuance of the newly returned view, other interactions,
regional consumption and independent-reader integration remain unimplemented.

All experiments use retained inputs; no pilot or native link is rebuilt and no
production optimization or weaker proof authority is adopted. The next useful
step is checked consumption of preservation facts across the actual service
and returned-view adapter, with each premise discharged, rather than another
equivalent retry of the large metadata query. The operator's proposed tradeoff
of explicit trusted models versus complete model qualification is under
assessment; testing or assumed summaries still do not authorize strong receipts.

Allocator boundary-cost investigation (2026-09-10): the unchanged real
`allocated:text` metadata query expands to 349,175 symbolic steps and 80,923
constraints (17,368,180 textual characters). Formula generation takes 13.36
seconds, including 11.48 seconds symbolic execution. The recorded first-variable
owner attribution identifies repeated native reference realization/resolution,
allocation-context reconstruction and origin/lifetime checks; it is not an
exclusive call-tree profile or independent solver CPU measurement
(`allocator-text-metadata-profile-v1`).

The proof-only native view observations currently invoke resolvers that can
enroll origins. Diagnostic snapshot/restore controls prevent those observations
from leaving origin mutations while retaining resolver checks. Making metadata
resolution read-only leaves 76,504 constraints; also making the proof-header
reference-span observations read-only leaves 71,201 (about 12% fewer than the
baseline). Both selected metadata queries still exceed 180 seconds
(`allocator-pure-metadata-v1/v2`). These changes are not adopted: their complete
semantic validation and a usable proof-time improvement remain unestablished.

A separate selected check derives exactly one input allocation from the existing
real admission relation in 14.31 seconds. Materializing that established count
before execution leaves 72,634 constraints; specializing the constructor to one
row leaves 80,842. Both still exceed 180 seconds on the metadata query
(`allocator-cardinality-v1/v2`). Another control proves the existing constructor
success assertion in 15.47 seconds, then adds the identical assumption immediately
after that assertion; it leaves 80,936 constraints and also exceeds 180 seconds
(`allocator-constructor-barrier-v1`). No general lower-bound field, constructor
specialization, observation rewrite or new assumption is adopted from these
controls. Some solver runs overlap, so these are bounded completion outcomes,
not controlled serial speedup measurements. All five controls are terminal.

The next structural experiment should qualify a reusable reference/lifetime
invariant or summary, binding its native implementation and actual state-change
dependencies, instead of expanding the same reconstruction in each observation.
Issuance, current liveness and byte correspondence remain separate obligations;
a cached pointer value cannot substitute for any of them. The real service also
still needs checked entry capability admission, caller/cut transport and matching
activation-domain evidence. No nonzero import-slot assumption or runtime fallback
is introduced. These retained-input investigations rebuild no pilot or link and
do not complete any full-network acceptance criterion.

Stable compilation across proof workspaces (2026-09-10): normal contextual
compilation now mounts generated inputs at one private compiler path using the
Nix-provided bubblewrap tool. Concurrent jobs use separate mount namespaces.
Authored source and external include paths retain their spelling, including
observable `__FILE__` strings; generated diagnostics record the virtual-to-retained
workspace mapping. Compiled bytes and exact query binding rules are unchanged
after compilation: there is no model normalizer or relaxed cache key.

`production-compiler-workspaces-v1` compiles the retained real service model in
two fresh directories. The resulting GOTO bytes are identical. Preparation takes
0.024/0.022 seconds and compilation 0.467/0.475 seconds. The selected null-target
query rejects in 11.781 seconds initially; exact reuse reparses the same rejection
in 0.012 seconds with zero new solver executions. No pilot or link rebuild occurs.
This is a real negative-query relocation experiment, not a repaired service or
neighboring-theorem reuse after an implementation edit. Authored source-package
relocation and changes in dependency paths are outside this demonstrated scope.

The complete contextual-receipt fixture also reuses all queries in a fresh
workspace. Concurrent compiler tests preserve authored file strings and reject
a changed included header's incorrect result. Twenty focused tests pass; the
affected integrated Nix gate passes 60 tests across six shards with zero skips
in 129.61 seconds, plus smoke/lint/metadata/architecture/target-SDK checks
(`stable-compiler-workspaces-nix-v1`). Full network, pilot/native-link/export and
portability obligations remain unresolved. This closes the generated-workspace
identity problem identified by the earlier relocation audit below, not the
allocator's expensive memory-transport checks or service-target admission.

Real length-service target rejection and relocation audit (2026-09-10):
`real-length-service-region-v1` is terminal and violated after 2,688.78 seconds.
Its complete safety phase and earlier authored queries, including both span
sites, pass, but call recording rejects at
`spx-bisimulation-call-target-valid:3535087774`. The actual original CALL at
`0x55cb` in the `0x55c8` region has target zero: the admitted relation permits
`EBX = current [image + 0xe158] = 0`. Equality to the current slot does not prove
nonnullness or a code capability. `length-service-target-rejection-audit-v1`
reparses the exact bound failed query in 0.017 seconds with one reused query and
zero executions. The next semantic work must establish checked target admission
and transport from the actual callers/environment, or implement matching invalid
target outcomes. No nonnull premise is silently added. The new regression uses
equal zero targets in both worlds and requires the original recording check to
reject, supporting rather than replacing the real counterexample.
The affected Nix gate passes 7 tests with zero skips in 36.11 seconds,
plus smoke/lint/metadata/architecture/target-SDK checks
(`call-target-rejection-nix-v1`).

The allocator retry `real-sized-region-v3` is also terminal: incomplete after
1,074.19 seconds, with the safety/witness evidence retained and seven authored

seconds: `allocated:text` metadata, extent and context (properties 34, 35 and
37). Its successful first metadata check concerned `edit_window`, not all views.
Neither regional run is currently live; do not restart either solely to repeat
its completed queries or classify the length-service failure as a timeout.

`proof-workspace-relocation-v1` recompiles the real length-service inputs twice
in about 0.59 seconds each. Identical paths reproduce the original GOTO bytes;
copying the same generated C/header bytes to another shard directory changes
the model hash. The relative-staging v2 control removes absolute include paths,
but its two GOTO files still differ by their source-location working directory.
The parsed function and symbol inventories agree when only their source-location
working directories are normalized (`proof-relocation-locations-audit-v2`).
`-fno-working-directory` does not restore byte identity in the pinned compiler.
These are diagnostic comparisons, not an adopted normalizer or a relaxed cache
key. Existing local cache wins use identical model bytes; fresh public-workspace
reuse still needs stable compilation and accurate diagnostic path provenance.
No checker, pilot or link rebuild was used for these relocation experiments.

Partial subdivision reuse and real allocator recovery (2026-09-10): the
scheduler now consumes any bound completed descendants of an assertion/safety

instead of rehashing it at each tree node; actual execution binds the model
again, so a stale scheduling hint cannot authorize changed compiled bytes.

The final implementation passes 18 focused tests, including cached

The final affected Nix gate passes 60 tests with zero skips across 5

The unchanged separated replay-span query now passes with a larger budget in

Separate recording and replay span sites (2026-09-10): the retained real

The terminated-read renderer now emits separate recording and replay assertion
sites with the same current-span predicate. The existing complete-inventory

`cleanup-service-cuts-v6-preparation` regenerates all fourteen real tasks through

Additional retained controls did not solve this bottleneck: array encodings,

The remaining investigation must address the portable replay's repeated
reference/memory reconstruction with checked composition, rather than infer

Paired native input origins and early register literals (2026-09-10):

`paired-origin-real-service-v1` compiles the full real successor's generated

The operation's existing ESI-zero fact was also missing from early admission:

and must not be reported as testing both changes.

The same retained span query including both repairs also times out at 120.12

The normal v5 whole entry passes in 959.04 seconds with 300 authored assertions,

Checked stack-word cut facts (2026-09-10): the existing derived-relation system
now represents current stack/register/image word equalities with non-wrapping
stack address checks. `real-stack-cut-facts-v3` applies the shared parser and
reader lowerings to the real producer's service boundary: the pushed handle
at ESP equals the current caller word at ESP+36, and the high-word argument
at ESP+4 equals EBX. The complete argument-setup region proves these facts in
63.63 seconds (2,878 properties), and the selected `GetFileSize` region consumes
them in 4.47 seconds (5,143 properties). Both same-model witnesses pass. A
compiled boundary assertion claiming the wrong retained handle slot rejects in 4.04 seconds at
`spx-bisimulation-derived:filesize:handle`. All previous regional state/frame
assertions remain enabled; no input memory is copied to make the facts true.

This is a real predecessor/successor fact experiment, not complete network
qualification. The retained service environment remains a premise, and the
full caller/heap/lifetime/composition and public edit/reuse obligations remain.
The added facts cost substantially more than the previous standalone setup
check; do not report the new contract as a performance optimization. Earlier
v2 results used an incorrect copied compiler include path and are rejected as
evidence for the intended Win32 model; v3 fixes the path and retains fresh
compiled/query bindings. Focused positive, alias mutation/reload, address-range
and independent-reader tests pass. The initial integrated gate passes 64 tests,
zero skips, across six shards in 50.10 seconds (`stack-cut-facts-nix-v1`).
The complete regional-engine integration suite adds 30 passing tests with zero
skips in 191.79 seconds (`stack-cut-composition-nix-v1`). Its new composition
case uses a small fixture; it does not qualify the real network. The retained
real-model Nix replay passes positives, witnesses and the wrong-slot assertion
in 78.06 seconds (`24zv22pc0vb45xflgx0akw90fms9rvcl-independent-lifting-real-stack-facts-recheck`).
The raw wrong-slot control changes a compiled assertion, not a separately
serialized operator contract proposal; public contract invalidation remains
unproved. The local audit verifies signed-64-bit address arithmetic, exact
inputs and repaired query recovery without executing a fresh solver query.

The pinned Nix replay recorded in `real-network/checkpoint-real-caller-granularity-v1.json`
passes in 83.89 seconds: four complete
positives, their witnesses and four exact boundary rejections, while checking

The separate first producer region (`producer-first-region-v2`) uses the

All these experiments reuse retained transfer inputs; pilot and link costs

count. Recompiling all twelve generated real regions takes 7.63 seconds and
checking their unchanged required assertion manifests takes 18.89 seconds;
every required goal occurs exactly once (`cleanup-scope-inventory-audit-v1`).
This is an inventory audit, not a proof of those goals. Full scoped-cut fixture
proof and reachability pass; the integrated scope selection passes 58 tests,
zero skips, in 219.96 seconds plus its selected repository/SDK gates.

The value-copy constructor follow-up finishes with both pointer halves passing
in 109.26/109.97 seconds and its bounds group incomplete at 180.73 seconds.
Replacing dynamic metadata indexing with constant-index value selection then
checks the complete generated constructor safety inventory (370 properties) in
147.33 seconds (`cleanup-rule-switch-values-v2`). Its arbitrary metadata
differential passes in 2.64 seconds; a wrong selector is rejected in 1.26 seconds.
The production constructor now has the same tokens as the measured variant.
Its independent indexed-loop regression and native allocation projection tests
pass all twelve direct cases. This closes the selected constructor safety
experiment, not cleanup's complete safety/semantic/coverage inventory. The
normal full v28 proof is running. Five integrated allocation/namespace shards
pass 44 tests with zero skips in 225.26 seconds, plus smoke, lint, metadata,
architecture and target-SDK checks (`authority-value-selection-nix-v1`).
No pilot or link is rebuilt. These are checker wall times under concurrent load;
separate symbolic-processing and solver CPU measurements remain unresolved.

Complete file-prefix result (2026-09-10): `producer-file-local-cache-full-v2`
passes its full inventory in 740.64 seconds and same-model coverage in 21.10
seconds. The independent partition audit verifies 54 authored assertions, 104
completed property/safety/unwinding queries and their retained raw model/tool
bindings (`file-local-cache-inventory-audit-v1.json`). This is a successful
conditional prefix check, unlike the earlier incomplete thirteen-slot attempts;
it still does not qualify the service, full producer or heap. The caller's
original body/assertions and domain are unchanged. This experiment demonstrates
that unrelated auxiliary cache entries can materially affect proof cost, not a
general component-locality theorem or neighboring-proof reuse.

Cleanup v25 has finished its entry region incomplete at pointer bounds for
`rule->locator_kind` in `spx_proof_allocation_authority_context`
(`pointer_dereference.140`). The raw output reports 32-GiB exhaustion during
propositional reduction. Entry query durations sum to 7,552.82 seconds, not a
separate solver CPU measurement. The normal engine retains the region result
and continues later regions. A pointer lookup switch with constant indices
passes an arbitrary-metadata constructor differential in 3.77 seconds and
rejects a wrong selector in 1.48 seconds, but its four real lookup checks time
out at 180 seconds (`cleanup-rule-lookup-v1`). It is not adopted.

Copying the immutable rule and producer records by value passes the same status,
partial-output, identity and lifetime comparison in 3.28 seconds and rejects a
wrong selector in 1.22 seconds (`rule-context-values-equivalence-v1`). Its broad
real constructor query, covering 378 safety properties, still times out at
180.83 seconds during SSA conversion (`cleanup-rule-values-v1`). A retained
follow-up separates all eighteen bounds checks and both halves of the 360 pointer
checks; their union remains the complete constructor inventory. That follow-up
does not recompile, weaken the domain, authorize the lowering or replace the
normal full-region proof. Production code remains unchanged pending useful real
evidence and integrated validation.

Caller-specific cache experiment (2026-09-10): the corrected complete dialog
inventory passes in 677.16 seconds, with 54 authored assertions and 104 completed
property/safety/unwinding partition queries. Fresh coverage takes 20.76 seconds.
`focused-dialog-inventory-audit-v1.json` checks the raw model/tool/output bindings
and independent JQ partition rules. This closes the missing-focus-entry defect
for that conditional prefix; it does not qualify the service, producer or heap.

The remaining file-buffer assertion passes in 57.35 seconds when the optional
stack-cache lookup is bypassed (`private-read-without-cache-v1`). A less broad
experiment uses the existing renderer with nine file-prefix cache offsets,
omitting the four positive offsets used by the other caller; it passes in 63.74
seconds (`private-read-file-cache-v1`). Both retain the complete byte histories
and original entry/service domain. These are selected controls under concurrent
load, not a controlled end-to-end speedup. The full file inventory and coverage
are running under `producer-file-local-cache-full-v2` with that caller-specific
configuration. The first full attempt stopped in 326.69 seconds because the
experiment called the coverage-only builtin from its main property entry; its
unwinding query reported `no body for callee __CPROVER_cover`. The corrected
attempt uses a separate witness entry, as the normal engine does. This setup
failure is retained and is not a semantic memory counterexample. The earlier
thirteen-slot model and its 90/180-second incomplete
queries remain separate. Small source regions also need appropriately local
auxiliary state; carrying optional cache entries for another caller was costly
in this experiment.

Returning initial bytes after a disjoint private-history scan does not solve the
file query: it still times out at ninety seconds, while the dialog control passes
in 15.81 seconds (`private-disjoint-read-v1`). Its isolated equivalence preparation
fails on omitted shared cache declarations before any proof query. That shortcut
is not promoted to the production renderer. The successful caller-specific cache
experiment uses the existing renderer, not this unqualified shortcut.

Successor-domain alignment uses the caller invocation anchor, 36 bytes above
allocation-size block entry ESP. The revised full local block checks 2,555
properties in 7.31 seconds; widened-arithmetic and nonzero-size mutations reject
in 4.35/7.03 seconds. Zero-request coverage takes 0.97 seconds without repeating
compilation after a legacy driver API error. A separate scalar transport lemma
checks the admitted prefix frame/cell projections and rejects both restrictive
old region-relative bounds (`producer-invocation-domain-v1`). Neither a nominal
frame offset nor this scalar lemma establishes full actual-caller admission.
The complete block and repaired coverage are retained under
`producer-allocation-size-v2` and `producer-allocation-size-v2-completion`.
The public caller-admission draft displays these findings in 1.98 seconds via
the existing local `component check`, preserving its blocked status and source
bytes (`public-caller-admission-v26`). No supplier qualification, completed-query
reuse or activation is inferred from this diagnostic refresh.

Pinned SMT checkpoint (2026-09-10): the normal contextual engine can explicitly
select Z3 while preserving the existing CaDiCaL default. The backend uses the
existing single-assertion policy and complete safety/unwinding/coverage rules.
Root and regional receipts bind the absolute solver executable, digest and
version; actual query keys include the external solver's content digest.
Unchanged qualified SMT queries reuse without executing a checker in the complete
fixture test. Different solver bytes invalidate those queries. Timeout cleanup
kills the external solver process group and retains partial output, which cannot
be reused as completed evidence. Integrated checks pass 62 tests with zero skips
in 236.33 seconds, plus smoke, lint, metadata, architecture and target-SDK checks
(`smt-backend-nix-v1`). A follow-up corrects the new test's heavy-tool discovery
spelling; its CBMC scheduling recheck passes both tests with zero skips in 38.66
seconds plus smoke, lint, metadata and architecture (`smt-scheduling-nix-v2`).

On the exact original caller-prefix compiled bytes, selected length-output and
untouched-memory checks now pass in 28.58 and 22.18 seconds with SMT
(`producer-caller-smt-v1`). No preparation, compiler, pilot or link work is

782.77 seconds and dialog 538.02 seconds. These are incomplete-attempt costs,
not successful whole-proof speedups over the 1,649.04/1,612.32-second incomplete
SAT attempts. The file attempt stops at output preservation; its unchanged
singleton still times out at 180 seconds (`producer-caller-smt-remaining-v1`).
Separate mandatory conjuncts localize this to the stack buffer cell: that check
times out at 90 seconds, whereas the image-backed encoding cell passes in 15.19
seconds (`producer-caller-output-split-v1`). Neither split is a production API.
The retained checked-count model proves the mandatory twelve-store equality in
14.33 seconds with SMT, but its output preservation still times out at ninety
seconds (`producer-caller-normalized-smt-v1`). Neither count normalization nor
a longer singleton budget has solved the remaining stack-buffer query.
Both real callers reject the retained wrong output-cell and wrong service-pop
mutations with SMT in 12.19–16.44 seconds (`producer-caller-smt-negatives-v1`).
Eleven positive, negative and incomplete control queries have independently
rechecked raw-output, compiled-model and actual-tool byte bindings under
`smt-caller-controls-verified-v1.json`; no compilation or pilot/link work was
repeated for these negatives. This is local rejection, not the public network's
negative/repair acceptance workflow.

The dialog attempt instead exposes a missing focus-entry function in the

Full cleanup v26 has started through the normal engine and pinned backend. Its
4.35-second preparation preserves all 88 generated C/header inputs from v25;

measured symbolic-conversion and solver CPU totals: raw stage messages and
external-process observations identify where execution reached, not precise
phase durations. Small component definitions still do not establish locality of
the generated memory proof or multilevel neighboring-theorem reuse.

Caller-prefix reachability now passes for both actual callers in 18.87 and
19.11 seconds (`producer-caller-cover-v2`). This reuses the exact v1 coverage

entry and GetFileSize premises.

The full partitioned file-prefix run has now isolated several incomplete memory
assertions, including untouched-memory preservation, the length output, saved

The expensive singleton outputs stop during SSA conversion. A diagnostic that
replaces nine unused runtime callback bodies with mandatory failing entry guards
still exhausts 8 GiB for the file caller and times out for the dialog caller
(`producer-runtime-projection-v1`); it is not promoted as a checked projection.

The cut-count experiment rejects an eleven-store premise for both callers;
the concrete trace includes the additional local-slot initialization at 0x5827.
The corrected twelve-private-store/no-public-store assertion passes for the file
caller in 76.53 seconds. Assigning those exact counts only after the mandatory
check lets its length-output assertion pass in 82.31 seconds, but the untouched

seconds (`producer-caller-normalization-v2`). This is experimental checked count

The existing private-stack/view footprint guards were also instantiated for the

local as well as its memory representation.

The actual caller-prefix locality experiment has only eight original transfers,

Normal partitioning reuses the v3 compiled bytes without regeneration or

Its static inventory has 4,522
properties, including 4,389 pointer dereference checks and proof-runtime helper

Selected output-cell register checks pass for the two actual callers in

Constant-index origin enrollment removes the next measured cleanup bounds

The changed append tail is checked against the indexed version with arbitrary

Small source components alone do not bound proof costs when their harnesses
expand shared infrastructure. This still-expensive real bounds result motivates
the planned checked frame projections and reusable invariants. It does not
establish those general composition rules or parent independence from unrelated
state growth.

Safety query exhaustion now returns an ordinary incomplete receipt instead of

Cleanup's dynamic allocation-authority range append still exhausts 32 GiB during

Final Nix validation passes 44 tests, zero skips, in 171.52 seconds plus the

Retained scan measurements identify symbolic array selection as a concrete

The canonical two-transfer notification consumer prepares without a pilot

V12 groups up to four authored assertions with identical entry, source owner

The real canonical-entry measurement groups four assertions on identical bytes:

The canonical dialog loop exposes a mixed-exit closure limitation: one branch

The stronger current-suffix proposal compiles in 0.24 seconds, but its v2 full
entry query times out at 180 seconds. The corrected v3 harness adds the standard
focus wrappers and compiles in 0.21 seconds. Its complete conditional entry
inventory passes in 486.24 seconds, with 60 authored assertion and 67
language-safety queries. The independent jq safety reader accepts it in 0.11
seconds. Seven witnesses for the stronger premise pass in three queries taking
11.07/14.25/37.20 seconds. Its full iteration inventory also passes, in 1,199.13
seconds, with 60 authored assertion and 67 language-safety queries. The current
independent jq safety reader accepts both entry and iteration inventories.
These are conditional local entry/step checks, not qualification of the whole
ordinary source loop. These are different
models, so their positive query evidence is not interchangeable. Neither model
rebuilds the pilot or performs a native link. Retain `dialog-buffer-loop-v4/` and
`dialog-stack-fact-v2/`/`dialog-stack-fact-v3/` with their explicit conditional
premises and full-query statuses.

Allocation-composition reader checks also reuse the retained qualified helper.
Canonical preparation of all twelve cleanup regions takes 5.56 seconds, including
4.21 seconds of preparation and supplier validation. This experiment executes
no cleanup proof queries and marks every region incomplete. A positive case and
eleven adversarial mutations then check Python, jq and native dependency
extraction in 1.88 seconds using those same inputs. There is no pilot rebuild,
compiler/solver run for cleanup, or native link. See
`framed-allocation-composition-v2/` under the real-network experiment.
The pinned replay validates retained supplier source and auxiliary frame/entry
evidence and repeats the cases in 2.19 seconds. All 88 generated cleanup C/header
inputs match the live canonical run byte for byte. The targeted allocation-link,
lifetime-admission and shared-allocation-frame Nix selection passes 18 tests with
zero skips in 268.95 seconds, alongside smoke, lint, metadata and architecture
checks. These costs remain separate from the incomplete cleanup proof.

Actual caller gates and CALL transport can be checked independently of a pilot
rebuild. Generating the seven real file/dialog transfers takes 1.80 seconds,
and compiling the positive model takes 0.21 seconds. Complete local checks take
9.60/1.73 seconds for the two callers (3,489 properties each). An incorrect
encoding-3 gate rejects in 7.79/1.33 seconds. Ten independently selected coverage
witnesses, including null pointers at the local call boundaries, complete in
1.77/0.77 seconds. These checks stop at the absent cleanup body and leave its
contract, producer provenance and actual incoming heap admission open. The
public draft check displays the bound remaining obligations in 2.27 seconds;
it performs no qualification or link work. Retained inputs and results are in
`actual-caller-calls-v2/` and `public-caller-admission-v24/` under the real-network
experiment.
The pinned Nix replay reuses the exact positive, negative and coverage GOTO
bytes, checks their hashes and repeats all six queries in 22.43 seconds with no
compilation. The selected canonical allocated-region outcome query remains
incomplete at its 180-second timeout; it must not substitute for complete
regional safety, coverage and outcome checks.

Failed checker attempts must remain inspectable without becoming reusable proof
evidence. Query diagnostics now retain timeout/abnormal-exit stdout and stderr,
their digests, exact model/tool/argument bindings and termination information in
separate attempt directories. Incremental timing rows include parser detail.
Repeated failed attempts are preserved separately and never accepted by the
existing exact-query reuse reader. The targeted retention, diagnostics, partial
reuse and safety-selection Nix checks pass 15 tests with zero skips in 48.92
seconds, alongside the smoke, lint, metadata and architecture gates. This fixes
the missing raw diagnostics in earlier canonical bounds-query investigations;
it does not recover bytes discarded by a process using the old implementation.

The retained canonical entry isolates the remaining cost further. Two singleton
dynamic-object bounds checks (`allocation_at` and `allocation_input_admitted`)
complete diagnostic runs in 95.34 and 106.69 seconds at about 1.8 GiB peak RSS.
Symbolic execution accounts for 90.72 and 101.61 seconds, producing roughly
1.41 million steps; their verification conditions simplify without SAT solving.
These plain-text stage probes intentionally cannot qualify a contextual receipt.
They show that a small selected property set can still carry substantial model
processing, not that the complete entry is proved.

Two further identity-comparison prototypes do not justify a production change.
Recognizing immutable identity pointers times out at 120 seconds. Expanding the
five known names into byte comparisons removes all identity-loop unwinds, but
still takes 96.62 seconds (91.57 seconds symbolic execution, 1.44 million steps),
against the 95.34-second baseline. Bounded semantic comparisons and invalid
string controls are retained separately; they do not demonstrate a speedup.
An existing-format proposal to reduce issued-origin storage from 19 to five
slots also remains unqualified: its required capacity assertion exhausts solver
memory after 98.70 seconds at an 8 GiB limit. Neither prototype changes the live
canonical model. Retain `known-reference-identities-v1/`,
`known-reference-identities-v2/`, `known-reference-identity-equivalence-v1/` and
`canonical-origin-budget-v1/` instead of repeating these variants blindly.

A prototype adds explicit prefix bounds before each allocation-range projection
store. Comparing the actual helper implementations over arbitrary allocation
metadata proves equal outcomes and full output context, including partial context
on rejection, in 2.68 seconds. An incorrect guard rejects in 2.03 seconds.
However, the first range-slot query remains about 96 seconds for both versions,
and both last-slot queries time out at 120 seconds while still processing
reference-authority loops. The prototype is not promoted: these measurements
establish no performance benefit. Retain `authority-prefix-equivalence-v1/`,
`authority-projection-bounds-v2/` and `authority-projection-last-v1/` under the
real-network experiment. Both real timeout attempts now preserve their partial
stdout/stderr in diagnostic-only evidence, exercising the retention path outside
mocked tests. The ongoing canonical run continues without a restart or model
change.

The complete cleanup now reaches canonical semantic preparation using the
existing slice, normalization and overlay interfaces. Reusing retained linked
inputs takes 4.48 seconds for its 35-definition slice and 4.87 seconds for
normalization and selected transfer loading. No pilot rebuild is needed.
After resolving actual service results and helper entry dependencies, preparing
the canonical full-engine inputs takes 4.17 seconds, separately from its model
construction and proof queries. This run uses ordinary engine entry-state
construction; the earlier regional harness timings cannot stand in for it.
The updated interface/source proposal passes the public source check in 4.81
seconds; that result does not qualify the contextual proof or activation.

The canonical entry exposes avoidable regional over-allocation: summary effects
were multiplied by every call in the component, including external calls and
calls after a barrier. Deriving added summary costs from component CALLs on the
region's longest path reduces entry public/private write slots from 121/99 to
13/9 and call slots from eleven to two. Eight call-free regions drop from
111/91/10 to 3/1/1. Exact code, authored source wrappers, cut headers and supplier
inputs compare byte-identically across all twelve generated regions. Capacity
assertions remain checked; insufficient budgets cannot silently restrict source
behavior. The old run's four initial bounds groups each exceeded 600 seconds;
its inputs and completed outputs are retained before stopping it for this model
change. Full v23 checks now measure the reduced models. Do not infer full solver
tractability or neighboring theorem reuse from the smaller arrays; common
connected transcript arrays are still component-wide.
The affected region-cost regression selection passes 54 tests without skips in
47.58 seconds, alongside smoke, lint, metadata and architecture gates. This is
validation cost, separate from the still-running real proof queries.
The allocation-result/lifetime/service selection passes 27 tests with zero
skips in 1,637.26 seconds. Its full paired lifetime regressions account for a
substantial validation run despite using small retained fixtures and no pilot
rebuild; keep this cost separate from source preparation and regional solving.

The actual resource/message consumer now compiles from retained transfers and
the qualified body-free helper in 4.211/0.067/0.254 seconds for preparation,
model generation and compilation. Both helper bodies remain absent. The
one-transfer aggregate check exceeds 300 seconds; the two-transfer aggregate
bounds query exhausts 16 GiB during propositional reduction. Reducing origin
capacity from thirteen to an asserted three does not eliminate that exhaustion.
The current three-origin model has 158 functions and 30,809 instructions.

Before repeating full consumer proofs, check the actual admitted path. A
retained twelve-write private budget made that path unreachable: five caller
stores plus one connected return-address store plus seven checked helper
private stores require thirteen events. Deriving the capacity through the
existing helper rule restores positive coverage in 62.81 seconds, with no
input-domain weakening. The earlier coverage timeout and unreachable result
remain retained. The corrected full v4 proof is separate from this witness.

A local C edit replaces the helper's repeated service capacity constant with
its declared buffer extent. Public local qualification takes 5.57 seconds
(two new queries, no query hits). Current validation and comparison take 2.87
seconds: the checked shared contract and summary C are unchanged, and replacing
that rendering leaves the caller C identical. The current checker rejects the
old paired supplier proof for the edited source. Full supplier requalification
passes in 1,552.33 seconds with 171 executed contextual queries and zero hits,
plus two local source queries. Current readers take 3.77 seconds without new
solver queries. The qualified supplier regenerates the same sixteen caller
C/header files. Using stable input paths then yields byte-identical compiled
caller evidence; changing debug paths changes the raw digest. The comparison
takes 8.25 seconds, including 0.64 seconds of compilation. Full neighboring
theorem reuse still requires caller qualification and more consuming levels.
An initial pure-Nix input registration error fails before proof work and is
archived. No pilot rebuild is involved.

The sixteen-GiB message run also exhausts an individual safety query. Raising
only the process allowance to 32 GiB makes that exact query pass in 188.04
seconds at 24,268,728 KiB peak RSS. Stop the inadequate run with its completed
evidence retained; do not repeatedly subdivide an exhausted singleton. The
unchanged full inventory continues with 32 GiB per process and exact reuse of
completed experiment processes. Its frozen observation is non-authorizing and
does not replace a contextual receipt or establish neighboring theorem reuse.

That run ends incomplete after 1,760.03 seconds, with 62 executed processes and
35 exact process hits. Its 87 final safety queries all pass, including unwinding;
four authored assertions pass. The next prefix-correspondence assertion exceeds
600 seconds. The next investigation is that specific comparison; a whole-run
restart or another memory increase is not justified by this timeout alone.
Two retained optimization probes also exceed 600 seconds on that comparison:
a checked latest-visible-zero shortcut (600.31 seconds) and narrowing the
already bounded 0..499 choice to sixteen bits (600.29 seconds). Neither is
promoted to the production renderer. The shortcut's small equivalence checks
and deliberate missing-guard mutants establish its local rule, not a useful
performance improvement on the real consumer.

Hand-defined notification split/move/merge proposals on the full cleanup source
pass public source checks in 4.24/3.92/3.73 seconds. Their original machine-region
union and unrelated region sets are unchanged; merging restores the original
source and intent. Semantic transport, coverage and progress remain proof work.

The actual notification decision uses the full ordinary cleanup source and the
existing cut-header lowering. Preparation/model/compiler costs are
4.69/0.013/0.314 seconds. Positive coverage of both successors and a retained
released allocation takes 83.64 seconds. The first harness omitted the existing
positive-extent entry invariant; retain its counterexample and repair the
harness before interpreting a transport failure. A wrong successor stack slot
then rejects at the intended capture assertion in 151.16 seconds with no
property filter. Repair reconstructs the exact original model after all
seventeen C/header inputs are compared. The positive aggregate query times out
at 600.19 seconds (4,880,312 KiB peak child RSS). Its partitioned run is stopped
after finding that zero-initialized ESI was an undeclared cut premise. The
corrected v20 cut explicitly declares ESI zero and havocs unrelated registers;
preparation/model/compiler costs are 4.25/0.011/0.257 seconds, coverage is 78.74
seconds, and the complete inventory has 9,386 properties. The actual predecessor
must establish the register fact across copy/release calls. This correction
motivates using canonical engine entry construction for the complete cleanup.
The conditional v20 check completes in 1,896.41 seconds: 179 authored assertion
queries and 111 safety queries pass, including unwinding. Its current independent
JQ safety-coverage check takes 0.159 seconds. This is a complete regional check
under its explicit premises. Actual caller admission and complete network
recovery remain pending.

Safety queries now retry resource exhaustion or timeout by splitting the
selected property IDs into ordered halves. The model, paired entry, assumptions,
instrumentation and per-query limit stay unchanged. Each failed group has a
finite refinement tree; an inconclusive singleton remains incomplete. Definite
counterexamples and unwinding queries are not retried this way. Both readers
require exact coverage of every original group, rejecting missing, duplicated,
reordered or foreign properties. Completed outputs keep their existing exact
query evidence bindings; failed parent timings remain visible. This changes
query scheduling and receipt validation, not the semantic input domain.

Four focused tests pass in 15.976 seconds. The integrated safety-selection and
query-evidence tests pass (ten tests, no skips) with repository gates in 47.43
seconds. The current readers accept the retained helper provider in 4.09 seconds
with zero new solver queries. The adaptive actual-message run remains live;
its completion and any child-query failures must be inspected before claiming
caller success. No pilot or native-link rebuild runs. See the retained
`checkpoint-message-admission-and-edit.json` and `adaptive-safety-nix-v1` records.

The body-free current-memory rule adds one public write per invocation; the
parent budget does not consume the callee's local event capacity. For the real
500-byte helper interface, increasing that local capacity from two to 64
leaves the summary C byte-identical. Retained model inspection reports 125
GOTO functions and 5,850 instructions with both supplier bodies absent;
model/compiler/inventory costs are 0.006/0.168/0.392 seconds. This inspection
does not run a solver or qualify an actual caller.

The new complete paired consumer test first exceeds its 180-second limit.
Searching recent zero stores first and stopping at a current valid witness
avoids computing an unneeded minimum prefix. The four-test rerun passes in
176.313 seconds with the same limit, including overwrite and retirement cases.
Twelve local/exit-reader tests pass in 30.534 seconds. The integrated run passes
30 tests with no skips and repository gates in 345.83 seconds. The initial
timeout remains a performance finding, not a proved counterexample.

The real helper's new public provider build uses retained inputs and the
new current-memory source contract. The earlier standalone paired result is
not a contextual provider receipt usable by `previousQueryEvidence`; this
build establishes that artifact through the existing provider path. It completes
in 1,311.93 seconds with a complete source-provider qualification and the checked
current-memory consequence. Its SDK predates the lookup optimization, but the
current supplier/postcondition and independent JQ readers accept the same
retained artifacts after adapter/local-model regeneration in 3.00 seconds.
No solver queries or SDK refresh build are required. This is existing evidence
reuse through current validation, not neighboring proof reuse after a C edit.
No pilot or native-link rebuild was requested. Model inspection and test
evidence are under `service-buffer/current-memory-summary-models-v1` and
`shared-current-memory-summary-nix-v1` in the retained real-network directory.

The explicit local `view_has_zero` postcondition searches bounded store events
and rechecks current bytes, with no buffer-length enumeration. The unchanged
real helper's public source/local-contract check passes in 4.58 seconds using
its real 500-byte view. Preparation/compiler/model/solver phases take
0.003/0.217/0.258/0.866 seconds; evidence validation takes 0.083 seconds.
Other public-command costs are not separately measured. Two queries execute and none are
reused. Both current evidence readers accept the retained certificate.
Four focused tests pass in 7.118 seconds, nine related tests in 12.049 seconds,
and the integrated run passes 22 tests with zero skips plus repository gates
in 54.87 seconds. No pilot or link rebuild runs. This proves a local fact;
the existing body-free summary and provider exit-guard derivation continue to
reject its export. Inputs and costs are in
`service-buffer/public-current-memory-helper-v10` and
`shared-current-memory-nix-v1` under the retained real-network directory.

An actual helper alias-overwrite edit rejects through the public local command
in 6.87 seconds. Repair recovers in 0.93 seconds and resolves to the exact same
Nix product as the initial successful check. The cached certificate still
describes its original two executed queries and zero internal query-cache hits;
those counters do not describe new solver work during the cached repair. This
is verified Nix product reuse, not a neighboring-proof reuse demonstration.
Both local proof failures identify the current-zero assertion; public blocker
locations remain null and still need the full workflow's location diagnostics.

The corrected helper paired v2 finishes in 1,517.97 seconds after 4.477 seconds
preparation; compilation takes 0.272 seconds. Its normal paired check passes.
The selected private-stack and declared-clobber auxiliary rules pass, while
stronger image-only-write and exact-register-preservation probes reject. The
per-query timing record retains both results; concurrent query durations are
sums of elapsed query times, not disjoint wall-time phases. Model generation,
symbolic processing and solver internals are not separately measured by this
older driver. Do not rerun this expensive paired check merely to observe its
status or eliminate stronger optional probes. Full provider binding and the
body-free current-memory composition rule remain required.

Local shared output contracts use one range event plus one zero-byte event,
independent of buffer length. The real helper keeps its 500-byte view and its
ordinary C unchanged. Its public source/local-contract check passes in 4.88
seconds, executing two queries and reusing none. Measured preparation/compiler/
model/solver/evidence phases take 0.024/0.230/0.267/0.656/0.080 seconds;
the remaining wall time is outside those phase measurements. Source opacity, frame and
input-dependence are satisfied; both current readers accept the retained
certificate. This remains local evidence, not summary or provider qualification.

Three focused tests pass in 8.386 seconds and seven related tests in 9.198
seconds. The integrated run passes 29 tests with zero skips in 174.95 seconds.
After the independent-reader extension, its three tests and repository gates
are rechecked in 30.40 seconds; these are not three additional unique tests.
No pilot rebuild or native link was needed. Body-free final-memory export of
the termination fact is still missing. Evidence is under
`service-buffer/public-output-helper-v9`, `shared-output-termination-nix-v1`
and `shared-output-readers-nix-v2` in the retained real-network directory.

The explicit output-termination relation adds one sparse zero-byte event per
selected service call, alongside its arbitrary buffer-range effect. It adds no
string body or buffer-sized loop. Six new tests pass in 22.864 seconds, and
twenty related tests pass in 61.737 seconds. Regenerating the actual retained
copy world without selecting the relation produces byte-identical C. This
does not rebind old proof receipts to the changed implementation closure.
The first seven-shard integrated check stops after 341.22 seconds because the
existing mutable-composition setup exceeds a 30-second query limit; two shards
are cancelled. The exact failed/cancelled derivations pass on retry in 274.96
seconds without changing limits. All seven reports pass (48 tests, zero skips),
with the original timeout retained. Smoke, lint, metadata and architecture
gates pass; current-tree metadata/lint are also checked after the refresh.

The real resource helper's additional output-consequence model compiles in
0.515 seconds and exceeds its 180-second combined-query limit. Its complete
paired operation check passes in 851.78 seconds after 4.980 seconds of
preparation (72 language-safety queries, 80 authored assertions and
nonvacuity). Its auxiliary physical write-frame probe rejects in 9.25 seconds:
the older driver omitted the v8 public intent's existing private stack grants.
A corrected v2 run uses the current binding and intent; its check is running.
Paired success does not establish summary framing. Shared-summary post-memory composition
and external-provider qualification remain required; a fast fixture pass is
not a substitute. The earlier copy/release full partitioner is now incomplete
after 2,045.60 seconds, and third-byte v2 is incomplete after 2,393.40 seconds;
each has a terminal 600-second query timeout. Inputs, results and process
observations are in `checkpoint-output-termination.json` under the retained
real-network directory. No pilot rebuild or link ran for these steps.

Extending the real copy step through conditional copy/release requires three
actual transfers. Preparation/compiler take 6.238/0.453 seconds. The combined
query exceeds 300 seconds; the production complete-inventory partitioner is
running on the same compiled model. All three null/no-copy/copy paths have
reachability witnesses in 104.56 seconds combined. A selected conjunction of
the two successor-state assertions exceeds 180 seconds; response erasure is
still unproved pending the complete partitioned check. This remains conditional service transport, with no
pilot rebuild or native link. The resource-text/message dependency audit also
records that the current supplier contract lacks the consumer's required
termination fact, avoiding a premature full-tail build under that assumption.

The complete retained first-byte region (`cleanup-lookahead-first-v5`) passes in
4,559.39 seconds: 105 language-safety queries and 192 authored assertions.
That is a conditional regional result on the exact retained model, not a new
provider receipt after the service-effect implementation changed. The cost is
still substantial despite the one-transfer region and remains part of the
real-network performance baseline.
