The bounded-NUL footprint extension keeps one sparse event per writable range
and resolves memory-dependent extents before writes. Six focused tests pass in
41.78 seconds; seventeen existing buffer tests pass in 54.95 seconds. Six
integrated Nix shards pass 42 tests with zero skips, plus smoke, lint, metadata
and architecture checks, in 366.79 seconds. No pilot rebuild or native link
was needed to establish this step.

The real copy-boundary proposal (`public-cleanup-copy-v16`) takes 4.890 seconds
to prepare and 0.003 seconds to generate its world model. Native/proof adapter
compilation takes 0.035/0.080 seconds; public source checking takes 5.02 seconds.
The complete cleanup C is unchanged. Its copy return is now discarded in the
proposed service signature, with downstream machine irrelevance still to prove.
The actual-transfer paired query first hit the default eight-bit CBMC object
limit. The same model at the production twelve-bit setting rejects an incoming
origin in 260.11 seconds; the complete focused trace takes 12.42 seconds. The
harness omitted the existing public cut's positive-extent facts, admitting
zero-sized text/scratch and testing `base - 1`. The corrected `real-copy-call-v3`
model restores those facts and the typed-call focus entry; its full query passes
all 5,791 reported properties in 270.52 seconds, after 1.808/0.203 seconds of
preparation/compiler work. Its reachability witness passes in 24.79 seconds.
It retains conditional allocation,
termination and capacity premises. This is not neighboring-proof reuse or
network qualification. Results are in `terminated-call-ranges-nix-v1` and the
copy proposal's `compiled-adapters-v2` and `real-copy-call-v1` subdirectories.

Allocated service buffers now use existing checked origin/lifetime state and
one sparse event per writable footprint. Seventeen focused buffer tests pass
in 66.58 seconds. Integrated validation records a 30-second mutable-composition
setup timeout after 199.74 seconds overall; a one-job recheck passes 95 tests
with zero skips and supporting gates in 1,112.64 seconds, keeping the same
limits. The five new heap tests pass again in the explicitly classified CBMC Nix shard
with zero skips; lint, metadata, smoke and architecture checks also pass, in
75.94 seconds overall. The image-only generated range check retains its previous form.
Regenerating the real call-free first-byte region takes 4.459/0.012/0.298 seconds
for preparation/model/compiler; all 12 C/header files match the retained region
byte for byte. Old query/provider evidence is not rebound by this comparison.

The original-code copy-admission base case passes in 25.55 seconds and the real
thirteen-transfer scan/tail induction in 34.88 seconds. Null predecessor, removal
backedge and no-copy tail witnesses pass in 22.06/27.61/23.22 seconds. These are
conditional original-only implications, not paired component qualification or
checked string-copy semantics. No pilot/link work ran. See the corresponding
`cleanup-copy-admission-*`, `cleanup-no-range-regeneration-v1` and
`heap-call-ranges-nix-v2`/`v3`/`v4` directories and
`checkpoint-heap-service-composition.json` under the retained real network.

The nullable scratch counterexample rejects the real advance fault-outcome
relation in 53.16 seconds. Its complete run was stopped after that verified
failure. The current-engine actual predecessor proves the missing zero-output
implication in 8.49 seconds, with a null-case witness in 15.41 seconds. The
complete nine-transfer original scan proves inductive preservation in 3.89
seconds; null backedge, tail and fault witnesses also pass. These conditional
original-only implications do not discharge actual callers or authorize a public
contract refinement.

The public proposal expresses the added fact using existing invariants, leaves
the preceding proposal's C byte-identical, and passes its source check in 1.01
seconds. The refined advance model prepares/models/compiles in
4.346/0.020/0.215 seconds. Its selected fault-outcome recheck exceeds 180 seconds;
the complete regional run ends incomplete in 1,802.26 seconds at the
`spx-bisimulation-view-reference-address` query's 600-second limit.
First and second tail models prepare/model/compile in 4.479/0.014/0.294 and
4.519/0.013/0.295 seconds. Constant second-iteration state transport passes in
21.28 seconds; the complete conditional first-tail check passes in 2,796.71
seconds, with 105 safety queries and all 141 authored assertions satisfied.
The selected second-tail invariant query exceeds 180 seconds and remains
recorded as incomplete; its complete regional check passes in 2,378.47 seconds
with 106 language-safety queries and 136 authored assertions. Its paired branch
witnesses both pass. The first tail's
zero-byte and copied-byte paired witnesses pass in 86.85/90.17 seconds. The tail
partition is exactly 2 + 2 + 10 transfers, preserving production C after removing
the two proof markers. Regenerated lookahead models use the refined contract and
standard proof-entry wrappers; the first-byte check is running, with the second
and third prepared. Their costs and inputs are retained in the checkpoint.
No pilot or link work ran. See `real-network/checkpoint-null-output-refinement.json`.

The first-byte complete run terminates incomplete in 1,604.56 seconds at the
outgoing tail `text` context query's 240-second limit. Its continuation uses the
identical compiled model, tool and command bindings, reuses 144 completed process
records, and allows 600 seconds per new query. The previously incomplete query
passes in 368.32 seconds; the continuation later ends incomplete in 3,251.22
seconds because the retained model lacks `main_focus_typed_call_0`. New tail and
refined advance models include the existing standard wrappers. The second-byte
run reaches the analogous timeout in 1,629.76 seconds and continues with
the same process-reuse policy and 600-second limit. Inspection then confirms
that this model also lacks the entry required by two typed-call assertions; the
continuation was stopped with its completed queries retained. This establishes
exact process reuse on an unchanged model, not neighboring-theorem independence
after a C or contract edit. The 188-assertion combined diagnostic still exceeds
240 seconds, so no batching policy was adopted.

The two later real regions prepare/model/compile in 4.978/0.013/0.441 seconds
and 4.483/0.022/0.271 seconds. Together with the first two regions they partition
the prior nine-transfer scan exactly; erasing new proof markers preserves the
previous C source byte for byte. Third-byte exit witnesses take 114.24/135.99
seconds. Copy/remove loop-return witnesses take 81.51/104.26 seconds, and its
paired null-scratch copy fault takes 86.73 seconds. These remain conditional
reachability results. The third-byte universal check ends incomplete in 2,380.70
seconds at a 600-second outgoing tail-context timeout. The second-byte
continuation was stopped as described above. The original copy/remove run was stopped after
the counterexample recorded above.

A focused real pending-byte relation passes in 40.87 seconds, rejects a local
`b ^= 1U` edit at the advance cut in 51.38 seconds, and passes after restoring and
recompiling the C in 32.46 seconds. This is selected-assertion evidence, not the
full public edit/refine/reuse/recovery workflow. No pilot or link work ran and no
semantic-engine implementation drift was found. See
`real-network/checkpoint-scan-regions.json`; earlier checkpoints follow.

The first-byte model now clears its complete language-safety phase. Command
policy v11 groups a non-unwinding class by function when it exceeds 1,024
properties; small classes and unwinding remain whole. Python and JQ independently
check the canonical groups and complete inventory. Grouping alone left one
396-property borrowed-origin realizer query incomplete at 240 seconds in the
430.04-second aggregate run. Smaller diagnostic selections located its stall in
SSA conversion around selected-origin pointer dereferences.

Copying that selected record by value preserves status and output behavior in
a direct arbitrary-registry/current-lifetime comparison (four origin rows, two
allocation rows, 17.50 seconds). Current-world lifetime checks still execute.
The corresponding real-model safety group passes in 10.60 seconds; all 100
safety queries pass, ending 277.04 seconds after the run began. Their summed
elapsed time is 1,029.52 seconds, not a separate CPU or solver measurement.
The original full C is unchanged. The model has 188 bodies and 7,831 instructions
(previously 7,816); preparation/model/compiler costs are 4.262/0.011/0.209 seconds.
Both exits have witnesses in 138.37/139.28 seconds. Full authored assertion
checking initially ends incomplete as recorded above, so no complete-proof
speedup or target qualification is established. A same-model diagnostic selecting all 188 authored assertions
together exceeds 240 seconds (240.205 seconds overall); no batching policy is
adopted from that result. Earlier-model query outputs are not rebound to this model.

The next second-byte region prepares and compiles in 4.74 seconds from retained
inputs and the preceding cut's declared premises. Both paired exits have
feasibility witnesses; its universal proof is now running and actual caller
admission remains open. No pilot or link
work ran. Targeted Nix validation passes 180 tests and supporting gates in
227.75 seconds with two concurrent build jobs, following a retained 20-second
negative-test timeout during the more concurrent attempt. See
`real-network/checkpoint-origin-values.json`. Earlier checkpoints follow.

Outgoing native cut checks now share the physical address established by their
fresh context/reference relation. They retain full-origin memory comparison and
conditional index/overflow checks. This reuse ends at the barrier: incoming
restoration may mutate aliased descriptors, and later barriers recheck current
lifetime and metadata. Generated C and its model identity change; previous
query outputs are not rebound to the new model.

The real full-source first-byte model has 188 bodies and 7,816 instructions;
preparation/model/compiler costs are 4.244/0.011/0.204s. Both paired exits now
have witnesses: nonzero lookahead in 118.192s, and NUL tail in 138.742s with a
180s limit after the initial 120s timeout. This is conditional reachability,
not complete equivalence or a same-budget comparison for the latter branch.
The full property run remains incomplete in 241.708s at its 8,636-property
pointer partition. Four other safety partitions pass.

Splitting that exact pointer inventory diagnostically reveals that all 2,470
properties owned by `cleanup` pass in 8.310s, while the 6,166 helper properties
still exceed 180s. The two groups are disjoint and cover the original inventory.
The public policy is unchanged; adopting finer grouping requires producer and
independent-reader checks of complete coverage and exact query binding. Targeted
Nix validation passes 141 tests with zero skips and supporting gates in 99.496s.
All jobs are terminal. No pilot or link work ran. See
`real-network/checkpoint-checked-cut-addresses.json`; earlier records follow.

The first-byte region now uses the full ordinary cleanup C with hand-defined
lookahead markers and pending-byte transport. Public source checking passes in
4.215s. Specializing incoming/outgoing proof-header phases reduces its header
from 122,644 to 72,286 bytes and its compiled model from 9,002 to 7,852
instructions, without changing source C or conditional inputs. Preparation,
model generation and compilation take 4.191/0.010/0.226s. The changed generated
header changes model identity; previous completed queries cannot be rebound to
it. Targeted Nix validation passes 117 tests, zero skips, and supporting gates
in 115.456s.

The complete regional check still ends incomplete in 241.989s: its 9,056-property
pointer partition exceeds 240s, although four other safety partitions pass.
Both paired exit witnesses exceed 120s. A separate diagnostic on the same
coverage model starts symbolic execution at 0.662s and remains there at its
90s limit, with no propositional-conversion or solver event. Repeated namespace
resolution/realization dominates the logged loop activity; message intervals
are not a function profiler. This establishes a symbolic-processing bottleneck
for that run, rather than a measured SAT-solving cost. No speedup in complete
proof time is established by the smaller header. Exact snapshots and subsequent
bounded resolver experiments are in `real-network/checkpoint-lookahead.json`.
No pilot or link work ran. The following paragraphs retain earlier checkpoints.

The tighter-range experiment leaves the GOTO model unchanged and changes only
the nested namespace-loop limits from six to four, retaining unwinding checks.
Its full run ends incomplete in 241.868s; both witnesses still exceed 120s.
The selected-rule value-copy experiment compiles in 0.278s and preserves
resolution/realization statuses and outputs in a separate 755-property check
(25.454s). Its full run nevertheless ends incomplete in 242.118s and both
witnesses exceed 120s. The comparison covers the retained five-rule,
three-range namespace, not arbitrary target qualification. Both changes remain
experimental; the public checker and native resolver are unchanged.

The full ordinary-C scan now has a hand-defined tail cut at `0x560d`; source
checking takes 3.845s and marker erasure preserves production C exactly. This
makes the nine-transfer scan separately reviewable without assuming three
nonzero lookahead bytes or imposing a small buffer limit. The repaired driver
uses four origin slots with overflow checks, includes the production unexpected
cut helper and has no undefined `spx_*` functions. Its compiled model has 171
bodies, 9,266 instructions and 885,930 bytes; preparation/model/compiler takes
4.121/0.045/0.197s. These are conditional experiment inputs, not activation.

The earlier combined run exhausts 16 GiB in 146.793s; the repaired partitioned
run passes four safety partitions but its 9,956-property pointer partition times
out at 240s (242.038s overall). Of those pointer properties, 3,556 are in generated
`cleanup`; 87 functions participate. Authored semantic queries do not run after
the failed safety gate. All six paired branch witnesses time out independently
at 120s. An alternative array encoding also times out at 240s and is not adopted
into the public checker. No branch witness or complete scan proof is claimed.

This exposes a local model/query cost, independent of pilot rebuilds. Refine the
actual lookahead cuts and transport pending values before repeating broad solver
tuning. All recorded jobs are terminal; exact models, failed queries, capacities,
body inventories and remaining exits are in `real-network/checkpoint-scan-tail.json`.
No pilot or link work ran. The following paragraphs retain earlier checkpoints.

Public filled-suffix cut transport now uses the range constructor established by
the earlier experiment. Constant/register coordinates are available before any
input-byte observation; byte count does not expand initialization. The policy,
facts and required construction/successor assertions enter proof identity and
both receipt readers. Fact-free operations retain the absent overlay/policy.
This is a supported internal-cut rule, not caller admission or an implicit
body-free summary fact.

The real nine-transfer positive query passes 6,681 properties in 71.632s;
copy/removal coverage takes 7.767/7.765s. Preparation/model and compiler cost is
1.715/0.206s; symbolic processing and solving remain combined in query time.
The unchanged ordinary cleanup C compiles through the public command in 3.943s,
with structural proposal preparation/plan cost 2.253/0.009s. Final Nix validation
passes 108 tests with zero skips and supporting gates in 105.413s. No pilot or
link is rebuilt. These are conditional region measurements, not completion of
the real-network workflow. See `real-network/cleanup-memory-facts-v2/`,
`real-network/public-cleanup-memory-facts-v8/` and
`real-network/memory-facts-final-nix-result.json`.

The corrected corruption query takes 78.664s and rejects current suffix/frame
contents with an adequate two-write budget. It supersedes the initial negative
run that also exhausted capacity, without rerunning the unchanged positive
model. Source snapshots and remaining exits are recorded in
`real-network/checkpoint-memory-facts.json`.

The following paragraphs retain earlier observations; the prototype's lack of
public range syntax is superseded by the checkpoint above.

The current-memory range experiment executes the actual nine-transfer cleanup
iteration with two live imported heap objects and arbitrary valid 32-bit extents.
A zero-suffix constructor makes each initial byte lookup conditional on range
membership, avoiding a byte-count-sized initialization loop. It is installed
before heap observations. The invariant/frame/lifetime check passes 6,639
properties in 52.943s; replacing the range with one zero byte fails the next
output terminator and suffix in 59.932s. Preparation/model and compiler costs
are 1.722/0.214s. Copy/removal witnesses pass in 6.089/6.199s. These are
conditional machine-iteration checks, not paired component qualification or
interactive-latency claims. Public range-fact transport and its receipt rules
are not implemented by this experiment. See `real-network/cleanup-zero-suffix-v1/`.

Both complete-inventory heap predecessor attempts are terminal and incomplete.
The newer v2 passes five safety partitions and 20 authored queries, then times
out on `__CPROVER_spx_view_address.assertion.1` at 600.296s; total time is
2,309.308s. The old v1 result remains separate. No timeout triggers an automatic
restart or a pilot rebuild. Later paragraphs retain their earlier observations.

Conditional operation-entry histories now share the existing class/current-memory
constructor with cuts. The entry premise participates in proof-plan identity,
generated C and the maximum entry/cut input capacity; it cannot reuse an older
empty-entry model merely because that maximum is unchanged. Restoration checks
use a shared helper so multiple rows retain one static semantic assertion
identity, with a check at every restoration. Existing retained predecessor jobs
keep their original sources and model identities.

The real Metapad entry-contract test passes 5,611 properties in 15.948s, rejects
missing class provenance in 10.022s, and reaches its witness in 3.514s. Its
95-body/3,950-instruction model omits allocating call-oracle bodies; this is
conditional input-contract evidence, not multilevel independence or caller
qualification. The unchanged ordinary cleanup C passes the public source check
in 3.858s; its 35-transfer entry/two-cut proposal prepares/plans in 2.326/0.009s.
Targeted Nix validation passes 87 tests, zero skips, and supporting gates in
108.366s. No pilot is rebuilt. See `real-network/real-entry-history-v1/`,
`real-network/public-cleanup-entry-history-v7/`, and
`real-network/entry-allocation-history-nix-result.json`.

Native reference range-loop limits now use each shard's represented allocation
capacity (maximum calls plus imported instances), with the existing rule-count
floor. One class no longer forces a one-instance loop limit. Both evidence
readers derive the bound from existing metadata for property, safety and
reachability queries. Only commands whose derived bounds change lose command
reuse; this edit does not change generated C. The retained Metapad four-slot
world has five rules, so its canonical override remains unchanged. Its original
implementation snapshots remain recorded separately from current checker code.
Targeted Nix validation passes 63 tests, zero skips, and supporting gates in
84.381s; the failed first JQ-guard attempt is retained separately. See
`real-network/authority-instance-bounds-nix-result.json`.

The older heap predecessor v1 terminates incomplete in 1,755.855s. All five
safety partitions pass; `cleanup.assertion.38` exceeds 600s. The v2 predecessor
remains in progress; its separate live/null coverage queries pass in
128.200/171.715s under the retained conditional input premises.
No pilot rebuild accompanies this bound repair. Current results, source changes
and verified processes are indexed in `real-network/checkpoint-instance-bounds.json`.

Incoming allocation recipes now depend on checked class contracts, not the
allocator-call inventory of the consuming body. The actual Metapad class test
renders identical consumer source with no local allocator calls, the three
retained real sites, and renamed service bindings. Its compiled 91-body model
(3,870 instructions) omits allocating call-oracle bodies. The conditional
input-memory/alias/lifetime query passes 5,557 properties in 22.841s; deleting
provenance fails in 13.100s, and positive coverage passes. These measurements
establish this input-contract boundary's independence, not multilevel component
independence. History policy v2 invalidates earlier call-ID-based transport
receipts. Targeted Nix validation passes 76 tests, zero skips, and supporting
gates in 110.773s. Retained evidence is in `real-network/real-class-consumer-v3/`
and `real-network/allocation-class-provenance-nix-result.json`.

Both earlier complete successor runs have terminated incomplete. Image-backed
v10 records 113 authored queries and two 600s control-query timeouts; heap-backed
v11 reaches a 600s pointer-safety timeout before authored assertions. The current
heap entry-to-allocation predecessor uses four real transfers, with
preparation/model/compiler costs of 4.362/0.039/0.351s. Its new class-based model
has separate complete-inventory execution under `real-network/cleanup-heap-predecessor-v2/`;
old and new proof-model identities are not interchangeable. No pilot or link
work runs. The following checkpoints retain the earlier observations.

The real callers supply heap text. The September 9 v11 successor replaces the
earlier image-backed premise with separate input/scratch allocation instances,
using the same ordinary C and ten original transfers. Preparation/model/compiler
take 4.347/0.043/0.409s without a pilot rebuild. Its selected origin query exceeds
180s and the initial coverage query exceeds 300s. Checking the identical input
constructor and production decoder in isolation takes 8.601s. This is evidence
for separating the input relation from the full consumer; it is not a complete
successor proof. The full partitioned run and costs are retained under
`real-network/cleanup-allocation-successor-v11/partitioned-v1/`.

The actual dialog caller's normalization loop is much smaller: its complete
entry and iteration checks take 1.237s and 4.900s, and weakening the index bound
produces a counterexample in 6.261s. Both positive queries check 6,266 properties.
Six independent empty/nonempty, backedge/exit and zero/nonzero-byte witnesses
pass. Aggregate witness queries now select their complete requested property
set, so unrelated entry witnesses cannot make a region appear uncovered.
Evidence readers check the exact selection; omitted required witnesses still
fail. This changes coverage query identities and their evidence, not generated
machine/source model bodies. Targeted Nix validation passes 33 tests, zero skips,
and supporting gates in 31.013s. Evidence is in `real-network/dialog-input-frame-v2/`
and `real-network/coverage-selection-nix-result.json`.

The preceding image-backed v10 run remains live on its retained model and
original checker process. Its compiled input snapshot predates the coverage
runner/reader edit; do not claim every current implementation file is unchanged
when reporting that run. The new heap model has a separate identity and cannot
reuse v10 query evidence merely because the operation or interface is unchanged.

The next retained run uses the existing complete inventory-partitioned checker,
not the monolithic assertion query used by the diagnostic driver. The compact
v10 model has 209 authored assertions. Eight exact completed process records
from the same conditional run are reused after a 300s pointer-safety timeout;
the 600s retry completes pointer safety in 358.048s and proceeds through the
whole authored inventory. At this checkpoint 35 assertions pass, with remaining
queries live. Descriptor/context queries can still take 95–117s individually;
this does not establish interactive latency or full regional equivalence.
The standalone full C-safety query passes 13,107 properties in 383.587s. A Z3
experiment times out at 180s; solver policy is unchanged. Current results and
live handles are indexed in `real-network/checkpoint-partitioned-consumer.json`.

The same compact memory predicate is checked against an active normal captured
stack, using the actual rendered native ingress function and the retained
Metapad map. This relaxes an unnecessarily strong inactivity premise without
changing the consumer model. All 255 properties pass in 0.889s; breaking the
frame/context correspondence is rejected in 0.305s. Actual caller admission and
other priority-provider premises are not established by this check. The 51-test
Nix selection and supporting gates pass in 24.456s with no skips. No pilot or
link work runs.


The September 9 native-admission experiment reuses the actual native access
predicates and the retained Metapad PE table. Its v9 model has 184 bodies and
12,453 instructions; preparation/model/compiler take 4.129/0.043/0.328s. The
complete assertion query exceeds its 600s limit under 16 GiB after 14.161s
symbolic execution (330,573 steps), 5.091s SSA conversion and 0.238s postprocessing.
It does not prove the region. A compact predicate is separately proved equal to
the native read/write predicates for all addresses and widths and up to three
arbitrary external ranges under the fixed context: 190 properties pass in
0.568s. Removing executable-write rejection produces a counterexample in 0.663s.
The v10 consumer omits those native predicate bodies and has 173 bodies/11,815
instructions; preparation/model/compiler take 4.295/0.045/0.393s. The v9 complete C-safety query passes 13,096 properties in 407.054s
(120.059s symbolic execution, 5.563s SSA conversion and 277.802s solver work).
The v10 assertion query also exceeds 600s; its full safety result remains
pending in `real-network/checkpoint-native-access-domain.json`. All three v10 live/null/fault coverage witnesses pass in 182.289s. These runs overlap; model
size reduction is established, but latency improvement and region qualification
are not. Both versions retain identical full ordinary cleanup C. No pilot or
link work runs. Actual runtime-context admission and public composition remain
missing; the conditional map abstraction is not a component-network receipt.


Performance is a correctness property of the interactive reconstruction
workflow. A phase that reads an undeclared global artifact may produce the
right answer, but it makes unrelated candidate edits expensive and is not an
accepted v3 phase design.

The subsequent assumed-restoration experiment profiles the complete retained
successor before changing code. In its selected-control SSA expression,
reference-origin bookkeeping contributes 78,158 unsliced rows and the exact
ten-transfer function 3,582. Havoced callbacks from rejected reconstruction
paths survive symbolic merging until the caller's assumption; this introduces
irrelevant callback and allocator work. Stopping only failed assumed restoration
inside the proof codec preserves its successful-input premise and removes that
source of expansion. Ordinary constructor and outgoing failure checks remain.

The diagnostic control query then passes with unwinding in 55.904s under the
same 16 GiB cap. The production-mode model takes 4.279/0.037/0.317s to
prepare/generate/compile. Its complete assertion query reaches a real access-domain
counterexample in 100.469s instead of running out of memory: 13.464s symbolic
execution, 302,882 expression steps, 4.969s SSA conversion and 79.017s solver work.
The full C-safety query passes 12,915 properties in 510.024s under 16 GiB:
86.872s symbolic execution, 5.250s SSA conversion and 414.705s solver work. The
compiled model has 171 function bodies and 11,703 instructions. The three
live/null/fault witnesses pass in 168.427s. These single measurements
show useful diagnostic progress, not successful qualification or the target
interactive latency. Evidence is under `real-network/cleanup-allocation-successor-v6/ssa-profile-v1/`
and versions v7/v8 of that consumer. The full operation, real callers and public
network workflow still need their checked memory-access/closure premises and
complete proofs. No pilot rebuild or link runs.

The September 9 successor experiment must use control-flow closure, not an
address interval: the actual allocation-to-loop region includes a bridge at
`0x5675`, for ten transfers in total. Earlier nine-transfer queries omitted that
bridge and are invalid as successor evidence. The corrected ten-transfer query
still exhausts the 1,024-addressed-object encoding in 210.555s.

The source-cut repair enforces the documented matched-barrier rule even when
the exact side returns or faults. Preparation/model/compiler costs for the
corrected real consumer are 4.244/0.035/0.307s, using unchanged full ordinary C.
Its complete assertion query takes 167.616s before solver memory exhaustion
after a 42 GiB virtual-memory cap is applied. Symbolic execution takes 44.092s
for 923,453 expression steps, SSA conversion 10.278s and solver work 101.790s.
The independent full safety query exhausts its 32 GiB cap in 276.634s. A selected
control query exhausts 16 GiB in 70.837s. Restoring the production affine stack
cache supplies three slots and takes 4.393/0.036/0.378s to prepare/model/compile;
the same selected control query still exhausts 16 GiB in 67.991s. These single
failed samples do not establish a speedup, successful proof or interactive
latency. No pilot preparation or native link runs. Exact source/model/query
bindings, output and resource limits remain under
`build/independent-lifting/real-network/cleanup-allocation-successor-v4/` through
`cleanup-allocation-successor-v6/`. Further work must attribute the remaining
model cost and complete the real caller/composition obligations.

The September 8 hand-defined service-buffer experiment adds one sparse write
event per external footprint, independent of its byte length. Matching calls
share an arbitrary final byte function; the model does not enumerate 500 bytes
or copy a heap. The actual four-transfer resource helper and ordinary C candidate
pass a conditional two-call check using retained exact C. Initial successful
costs are 0.003s preparation, 0.002s model generation, 0.256s GOTO compilation and
6.218s CBMC process time. The latter includes symbolic execution and solving;
it is not isolated SAT time. No pilot build or native link runs. The first
attempt exceeded CBMC's default 256 addressed objects; the retained successful
run uses 12 object bits. This changes checker capacity, not the logical contract.
Inputs, proposed premises, model, GOTO and process hashes are retained under
`build/independent-lifting/real-network/service-buffer/`. Conditional success
does not qualify the service profile or the helper for activation.
That checkpoint also reserves external range events separately from static
instruction stores. Its costs are 0.003s preparation, 0.002s generation,
0.247s compilation and 6.368s CBMC. A pinned Nix replay checks 573 current source
hashes and the retained GOTO hash before passing all 6,109 properties in 6.466s.
The eleven new Nix tests take 5.524s. An affected selection through old shared
test helpers pulled in the long returned-view suite and was stopped; direct
selection of the new test module avoids that unrelated repeat. Existing
memory/call regressions ran directly with the pinned tools.

The subsequent selected-contract run reuses the same exact helper inputs.
Canonical module/environment selection and complete overlay generation take
4.222s, with unrelated application imports still reported unresolved. The new
positive-buffer domain is checked before effects. The retained two-call model
takes 0.004s preparation, 0.002s generation, 0.254s GOTO compilation and 6.220s
CBMC; its pinned Nix replay checks 575 implementation hashes and all 6,124
properties in 6.194s. Image authority remains a fixture premise, so this is not
normal paired qualification. No pilot rebuild or native link is needed for this
step. Evidence is in `service-buffer/selection/`, `selected-current/` and
`selected-nix-recheck/` under the experiment root.

Paired admission uses the actual PE section objects and the same selected
service. Before the component-scoped import fix, the semantic-contract reader
rejected unrelated unresolved imports; those earlier paired attempts are not
admitted qualification. After the fix, preparation takes 4.253s and the paired
engine takes 251.856s before returning incomplete. Compilation takes about 0.2s;
pointer and bounds safety take 46.8s and 70.3s. The return-continuation-word query
then exceeds 120s. Exact commands and individual timings are retained beside the
incomplete proof, so the next experiment can recompile that small model and target
the failing query without any pilot preparation. A separate retained bounds query
passes in 66.917s; disabling its optional reachability prepass instead times out
at 60s. No checker optimization or completed proof is inferred from that comparison.

Inspection then found that those drivers omitted the standard checked typed-service
proof overlay. They measured direct native-adapter proof costs. The corrected
`paired-typed/` driver uses the existing provider overlay path with identical
production entries: preparation takes 4.268s, compilation 0.190s, bounds safety
18.689s and the return comparison 95.581s. Its full run returns incomplete after
263.512s because public-memory equality exceeds 120s. Replaying only that query
on the exact retained GOTO succeeds in 188.364s with a 300s limit. These CBMC times
include symbolic execution and solving; they are not isolated SAT times. The
follow-up with a 300s query limit takes 4.254s preparation and 715.313s in the
paired engine. It passes the return and memory comparisons (memory: 187.451s),
then times out at the typed buffer-argument correspondence assertion. Sixty-three
of 64 recorded steps pass; the current receipt remains incomplete. The retained
`paired-typed-complete/` directory names the attempted run, not successful proof
status. Its exact GOTO and individual query commands are retained for targeted
investigation. At that checkpoint, successful queries could not be reused from an incomplete
local receipt, so the full retry repeated them. The subsequent exact-query change
admits completed process outputs from validated incomplete receipts under the same
model/tool/argument/output checks. The real helper retry completes the local proof with 62 reused queries and
21 fresh queries, including those skipped after the previous timeout. The hard
argument check takes 384.725s; preparation takes 0.010s and the engine 441.225s.
Compilation and the cached prefix take about 0.347s. All 84 recorded steps pass,
and the GOTO remains byte-identical. No proof status is inherited: ordinary
current checking and receipt construction decide the new result. This is local
helper evidence, not provider qualification or real-network composition. A temporary stack-cache experiment did not
resolve the native-adapter timeout and was reverted; no production cache or
proof-semantics change remains from that experiment.

The hand-authored normal-exit view equality reuses the satisfied helper proof
without changing its model. A local retained-input run takes 1.744s to prepare
canonical inputs and 0.062s to validate the receipt, regenerate both overlays and
derive the fact. Compiler, solver and native-link work are zero. Exact overlay
bytes, canonical interface/binding and the complete local proof must still match;
unchanged function signatures do not permit reuse. The fact binds the relation
intent and proof receipt. Consumer invalidation for shared-state/service summaries
remains unimplemented and is not inferred from this cheap local derivation.
Evidence is under `service-buffer/hand-defined-postcondition/`.

The shared-state/service opacity checkpoint compiles only the actual helper's
proof-bound source package and canonical generated headers. Its pinned Nix costs
are 0.063s preparation, 0.031s compilation, 0.028s GOTO inventory extraction and
0.002s opacity checking. It verifies 576 current implementation hashes. No solver,
pilot preparation or native link runs. The opacity result binds the interface and
compiler inventory, and the retained experiment separately binds the exact source
package, headers, tools and satisfied local proof. It supplies no shared-state
summary or consumer invalidation authority. Evidence is under
`service-buffer/shared-opacity-nix/`.

The next shared-state local theorem initially reused dense byte storage. On the
actual 500-byte helper, its frame process reached about 36 GB RSS before the 120s
budget; the following input-dependence process was stopped. Those retained dense
artifacts are not a satisfied theorem. The shared model now records bounded source
stores and service ranges, with arbitrary initial bytes and chronological alias
updates. A source-inaccessible arbitrary-byte probe compares memory without a
per-buffer-byte loop. Event/call capacity and unwinding remain checked limits.
The actual helper's sparse source theorem passes in 0.899s: preparation 0.002s,
compiler 0.137s, model/inventory 0.298s and solver 0.402s. These solver times include
symbolic execution; they are not isolated SAT times. The model-size regression
compares 8-byte and million-byte view domains at the same event capacity. This
measures local source-model scaling, not parent-model body absence or proof reuse.
No pilot or native-link work is performed. The existing source-check Nix product
also compiles this exact source package for host and PE32 before presenting the
conditional local result. Evidence is under `service-buffer/shared-contract-sparse/`
and `shared-contract-nix/`; body-free provider composition remains unimplemented.

Selected service admission adds assertions for the actual argument domain and
argument-sized footprint inside the declared view. The real public Nix source
product spends 0.409s across its two solver queries. A separate pinned binding
recheck validates 578 current implementation hashes and joins this local evidence
to the existing exact-source proof and regenerated overlays: 1.690s preparation,
0.452s binding and 0.197s for two rejected cases. It reruns no paired solver, pilot
or link. Canonical local service premises omit call-site RVAs; their normalization
test confirms that moving a physical site leaves the premise unchanged. This is
not yet a measured parent-proof invalidation experiment. Source and native
contract/ABI identities remain checked; a stable signature alone grants no reuse.
Evidence is under `service-buffer/shared-service-nix/` and
`shared-service-binding-nix/`. Actual caller-entry and machine-frame evidence
remain absent from the helper's paired receipt.

The shared-image machine-frame experiment reuses that helper's retained exact C,
source and selected service binding. Candidate preparation, model generation and
compilation together take 0.368s; this exploratory driver does not separately
instrument those three costs. No pilot preparation or link is rerun. Three raw
cut/anchor probes take 3.324s, 3.263s and 3.502s. The write and revised exit-frame
queries each exhaust 180s, so no checked entry or reusable supplier receipt is
produced. This local cost is solver work, not pilot rebuilding. The small service
frame regression passes alongside its one-byte-too-small negative in under one
second. Retained models and outputs are under `service-buffer/shared-frame-probes-v3/`;
the earlier DF counterexample and 60s write timeout remain in `shared-frame-probes-v2/`.

The next experiment localizes those timeouts to symbolic-formula conversion,
before SAT solving. Removing the source suffix, forcing finite-array encoding,
and selecting SMT2 each still exhaust 60s; none is adopted. The existing stack
cache had been disabled for every nonzero external cleanup. Cache discovery now
uses the admitted ABI cleanup at the exact call site and discards post-call ESP
tracking for unknown sites. It preserves the existing address/width/validity checks
and partial-overlap invalidation; cache entries grant no memory permissions.
On the real helper, the production-generated model takes 0.023s preparation,
0.022s generation and 0.227s compilation. Its five raw frame queries pass:
71.137s write, 3.622s private anchor, 3.392s view transport, 3.556s cut clobbers and
52.975s exit clobbers. Evidence is under `service-buffer/shared-frame-produced/`.
These raw checks still require complete paired qualification and caller entry;
they do not authorize a provider or body omission.

The corrected complete local run takes 1.723s preparation and 1,126.702s in the
paired engine, including the wider-entry check in 442.243s. Its pinned evidence
binding recheck takes 1.703s preparation, 0.493s binding and 0.201s for two
negatives. It verifies 578 implementation hashes and requires the selected
source/service certificate plus private, entry and machine-frame evidence.
The earlier raw run's aggregate was rejected for an exact-slice intent mismatch;
the accepted run regenerates the canonical slice and plan. The producer now
rejects that mismatch before compilation. Evidence is under
`service-buffer/paired-shared-frames-corrected/`, `shared-frame-binding-nix/` and
`checkpoint-shared-frame-qualified.json`. These results do not qualify the real
callers or a body-free service summary.

The following connected-range transport check uses the retained four-transfer
helper and selected service/object authority. Its two invocations and intervening
module update take 0.002s preparation, 0.001s generation, 0.247s compilation and
4.386s solver processing; the pinned Nix recheck passes 5,874 properties in
4.337s. Range replay uses one event per footprint and preserves its call index,
rather than expanding 500 bytes into stores. Five small regressions take 6.777s
under Nix; the complete focused selection passes 53 tests plus 33 repository
checks. A current-source recheck also validates the prior helper entry/frame
evidence without rerunning its paired solver. Neither helper replay nor this
binding check demonstrates body-free parent-model independence. No pilot or
native link was rebuilt. Evidence is under `service-buffer/shared-range-replay/`,
`connected-range-recheck-nix/` and `checkpoint-connected-ranges.json`.

The first shared body-free prototype expanded the supplier's internal store
budget into the parent model. It was replaced because that would make internal
write histories a consumer dependency. The retained renderer chooses arbitrary
bytes over the writable union before interactions and at return. Its model
depends on the boundary's observable call bound and footprints; changing the
local source proof's memory-event budget from 1 to 64 leaves the wrapper bytes
identical. This is model independence from a proof resource parameter, not yet
the required public implementation-edit/reuse demonstration.

With the actual helper's selected contracts, object authority and full image,
two conditional body-free logical consumers pass 6,182 properties in 24.453s.
The pinned Nix recheck takes 24.788s and verifies 579 implementation hashes.
Preparation is 0.015s, generation 0.004s, PE32 compilation 0.116s and GOTO inventory
0.302s. The 121-function, 4,701-instruction model contains neither supplier body.
The preliminary narrower-image model is retained separately; use
`service-buffer/shared-summary-real-v3/` and its pinned Nix recheck for current
evidence. No pilot or native link ran. Actual machine caller admission, provider
composition, transitive reuse and public contract refinement remain open.

The actual cleanup-to-helper CALL-entry check uses one retained transfer at
`0x5646`, including the CALL at `0x564f`. Source-independent machine entry/frame
preparation takes 1.772s; GOTO compilation takes 0.103s and the 2,886-property
check 84.416s. A stack overlapping the image is rejected in 0.283s. Generation
and witness time were not separately isolated in this first 90.866s run.
The pinned Nix recheck passes in 79.636s against 579 current implementation hashes.
The model stops at the call boundary and does not compile the helper body.
Evidence is under `service-buffer/cleanup-helper-entry-v2/`; the preceding
attempt failed on a coverage intrinsic accidentally reachable in the ordinary
property model. No pilot or native link runs. Whole-loop entry and checked
shared-state/service substitution remain separate work.

The next integration adds the actual shared-state logical adapter and its trusted
runtime-view inspector. The combined cleanup CALL model takes 0.096s preparation,
0.004s generation and 0.228s compilation; its all-property process exceeds 240s.
The unchanged compiled model is retained for the existing partitioned checker.
The generated native helper entry is unchanged byte-for-byte, but its logical
sibling changes the whole overlay/GOTO hash and prevents reuse of the earlier
machine queries. This is a measured dependency between distinct entry paths,
not a callee implementation edit or evidence that local editing is ergonomic.
Keep this invalidation cost visible when integrating the public workflow.

The helper requalification finishes in 1,283.208s after 1.696s preparation,
executing all 100 queries with zero reuse. The combined CALL model's partitioned
run takes 1,034.894s, then stops on an absent typed-call focus entry in the
experimental harness. A separate check of the fourteen uncovered assertions
from the full entry takes 13.936s. The successful earlier queries are retained
and audited; neither compilation nor those queries are repeated. Combined
conditional coverage is complete, while the original partitioned run remains
incomplete and nonauthorizing. Rebinding the current qualified helper regenerates
identical caller-model C and headers and uses the retained compiled bytes.
This is model stability under refreshed helper evidence, not yet the public
implementation-edit/reuse workflow. The model contains 151 functions and 10,493
instructions, with neither helper implementation body present. Exact results
are retained in `service-buffer/checkpoint-shared-logical.json`.

Preparing the first real helper provider from retained inputs takes 3.244s for
the canonical semantic object, 2.618s for semantic linking and 4.480s for the
four-definition component slice. This is preparation, not native linking or
proof execution. The enclosing semantic module remains incomplete; the selected
helper slice has no residual obligations. The public qualification attempts
retain failures for the old authored binding blockers and a missing exact-C
directory dependency before solver work. A new nonauthorizing binding removes
only those stale descriptive blockers, keeps all operation semantics unchanged,
and requests fresh public qualification. Evidence is under
`service-buffer/public-provider-inputs-v3/` and `public-qualified-helper-v3/`.

For the ready real provider candidate, current direct normalization and overlay
preparation take 1.777s. Rechecking and retaining the selected shared-source
certificate takes 0.093s. An older, independently valid certificate without the
selected service premises is rejected in 0.389s. These checks execute no machine
queries and do not link native objects. The optional source-contract input is
attached after ordinary model generation, so exact-query reuse remains governed
by unchanged compiled bytes, tools and arguments, rather than a matching
signature or a promise of compatible effects.

The first real helper public qualification finishes with 100 fresh queries and
three PE32 objects. Its proof-engine timing window is 1,139.453s, including
0.224s GOTO compilation; the wider-entry probe takes 458.774s. Attaching the
checked shared-source contract through a second public qualification reuses all
100 queries, executes zero new solver queries, and takes 0.551s in the same timing
window, including 0.207s compilation. Machine models, checked shards and native
objects are unchanged. The qualified receipts change to bind the additional
source evidence. These are measured proof-engine windows, not total CLI/Nix
latencies. The provider preparation/model-generation split and native object
compilation duration were not independently recorded. No application native
link ran, and no implementation-edit or neighboring-proof reuse is claimed.
Both qualified products and current-reader audits are retained in
`service-buffer/checkpoint-public-shared-provider.json`.

The next consumer-readiness audit found that the Nix strong predicate lacked
the Python reader's auxiliary shared-source rule. After adding that rule, the
unchanged public v3/v4 products pass both current readers in 0.163s and 0.214s;
five altered real-proof cases reject. These are reader-only timings with zero
compiler or solver work and no application link. The original provider products
remain unchanged. The affected Nix regressions spend 2.703s on five shared-provider
tests, 4.810s on four mutable-source tests, 63.364s on three readable-receipt tests,
and 6.029s on 33 repository checks. Those suite timings include their small proof
fixtures and are separate from the retained real-product recheck. Evidence is in
`service-buffer/public-shared-reader-check.json` and `shared-reader-validation.json`.

The checked shared-composition entry experiment uses the retained public helper
without a pilot rebuild. Connected-input preparation takes 2.202s. The subsequent
current actual-CALL model records 2.352s preparation, 0.005s generation, 0.227s
compilation and 8.607s checking its single new shared-entry assertion. The separate
full conditional partition driver spends 0.664s preparing and compiling its
focus-enabled model, then 1,062.097s in the proof engine. All 120 assertions,
6,963 language-safety properties and nine model loop checks pass. Its inventory
contains 152 functions and 10,539 instructions, with neither supplier body.
These figures do not measure full cleanup qualification or public edit latency.
The latest multi-view guard Nix check passes five shared-summary tests in 90.764s
and 33 repository checks in 6.861s. A broader affected suite has an incomplete
transitive-memory fixture: its outer entry query exceeds the existing 120s limit
(435.407s fixture setup). Do not attribute that timeout solely to concurrent
jobs or call it a semantic counterexample. Retained evidence is under
`service-buffer/cleanup-shared-admitted-call-v2/`,
`cleanup-shared-checked-partition-v2/`, `shared-guard-nix.log` and
`shared-composition-nix.log`. No native link ran.

The framed shared extension qualifies the same public helper with an additional
image/private access theorem. The instrumented model changes, so v5 executes
102 fresh queries and reuses none: 957.478s in the proof-engine window, including
35.010s for the new access-frame query. This is a supplier theorem change, not a
component implementation edit. At the actual cleanup CALL with scratch release
history retained, the selected six-property check takes 2.358s preparation,
0.005s model generation, 0.239s compilation and 56.163s solver time. Its complete
conditional model contains 156 functions and 10,872 instructions, with both
supplier bodies absent. The full conditional partition check passes 125
assertions, 7,373 language-safety properties and nine model loop checks, taking
0.634s preparation/compilation and 1,650.571s in the proof engine. Its evidence is
under `service-buffer/cleanup-shared-framed-partition/`; selected-query timing does not
measure full caller qualification. No pilot rebuild or application link ran for
this consumer experiment.

Preparing the actual cleanup allocator/release bindings and local class takes
1.786s with retained inputs and no compilation or solver work. Pairing those two
real regions with ordinary C service calls, under the explicit non-wrapping size
premise, takes 1.662s preparation, 0.002s generation, 0.270s compilation and
120.519s checking 6,920 properties. The unrestricted size attempt finds the
zero-sized result-view counterexample in 138.313s and remains retained.

The hand-authored private access footprint uses the existing operation intent
and auxiliary evidence boundary. For the actual helper, preparing the retained
`[-28, +8)` proposal takes 5.197s, model generation 0.022s, compilation 0.239s
and the isolated raw-access query 86.904s. That probe is diagnostic evidence.
The subsequent public v6 qualification passes with 102 fresh queries, no reuse,
and a 1,176.740s engine window. Its wider-entry query takes 428.726s, the bounded
access-frame query 81.949s and compilation 0.204s. The exact Behavioral-C
files, ordinary C implementation, interface and selected service certificate
remain unchanged; the changed intent and instrumented model require their
normal evidence binding and invalidation. The initial five footprint tests pass
locally in 66.392s. The final six-test Nix shard, including explicit-null reader
parity, passes in 95.066s; all 48 selected tests pass, including 33 repository
checks in 6.550s. No pilot rebuild or application link is part of this probe.

The v6 consumer of the actual allocator/release/helper regions takes 2.480s
preparation, 0.008s model generation and 0.417s compilation. Its 164 functions and
21,461 instructions include neither helper body. The grouped eight-property
query exceeds 300s and is incomplete. The subsequent full partitioned check,
using this parent's authority loop bounds, takes 1.082s preparation/compilation
and a 603.418s engine window before reporting the pointer partition incomplete
at 601.5s. Bounds, signed-overflow, undefined-shift and unwinding checks pass.
Its pointer inventory has 10,380 properties in 90 functions; origin recording
and realization account for 1,254 and 1,188 respectively.

Reusing the grouped model for only the actual helper-entry assertion passes in
174.005s with standard assertion-partition flags. This resolves the entry
counterexample conditionally but does not discharge language safety. Grouping
the full model's pointer properties by function passes the helper adapter's 283
properties in 52.610s; the origin-recording group exceeds 180s. These two selected
groups are measurements, not full inventory coverage. The bounded-loop prototype
reduces pointer properties from 10,380 to 8,310, compiles in 0.342s and exceeds
300s in the pointer query. The selected-value-copy prototype has 10,440 pointer
properties, compiles in 0.400s and also exceeds 300s. Neither is adopted; fewer
generated properties alone do not establish a faster or completed proof.
A three-slot origin-capacity prototype retains the overflow assertion and passes
nine selected caller/lifetime/capacity properties plus two unwind properties in
223.163s. Its full partitioned check remains incomplete at the pointer query
after 600s; bounds and unwinding take 205.288s and 155.808s respectively.
The production resource parameter produces the same executable C apart from the
bound-specific assertion description and passes the same selected checks in
242.133s (2.417s preparation, 0.008s generation, 0.448s compilation). These are
regional assertion timings, not full caller qualification. The four new capacity
tests pass locally in 15.664s and under Nix in 16.287s; all 55 selected tests pass,
including 33 repository checks in 6.948s. Public helper qualification v7 passes
with a two-slot bound: 102 fresh queries, no reuse, and a 1,062.942s engine
window. Compilation takes 0.173s, wider entry 389.915s and access frame 64.049s.
Connected supplier preparation takes 2.230s and checks unchanged exact overlay
bytes. This is a proof-resource edit, not implementation-edit reuse; the
measurements do not establish a general speedup from reducing origin storage.
See `cleanup-shared-lifetime-call-v3/`,
`cleanup-shared-lifetime-partition-v3/`, `cleanup-pointer-groups-v3/`,
`cleanup-origin-loops-v4/`, `cleanup-origin-value-v5/` and
`cleanup-origin-capacity-v6/`, `cleanup-origin-capacity-v7/` and
`cleanup-origin-capacity-partition-v6/` under `service-buffer/`.

The terminated-byte-offset experiment uses the retained first three cleanup
transfers and an explicit proposed string-service contract, without rebuilding a
pilot or compiling the string implementation. The final v5 driver takes
1.734s preparation, 0.001s generation, 0.197s compilation and 0.996s checking all
2,604 properties. Its compiled inventory has 116 functions and 12,665
instructions. These costs measure the conditional predecessor through the first
length call, not the full cleanup or public qualification. V1's driver referred
to nonexistent per-transfer C functions; v2 omitted the indirect dispatcher.
Both failures are retained, and neither is a program-semantic counterexample.
V5 also corrects the unconsumed target-projection proposal to EBX at the call
region and binds the current implementation inventory; the raw target check
continues to use the original loader slot explicitly. See
`service-buffer/cleanup-terminated-read-v5/result.json` and `body-absence.json`.
The relation is part of the existing selected effect payload and therefore the
call-spec identity. Its renderer closure includes the checked normalizer,
current-memory result rule, call snapshots, sparse memory and origin/lifetime
implementation. No separate cache boundary or automatic boundary heuristic was
introduced. A refined result relation changes consumed semantics and must not
reuse a caller proof merely because its C signature is unchanged.
The final eight focused tests take 13.200s locally and 13.046s under Nix; all
41 targeted tests pass, including 33 repository checks in 6.935s. The broader
affected run passes 112 tests on the initial string-rule snapshot; the final
target/locality guards are covered by the separate 41-test recheck. The existing
13-test service-result-view shard takes 1,178.901s and dominates this broader
run, while the new string tests take seconds. No pilot rebuild or native
application link ran.

The subsequent static-slot transport check uses the generated typed length
adapter and canonical explicitly selected contract, with the same three retained
original transfers. Preparation takes 2.103s, model generation 0.002s, compilation
0.233s and solver 1.472s (`service-buffer/cleanup-typed-string-v2/`). Native
adapter object compilation takes 0.038s on the host and 0.022s for PE32. There
is no pilot rebuild or application link. The renderer inventory conditionally
binds the target helper and existing projection parser; no artifact format or
cache boundary was added. Full caller and service composition remain open.
The pinned Nix replay checks 587 implementation hashes and 3,015 properties in
1.212s. Six focused Nix tests take 4.482s, and the corrected 33-test repository
shard takes 19.139s. The qualified v7 supplier passes the current reader audit
unchanged, including eight negative cases, with zero new solver queries. These
results do not establish reuse after a real implementation edit.

The next consecutive length/allocation/length prefix takes 1.540s preparation,
0.002s model generation, 0.295s compilation and 119.348s checking for 6,555
properties. Its generated model has 130 functions and 51,006 instructions with
no external implementation bodies. This growth comes from admitted allocation,
reference and three-call composition support, not a compiled string or allocator
body. The original five transfers execute consecutively and the first result
derives the nonzero allocation size. Evidence is retained in
`real-network/cleanup-string-allocation-pair-v4/`; no pilot or application link
ran. The preceding failed driver runs are retained, including the absent runtime
read helper rejected by CBMC's missing-body assertion. These costs must not be
reported as callee-body growth or successful whole-network qualification.
The pinned Nix replay verifies 587 implementation hashes and checks the same
6,555 properties in 119.581s. Both runs bind GOTO hash
`9cbf8cbe3db1f33504a348a507dbd6d7a1aebab6fd22b7c42b44c04cf7d01890`.

The v8 public helper edit changes the source and authored GOTO while retaining
the same checked shared contract. Its public local check runs two fresh queries
in 0.534s; host/PE32 compilation takes 0.052s. Further local compiler/model
measurements are in `service-buffer/helper-edit-v8-local-build.log`. Full supplier
qualification passes with 102 fresh queries and zero reuse. Its retained query
timeline ends at 1,038.559s; summed phase durations are 1,245.262s because some
queries overlap. Compilation takes 0.186s, the mutable-entry query 372.541s and
the private-access-frame query 60.719s. Separate preparation/model/link timings
are unavailable in the retrieved derivation log and are not reported as zero.
The authoritative timing source is the product's `query-timings.jsonl`, indexed
by `service-buffer/helper-edit-v8-result.json`.

Current connected preparation admits the edited supplier. Regenerating the
conditional real consumer takes 2.220s preparation and 0.025s model generation;
all 13 C/header files match the retained consumer and omit both helper bodies.
No compiler, solver, link or pilot runs in this comparison. This is model
independence, not full parent qualification or compiled-query reuse. The old
regional checks cannot be promoted into complete query-cache evidence. This
edit uses the pinned v7 SDK and retained inputs; no pilot was rebuilt.

Explicit cleanup failure-result transport reuses the retained original transfers
and unchanged ordinary C. The empty witness takes 1.753s preparation, under
0.001s model assembly, 3.640s compilation and 2.546s CBMC for 4,494 properties.
The nonempty witness takes 1.716s preparation, under 0.001s assembly, 3.443s
compilation and 3.835s CBMC for 4,460 properties. Erasing the mapping fails the
outcome assertion in 4.303s. The larger-stack nonempty v4 attempt times out at
60s and remains incomplete. These are conditional transport checks, not parent
qualification or proof-cache reuse; no pilot or application link runs. Evidence
is retained in `real-network/cleanup-fault-outcome-*`.

The complete cleanup boundary proposal takes 1.651s preparation/admission;
the separate seven-service selection/lowering probe takes 1.657s. The public
source-only product records 0.002s input preparation and 0.069s host/PE32
compilation. These are builder phase costs, not end-to-end command latency.
No model qualification, solver, link or pilot phase runs. The source-only
product uses the retained v7 SDK and takes interface/source inputs; it does
not consume the proposed machine binding or validate its fault mapping.
Evidence is in `real-network/full-cleanup-boundary-v1-source-build.log`,
`full-cleanup-boundary-v1-source-realization.json` and
`public-cleanup-boundary-v1/`. The build log is retrieved from the realized
output's actual deriver; an empty outer content-addressed derivation log is
not evidence that no compiler ran.

The subsequent allocation-cut admission replay uses the actual five-transfer
length/allocation/length prefix and generated service adapters. It takes 1.545s
preparation, 0.001s model assembly, 0.313s compilation and 31.061s for assertion
inventory plus the focused solver query. The expected violated property is
`spx-bisimulation-allocation-cut-admission:allocated`: matching allocation
histories cannot justify resuming with empty histories. No pilot or link runs.
`real-network/cleanup-allocation-cut-v2/result.json` binds the current Python
inputs; v1 precedes relocation of the helper into its owning lifetime module.
This negative cut-admission evidence does not requalify the complete prefix or
establish transport of a local allocated view.

The incoming-allocation experiment first combines 13 real prefix transfers with
a conditional two-byte resumed-memory relation. Its full query times out at
60s; reducing write-history capacities to the selected prefix's needs still
times out at 180s. A focused alias query reuses that GOTO and times out at 60s;
its resume cover is reachable in 37.470s. These remain incomplete experiments,
not qualification or a controlled performance comparison.

The subsequent nine-transfer loop region consumes a declared current allocation
input and executes neither allocating nor string services. Version 4 takes
1.537s preparation, 0.001s model assembly, 0.196s compilation and 39.006s solver
time for 4,906 properties. Three branch witnesses take 10.432s solver time in a
separately compiled coverage model. An incorrect zero-initialization mutant
fails the current-input-byte check in 5.785s. Additional compiler costs and exact
commands are in `real-network/cleanup-loop-allocation-input-v4/additional-evidence.json`.
Its GOTO inventory has 128 functions/32,276 instructions and only the selected
loop's original function plus the common arithmetic overflow helper; the original
prefix function is absent. No pilot or native link runs. This is conditional
regional evidence, not a reusable complete loop proof or a benchmark against
the larger prefix experiment.

Version 5 uses the canonical bounded allocation-history declaration and generated
incoming/outgoing admission rules. The same nine original transfers pass 5,313
properties with 1.572s preparation, 0.002s model assembly, 0.244s compilation and
54.857s solver work. The model has 133 functions/32,588 instructions; the prefix
body remains absent. Separate coverage and zero-substitution checks take 12.235s
and 8.900s, with 0.248s and 0.261s compilation respectively. This checks additional
semantics, so it is not an unchanged-obligation speed comparison. Evidence is in
`real-network/cleanup-loop-allocation-history-v5/`.

The real allocating predecessor's corresponding named cut-admission query takes
1.706s preparation, 0.002s model assembly and 0.358s compilation, then reaches its
60-second solver limit (60.664s including inventory and process handling). It
remains incomplete, with no larger retry or pilot rebuild. The final affected
Nix selection passes 95 tests with zero skips plus smoke; exact products and
per-shard logs are in `real-network/allocation-history-final-nix-result.json`.

The next local-view experiment retains the ordinary source `for (;;)` loop and
uses its generated native descriptor codec. Version 6 takes 1.568s preparation,
0.003s model assembly and 0.237s compilation, then times out after 90s during SSA
conversion. Reusing that GOTO with a regional loop unwind and separate descriptor
input/output queries still times out at 60s per query. Adding the production
reachability and formula slicing also leaves both queries incomplete (60.470s
and 60.452s including process handling). No recompilation, pilot rebuild or link
runs for those retries. These are diagnostic experiments, not qualification or
an unchanged-obligation comparison with version 5. Their source predates the
final post-restoration alias assertion ordering fix. Exact commands and output
hashes are under `real-network/cleanup-loop-local-view-v6/`.

Follow-up inspection invalidates versions 6/7 as measurements of a correctly
assembled cut model: they omit two production memory-projection readers, and
CBMC reports removing the bodyless calls. The runner now reuses those readers
from the production harness. Version 8 takes 1.592s preparation, 0.003s model
assembly and 0.239s compilation. Its complete-property run remains incomplete at
60s; separate entry/outgoing descriptor queries reuse that GOTO and pass in
45.934s/58.873s. The entry query reports 6.807s symbolic execution, 1.345s SSA
conversion and 36.141s SAT solving, with 1,779,211 variables and 7,042,353 clauses.
This is selected-assertion evidence, not a complete regional or safety proof.

Version 9 additionally applies the existing production temporary compactor to
the retained original C. Preparation/model/compiler costs are 1.568/0.037/0.158s;
the complete query remains incomplete at 60s. No pilot or link runs. The query
flags include dereference caching, slicing and checked unwind limits; no input
premises were narrowed. Neither comparison establishes an interactive budget
for the complete operation. Next isolate the remaining proof partitions using
the production engine rather than maintaining a divergent diagnostic runner.

The retained version 9 now exercises the actual production partition runner and
shared command constructor. The first run fails the 256-addressed-object limit
in bounds, pointer and unwinding queries. Raising the checked object encoding to
10 bits removes that failure; dereference caching then lets bounds and unwinding
finish in 24.618s and 44.420s. The isolated pointer query remains incomplete at
60s and passes in 74.808s under the plan's 120-second larger-region budget. It
reports 19.142s symbolic execution, 1.613s SSA conversion and 52.254s SAT solving.
No input relation or safety obligation changes in these comparisons.

The complete production run with that budget passes all safety partitions, then
17 equivalence assertions. Even call-count and atomic-count assertions take
49.865s and 64.349s. Those 17 checks consume 1,037.181 summed process seconds;
the final completed query ends 413.343s after the run starts. The run is explicitly
interrupted after its current batch to evaluate grouping assertions that share
the same entry, not restarted after an observation timeout. Of 114 inventoried
assertions, 112 use `main` and two use `main_focus_typed_call_0`; a grouping must
preserve that distinction. Retained evidence is under
`cleanup-loop-local-view-v9/production-partitions-v1/` through `v4/`, with isolated
pointer results alongside them. A grouped diagnostic is not a new receipt rule.
The 112-assertion grouped query times out at 120s; symbolic execution and SSA
conversion take 6.398s and 1.426s, but the joint solver query remains incomplete.
Grouping alone is therefore not a demonstrated solution. A call-count query
with duplicate unwinding disabled passes in 8.714s, conditional on the retained
same-model, same-entry unsliced unwind proof. Its comparison with the earlier
49.865s run also differs in solver concurrency, so it is not a controlled speedup
ratio. Before production can consume this optimization, it must require the
matching satisfied unwind partition and retain unwind checks at distinct focused
entries. At that checkpoint the production receipt rule had not yet changed.

The version 10 strategy now implements this rule: same-entry assertion queries
omit duplicate unwinding only after the matching unsliced unwind receipt and
all safety partitions pass. Focused entries and entries without an unwind
receipt retain their own checks. Both readers bind the two command templates.
The corrected retained model also uses the normal typed-call focus generator.
Its full 114-assertion regional check and all five safety partitions pass in
697.674s, using four workers and a 120s per-query limit. Preparation/model/compiler
costs are 1.616/0.039/0.168s; pilot and link costs remain zero. This is still
11.6 minutes for one conditional region, not demonstrated interactive latency.
Current-model coverage takes 55.344s and the incorrect-write rejection takes
35.341s, separately from the full proof. Exact results are in
`cleanup-loop-local-view-v10/` and indexed by
`real-network/checkpoint-entry-unwinding-reuse.json`. The next acceptance work
is full ordinary-C boundary transport and checked progress, followed by caller
composition; these timings do not justify another standalone optimization cycle.

The full public cleanup source separately passes host/PE32 source validation.
Its compiler-derived local inventory takes 0.080s and includes the aggregate
scratch view; the proof-marker scanner still rejects two unannotated bounded
copy loops. Source compilation success does not establish their progress rule.
Do not hand-unroll production C or weaken input premises merely to hide these
proof-model and boundary-expression blockers.

The explicit finite-source-loop rule now removes that scanner blocker without
rewriting the C. The full 35-transfer proposal takes 2.288s to prepare from
retained inputs and 0.008s to assemble its diagnostic plan. The public source
check reuses the same source product in 2.152s; it does not consume the new proof
policy through the pinned SDK. There is no pilot, compiler, solver or link cost
for plan assembly. The five supporting finite-loop/receipt tests pass directly
in 3.410s; the targeted 46-test Nix selection and repository gates pass in
28.298s. `source_unwind_limit` is bound in the intent, plan and every relevant
checker command, so changing it invalidates those proof identities even when
source bytes are unchanged. No neighboring proof reuse or full-loop theorem is
inferred from successful planning. Evidence is under
`real-network/public-cleanup-source-loops-v3/` and `source-loop-nix-result.json`.

The following content-invariant step reuses those inputs. Its nine-transfer
regional model takes 1.565s preparation, 0.038s model generation and 0.164s
compilation. Selected invariant/access checks with their unwind assertions take
82.148s; the separate three-branch coverage query takes 115.082s. This has less
proof scope than the earlier 697.674s full partition run, so it is not a measured
speedup. The strengthened full-source boundary plan takes 2.340s preparation and
0.008s assembly while keeping source and compilation inputs unchanged. No pilot
or native link runs. The 44-test Nix selection and repository gates take 33.055s.
Evidence is under `cleanup-loop-content-invariant-v11/`,
`public-cleanup-content-boundary-v4/` and `local-byte-invariant-nix-result.json`.

The next full-source 14-transfer entry-to-loop query times out during SSA
conversion after 120.488s; its preparation/model/compiler costs are
1.690/0.111/0.292s. Adding a hand-defined allocation cut reduces the constructor
to four transfers without extracting a production function. Preparation/model/
compiler take 1.696/0.027/0.253s; selected invariant/access/frame checks take
54.014s. The broader cut-transport query finds a counterexample in 59.909s:
the original terminated span and the source view's capacity are not the same
contract. Live/null coverage takes 74.036/71.701s. These are different obligations,
not a demonstrated end-to-end speedup. Public source checking takes 3.573s;
no pilot rebuild or native link runs. `checkpoint-allocation-split.json` retains
the failed and successful selected checks without promoting either to provider
qualification. Solver process times include symbolic execution and conversion.

The subsequent borrowed-prefix implementation leaves the real source and cut
proposal unchanged. It reuses the registered terminated span after the adapter
has validated the borrowed reference, avoiding a second origin-registry search.
The current four-transfer model takes 1.695/0.026/0.252s for preparation/model/
compiler and contains 167 functions/10,625 instructions including generated
runtime adapters. Both the complete cut-assertion group and the selected string
admission/scratch-descriptor group exceed 120s during SSA conversion. The earlier
duplicate-origin-search version also exceeds 120s; these timeouts do not measure
a speedup or prove the real transport fix. Giving the unchanged complete cut
group 300s finds an allocation-history counterexample in 177.013s: the prologue
moves the recomputed private-memory window across 28 bytes of an admitted
allocation. This is a semantic admission failure, not a completed split proof. The five
string-admission/scratch-descriptor assertions plus three unwind assertions
subsequently pass on the same model in 146.031s with the 300s limit. The 56-test Nix selection and repository
gates pass with zero skips in 29.480s. This Nix run overlaps the full transport
query; its wall time is not an isolated solver benchmark. No pilot or native link
runs. Evidence is in `cleanup-entry-allocation-v3/`, `cleanup-entry-allocation-v4/`
and `borrowed-nul-prefix-nix-result.json` under the real-network experiment root.

Adding these actual lifetime regions to the helper consumer grows its model
from 156 functions/10,872 instructions to 164 functions/21,440 instructions.
The grouped selected-property query times out at 180s. Reusing its compiled model
for the helper-entry property finds a counterexample in 179.202s; a concrete
stack/address witness compiles in 0.349s and checks in 84.852s. The same compiled
witness checks all selected regional prerequisites in 94.163s: the pre-helper
lifetime checks pass and shared-entry fails. This is evidence that the current
allocator and callee private-window premises do not compose, not a compiler
failure or successful full caller proof. Evidence is retained in
`real-network/checkpoint-cleanup-lifetime.json`. No pilot or native link ran.

The transitive image-memory regression now retains three satisfied machine
levels. Fresh middle and outer checks took 150.035s and 454.048s respectively;
the initial outer attempt at a 30s query limit timed out and remains incomplete.
Replacing the grown leaf with its separately checked shorter implementation
then reused all 122 middle and 179 outer machine queries, plus two middle source
queries, in 5.393s overall. A guard rejected any fresh solver invocation. Both
consumer operation models and shards stayed identical while their proof receipts
changed to bind the current supplier closure. These are conditional regression
proofs, not provider qualification or measured real-network editing latency.
Evidence and the previous trees are retained under
`build/independent-lifting/transitive-machine/`. No pilot or link was rebuilt.

Fresh Nix validation of that regression took 924.373s and ran eleven tests:
unittest also discovered an imported `TestCase`, unnecessarily repeating its
two-level proof setup. Importing its module instead retains the reader helpers
while selecting only the six intended tests. This removes duplicated work; no
fresh end-to-end latency measurement of the revised suite is claimed. A separate
current-reader Nix check consumes the retained edited proofs and passes all six
tests, regenerating only the small negative source-theorem case.

The complete real cleanup candidate and its resource-text helper now use the
public source-check product. Retained input loading takes 1.702s and complete
exact-C emission, including the actual internal helper, takes 0.029s. Their
host/PE32 source checks spend 0.075s and 0.062s in compilation. Local summary
admission rejects both state/service interfaces before model or solver work;
those products spend 0.079s and 0.060s in compilation. These are phase costs,
not qualification times. Correcting the helper result from a NUL guarantee to
a fixed 500-byte view changes the cleanup interface and source-check product
despite unchanged authored C. No neighboring proof reuse is claimed for this
real contract refinement. Linking remains unperformed. Exact inputs, commands,
immutable products and phase logs are under `build/independent-lifting/real-network/`.

The hand-defined shared-view transport case reuses that real helper's ordinary C
